n24 src/Surface.zig:6219–6262 DIU
  6219 fn startClipboardRequest(
  6220     self: *Surface,
  6221     loc: apprt.Clipboard,
  6222     req: apprt.ClipboardRequest,
  6223 ) !apprt.ClipboardReadResult {
  6224     const effective_req: apprt.ClipboardRequest = switch (req) {
  6225         .paste => |clipboard| effective: {
  6226             // Snapshot the mode before asking the apprt for clipboard data.
  6227             // Event pastes request only a MIME listing, while ordinary
  6228             // pastes request the text representation as before.
  6229             self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  6230             const event = self.io.terminal.modes.get(.kitty_paste_events);
  6231             self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  6232 
  6233             break :effective if (event)
  6234                 .{ .list = clipboard }
  6235             else
  6236                 req;
  6237         },
  6238         else => req,
  6239     };
  6240 
  6241     switch (effective_req) {
  6242         .paste, .list => {}, // always allowed
  6243         .osc_52_read => if (self.config.clipboard_read == .deny) {
  6244             log.info(
  6245                 "application attempted to read clipboard, but 'clipboard-read' is set to deny",
  6246                 .{},
  6247             );
  6248             return .unsupported;
  6249         },
  6250 
  6251         // The clipboard access policies were already applied by
  6252         // kittyClipboardRead and kittyClipboardWrite, which own
  6253         // replying on denial.
  6254         .kitty_read, .kitty_write => {},
  6255 
  6256         // OSC 52 writes don't travel through this function; they go
  6257         // straight to the apprt setClipboard API.
  6258         .osc_52_write => unreachable,
  6259     }
  6260 
  6261     return try self.rt_surface.clipboardRequest(loc, effective_req);
  6262 }

n24 src/Surface.zig:6264–6341 DIU
  6264 fn completeClipboardPaste(
  6265     self: *Surface,
  6266     data: []const u8,
  6267     allow_unsafe: bool,
  6268 ) !void {
  6269     if (data.len == 0) return;
  6270 
  6271     const encode_opts: input.paste.Options = encode_opts: {
  6272         self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  6273         defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  6274         const opts: input.paste.Options = .fromTerminal(&self.io.terminal);
  6275 
  6276         // If we have paste protection enabled, we detect unsafe pastes and return
  6277         // an error. The error approach allows apprt to attempt to complete the paste
  6278         // before falling back to requesting confirmation.
  6279         //
  6280         // We do not do this for bracketed pastes because bracketed pastes are
  6281         // by definition safe since they're framed.
  6282         const unsafe = unsafe: {
  6283             // If we've disabled paste protection then we always allow the paste.
  6284             if (!self.config.clipboard_paste_protection) break :unsafe false;
  6285 
  6286             // If we're allowed to paste unsafe data then we always allow the paste.
  6287             // This is set during confirmation usually.
  6288             if (allow_unsafe) break :unsafe false;
  6289 
  6290             if (opts.bracketed) {
  6291                 // If we're bracketed and the paste contains and ending
  6292                 // bracket then something naughty might be going on and we
  6293                 // never trust it.
  6294                 if (std.mem.indexOf(u8, data, "\x1B[201~") != null) break :unsafe true;
  6295 
  6296                 // If we are bracketed and configured to trust that then the
  6297                 // paste is not unsafe.
  6298                 if (self.config.clipboard_paste_bracketed_safe) break :unsafe false;
  6299             }
  6300 
  6301             break :unsafe !input.paste.isSafe(data);
  6302         };
  6303 
  6304         if (unsafe) {
  6305             log.info("potentially unsafe paste detected, rejecting until confirmation", .{});
  6306             return error.UnsafePaste;
  6307         }
  6308 
  6309         // With the lock held, we must scroll to the bottom.
  6310         // We always scroll to the bottom for these inputs.
  6311         self.scrollToBottom() catch |err| {
  6312             log.warn("error scrolling to bottom err={}", .{err});
  6313         };
  6314 
  6315         break :encode_opts opts;
  6316     };
  6317 
  6318     // Encode the data. In most cases this doesn't require any
  6319     // copies, so we optimize for that case.
  6320     var data_duped: ?[]u8 = null;
  6321     const vecs = input.paste.encode(data, encode_opts) catch |err| switch (err) {
  6322         error.MutableRequired => vecs: {
  6323             const buf: []u8 = try self.alloc.dupe(u8, data);
  6324             errdefer self.alloc.free(buf);
  6325             data_duped = buf;
  6326             break :vecs input.paste.encode(buf, encode_opts);
  6327         },
  6328     };
  6329     defer if (data_duped) |v| {
  6330         // This code path means the data did require a copy and mutation.
  6331         // We must free it.
  6332         self.alloc.free(v);
  6333     };
  6334 
  6335     for (vecs) |vec| if (vec.len > 0) {
  6336         self.queueIo(try termio.Message.writeReq(
  6337             self.alloc,
  6338             vec,
  6339         ), .unlocked);
  6340     };
  6341 }

n24 src/Surface.zig:6343–6398 DIU
  6343 /// Send a Kitty clipboard-protocol paste event when mode 5522 is enabled.
  6344 /// The event only lists the available MIME types; it does not read any of
  6345 /// their data. The shared terminal paste implementation generates and records
  6346 /// the one-time password used by the program's follow-up OSC 5522 read.
  6347 fn completeClipboardPasteEvent(
  6348     self: *Surface,
  6349     clipboard: apprt.Clipboard,
  6350     available: []const []const u8,
  6351 ) !bool {
  6352     if (self.readonly) return false;
  6353 
  6354     const kitty_clipboard = terminal.kitty.clipboard;
  6355     const location: terminal.clipboard.Location = switch (clipboard) {
  6356         .standard => .standard,
  6357         .selection => .selection,
  6358         .primary => .primary,
  6359     };
  6360 
  6361     // The protocol implementation caps listings at this size too. Cap here
  6362     // so the temporary Content array stays on the stack.
  6363     var contents_buf: [kitty_clipboard.max_listing_mimes]terminal.clipboard.Content = undefined;
  6364     const contents_len = @min(available.len, contents_buf.len);
  6365     for (available[0..contents_len], contents_buf[0..contents_len]) |mime, *content| {
  6366         content.* = .{ .mime = mime, .data = "" };
  6367     }
  6368 
  6369     var aw: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = .init(self.alloc);
  6370     defer aw.deinit();
  6371 
  6372     self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  6373     defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  6374 
  6375     const pasted = try terminal.paste.paste(.{
  6376         .terminal = &self.io.terminal,
  6377         .alloc = self.alloc,
  6378         .writer = &aw.writer,
  6379         .kitty_clipboard = .{
  6380             .grants = &self.io.terminal_stream.handler.kitty_clipboard_grants,
  6381             .io = global.io(),
  6382         },
  6383     }, .{
  6384         .source = .{ .clipboard = location },
  6385         .contents = .{ .memory = contents_buf[0..contents_len] },
  6386         // A paste event discloses no clipboard data, so unsafe-text
  6387         // confirmation does not apply. If mode 5522 is reset, the empty
  6388         // stand-in representations cause the shared helper to write nothing.
  6389         .allow_unsafe = true,
  6390     });
  6391     if (!pasted) return false;
  6392 
  6393     self.queueIo(.{ .write_alloc = .{
  6394         .alloc = self.alloc,
  6395         .data = try aw.toOwnedSlice(),
  6396     } }, .locked);
  6397     return true;
  6398 }

n24 src/apprt/embedded.zig:104–137 DIU
   104     pub const KeyEvent = struct {
   105         action: input.Action,
   106         mods: input.Mods,
   107         consumed_mods: input.Mods,
   108         keycode: u32,
   109         text: ?[:0]const u8,
   110         unshifted_codepoint: u32,
   111         composing: bool,
   112 
   113         /// Convert a libghostty key event into a core key event.
   114         fn core(self: KeyEvent) ?input.KeyEvent {
   115             const text: []const u8 = if (self.text) |v| v else "";
   116             const unshifted_codepoint: u21 = std.math.cast(
   117                 u21,
   118                 self.unshifted_codepoint,
   119             ) orelse 0;
   120 
   121             // We want to get the physical unmapped key to process keybinds.
   122             const physical_key = keycode: for (input.keycodes.entries) |entry| {
   123                 if (entry.native == self.keycode) break :keycode entry.key;
   124             } else .unidentified;
   125 
   126             // Build our final key event
   127             return .{
   128                 .action = self.action,
   129                 .key = physical_key,
   130                 .mods = self.mods,
   131                 .consumed_mods = self.consumed_mods,
   132                 .composing = self.composing,
   133                 .utf8 = text,
   134                 .unshifted_codepoint = unshifted_codepoint,
   135             };
   136         }
   137     };

n24 src/apprt/embedded.zig:201–228 DIU
   201     /// See CoreApp.keyEvent.
   202     pub fn keyEvent(
   203         self: *App,
   204         target: KeyTarget,
   205         event: KeyEvent,
   206     ) !bool {
   207         // Convert our C key event into a Zig one.
   208         const input_event: input.KeyEvent = event.core() orelse
   209             return false;
   210 
   211         // Invoke the core Ghostty logic to handle this input.
   212         const effect: CoreSurface.InputEffect = switch (target) {
   213             .app => if (self.core_app.keyEvent(
   214                 self,
   215                 input_event,
   216             )) .consumed else .ignored,
   217 
   218             .surface => |surface| try surface.core_surface.keyCallback(
   219                 input_event,
   220             ),
   221         };
   222 
   223         return switch (effect) {
   224             .closed => true,
   225             .ignored => false,
   226             .consumed => true,
   227         };
   228     }

   715     pub fn clipboardRequest(
   716         self: *Surface,
   717         clipboard_type: apprt.Clipboard,
   718         state: apprt.ClipboardRequest,
   719     ) !apprt.ClipboardReadResult {

n24 src/apprt/embedded.zig:742–801 DIU
   742         // The representations the read wants served. Text-only
   743         // requesters ask for the canonical text type; Kitty clipboard
   744         // reads ask for exactly what the program requested, with
   745         // text-like aliases normalized so the embedder never has to
   746         // know about them.
   747         var mimes_buf: [terminal.kitty.clipboard.max_read_mimes][*:0]const u8 = undefined;
   748         const mimes: []const [*:0]const u8 = switch (state) {
   749             .paste, .osc_52_read => &.{"text/plain"},
   750 
   751             // A mode 5522 paste event only lists types and must not read
   752             // any clipboard data.
   753             .list => &.{},
   754 
   755             .kitty_read => |kitty| mimes: {
   756                 assert(kitty.mimes.len <= mimes_buf.len);
   757                 for (kitty.mimes, mimes_buf[0..kitty.mimes.len]) |mime, *dst| {
   758                     dst.* = if (terminal.clipboard.isTextMime(mime))
   759                         "text/plain"
   760                     else
   761                         mime.ptr;
   762                 }
   763                 break :mimes mimes_buf[0..kitty.mimes.len];
   764             },
   765 
   766             // Handled above.
   767             .kitty_write => unreachable,
   768 
   769             // No clipboard write code paths travel through this function
   770             .osc_52_write => unreachable,
   771         };
   772         const list = switch (state) {
   773             // Paste events need the full MIME listing without reading any
   774             // representation. Kitty reads only ask for it when requested.
   775             .list => true,
   776             .kitty_read => |kitty| kitty.list,
   777             else => false,
   778         };
   779 
   780         // We need to allocate to get a pointer to store our clipboard request
   781         // so that it is stable until the read_clipboard callback and call
   782         // complete_clipboard_request. This sucks but clipboard requests aren't
   783         // high throughput so it's probably fine.
   784         const alloc = self.app.core_app.alloc;
   785         const state_ptr = try alloc.create(apprt.ClipboardRequest);
   786         errdefer alloc.destroy(state_ptr);
   787         state_ptr.* = state;
   788 
   789         const result = self.app.opts.read_clipboard(
   790             self.userdata,
   791             @intCast(@backingInt(clipboard_type)),
   792             state_ptr,
   793             mimes.ptr,
   794             mimes.len,
   795             list,
   796         );
   797 
   798         // Only a started request completes later and keeps the state.
   799         if (result != .started) alloc.destroy(state_ptr);
   800         return result;
   801     }

n24 src/apprt/gtk/class/split_tree.zig:212–277 DIU
   212     pub fn newSplit(
   213         self: *Self,
   214         direction: Surface.Tree.Split.Direction,
   215         parent_: ?*Surface,
   216         overrides: Overrides,
   217     ) Allocator.Error!void {
   218         const alloc = Application.default().allocator();
   219 
   220         // Create our new surface.
   221         const surface: *Surface = .new(.{
   222             .command = overrides.command,
   223             .shell_integration = overrides.shell_integration,
   224             .working_directory = overrides.working_directory,
   225             .title = overrides.title,
   226         });
   227         defer surface.unref();
   228         _ = surface.refSink();
   229 
   230         // Inherit properly if we were asked to.
   231         if (parent_) |p| {
   232             if (p.core()) |core| {
   233                 surface.setParent(core, .split);
   234             }
   235         }
   236 
   237         // Bind is-split property for new surface
   238         surface.bindIsSplit(self);
   239 
   240         // Create our tree
   241         var single_tree = try Surface.Tree.init(alloc, surface);
   242         defer single_tree.deinit();
   243 
   244         // We want to move our focus to the new surface no matter what.
   245         // But we need to be careful to restore state if we fail.
   246         const old_last_focused = self.private().last_focused.get();
   247         defer if (old_last_focused) |v| v.unref(); // unref strong ref from get
   248         self.private().last_focused.set(surface);
   249         errdefer self.private().last_focused.set(old_last_focused);
   250 
   251         // If we have no tree yet, then this becomes our tree and we're done.
   252         const old_tree = self.getTree() orelse {
   253             self.setTree(&single_tree);
   254             return;
   255         };
   256 
   257         // The handle we create the split relative to. Today this is the active
   258         // surface but this might be the handle of the given parent if we want.
   259         const handle = self.getActiveSurfaceHandle() orelse .root;
   260 
   261         // Create our split!
   262         var new_tree = try old_tree.split(
   263             alloc,
   264             handle,
   265             direction,
   266             0.5, // Always split equally for new splits
   267             &single_tree,
   268         );
   269         defer new_tree.deinit();
   270         log.debug(
   271             "new split at={} direction={} old_tree={f} new_tree={f}",
   272             .{ handle, direction, old_tree, &new_tree },
   273         );
   274 
   275         // Replace our tree
   276         self.setTree(&new_tree);
   277     }

  4851     fn clipboardReadText(
  4852         source: ?*gobject.Object,
  4853         res: *gio.AsyncResult,
  4854         ud: ?*anyopaque,
  4855     ) callconv(.c) void {
  4856         const clipboard = gobject.ext.cast(
  4857             gdk.Clipboard,
  4858             source orelse return,
  4859         ) orelse return;
  4860         const req: *Request = @ptrCast(@alignCast(ud orelse return));
  4861 
  4862         const alloc = Application.default().allocator();
  4863         defer alloc.destroy(req);
  4864 
  4865         const self = req.self;
  4866         defer self.unref();
  4867 
  4868         var gerr: ?*glib.Error = null;
  4869         const cstr_ = clipboard.readTextFinish(res, &gerr);
  4870         if (gerr) |err| {
  4871             defer err.free();
  4872             log.warn(
  4873                 "failed to read clipboard err={s}",
  4874                 .{err.f_message orelse "(no message)"},
  4875             );
  4876 
  4877             // The requester may be waiting on a reply, e.g. an OSC 52
  4878             // read of a clipboard with no text representation. Denying
  4879             // the request answers the protocols that expect one.
  4880             if (self.private().core_surface) |surface| {
  4881                 surface.denyClipboardRequest(req.state);
  4882             }
  4883             return;
  4884         }
  4885         const cstr = cstr_ orelse return;
  4886         defer glib.free(cstr);
  4887         const str = std.mem.sliceTo(cstr, 0);
  4888 
  4889         const surface = self.private().core_surface orelse return;
  4890         surface.completeClipboardRequest(
  4891             req.state,
  4892             .{ .contents = &.{.{ .mime = "text/plain", .data = str }} },
  4893         ) catch |err| switch (err) {
  4894             error.UnsafePaste,
  4895             error.UnauthorizedPaste,
  4896             => {
  4897                 showClipboardConfirmation(
  4898                     self,
  4899                     req.state,
  4900                     &[_]terminal.clipboard.Content{.{ .mime = "text/plain", .data = str }},
  4901                 );
  4902                 return;
  4903             },
  4904 
  4905             else => {
  4906                 log.warn(
  4907                     "failed to complete clipboard request err={}",
  4908                     .{err},
  4909                 );
  4910                 return;
  4911             },
  4912         };

   451     fn newTabPage(
   452         self: *Self,

   462             priv.config,
   463             .{
   464                 .command = overrides.command,
   465                 .shell_integration = overrides.shell_integration,
   466                 .working_directory = overrides.working_directory,
   467                 .title = overrides.title,
   468             },
   469         );
   470

   475             }

   481             // If we don't have a config we just append it at the end.
   482             // This should never happen.
   483             return tab_view.append(tab.as(gtk.Widget));
   484         };

   486             .current => current: {

   488                     break :current tab_view.getNPages();
   489                 const current = tab_view.getPagePosition(selected);
   490                 break :current current + 1;
   491             },
   492 
   493             .end => tab_view.getNPages(),
   494         };
   495 
   496         // Add the page and select it
   497         const page = tab_view.insert(tab.as(gtk.Widget), position);
   498         tab_view.setSelectedPage(page);

n24 src/datastruct/blocking_queue.zig:99–168 DI
    99         /// Push a value to the queue. This returns the total size of the
   100         /// queue (unread items) after the push. A return value of zero
   101         /// means that the push failed.
   102         pub fn push(self: *Self, io: std.Io, value: T, timeout: Timeout) Size {
   103             self.mutex.lockUncancelable(io);
   104             defer self.mutex.unlock(io);
   105 
   106             // The
   107             if (self.full()) {
   108                 switch (timeout) {
   109                     // If we're not waiting, then we failed to write.
   110                     .instant => return 0,
   111 
   112                     .forever => {
   113                         self.not_full_waiters += 1;
   114                         defer self.not_full_waiters -= 1;
   115                         self.cond_not_full.waitUncancelable(io, &self.mutex);
   116                     },
   117 
   118                     .ns => |ns| {
   119                         self.not_full_waiters += 1;
   120                         defer self.not_full_waiters -= 1;
   121                         compat_thread.waitTimeout(
   122                             &self.cond_not_full,
   123                             io,
   124                             &self.mutex,
   125                             .{
   126                                 .duration = .{
   127                                     .raw = .fromNanoseconds(ns),
   128                                     .clock = .awake,
   129                                 },
   130                             },
   131                         ) catch return 0;
   132                     },
   133                 }
   134 
   135                 // If we're still full, then we failed to write. This can
   136                 // happen in situations where we are interrupted.
   137                 if (self.full()) return 0;
   138             }
   139 
   140             // Add our data and update our accounting
   141             self.data[self.write] = value;
   142             self.write += 1;
   143             if (self.write >= bounds) self.write -= bounds;
   144             self.len += 1;
   145 
   146             return self.len;
   147         }
   148 
   149         /// Pop a value from the queue without blocking.
   150         pub fn pop(self: *Self, io: std.Io) ?T {
   151             self.mutex.lockUncancelable(io);
   152             defer self.mutex.unlock(io);
   153 
   154             // If we're empty we have nothing
   155             if (self.len == 0) return null;
   156 
   157             // Get the index we're going to read data from and do some
   158             // accounting. We don't copy the value here to avoid copying twice.
   159             const n = self.read;
   160             self.read += 1;
   161             if (self.read >= bounds) self.read -= bounds;
   162             self.len -= 1;
   163 
   164             // If we have consumers waiting on a full queue, notify.
   165             if (self.not_full_waiters > 0) self.cond_not_full.signal(io);
   166 
   167             return self.data[n];
   168         }

n24 src/datastruct/message_data.zig:38–74 DI
    38         /// Initializes the union for a given data type. This will
    39         /// attempt to fit into a small value if possible, otherwise
    40         /// will allocate and put into alloc.
    41         ///
    42         /// This can't and will never detect stable pointers.
    43         pub fn init(alloc: Allocator, data: anytype) !Self {
    44             switch (@typeInfo(@TypeOf(data))) {
    45                 .pointer => |info| {
    46                     assert(info.size == .slice);
    47                     assert(info.child == Elem);
    48 
    49                     // If it fits in our small request, do that.
    50                     if (data.len <= Small.Max) {
    51                         var buf: Small.Array = undefined;
    52                         @memcpy(buf[0..data.len], data);
    53                         return Self{
    54                             .small = .{
    55                                 .data = buf,
    56                                 .len = @intCast(data.len),
    57                             },
    58                         };
    59                     }
    60 
    61                     // Otherwise, allocate
    62                     const buf = try alloc.dupe(Elem, data);
    63                     errdefer alloc.free(buf);
    64                     return Self{
    65                         .alloc = .{
    66                             .alloc = alloc,
    67                             .data = buf,
    68                         },
    69                     };
    70                 },
    71 
    72                 else => unreachable,
    73             }
    74         }

  1491     /// Returns a union type that only contains actions that are scoped to
  1492     /// the given scope.
  1493     pub fn Scoped(comptime s: Scope) type {
  1494         @setEvalBranchQuota(100_000);
  1495 
  1496         const all_fields = @typeInfo(Action).@"union";
  1497         const field_names = all_fields.field_names;
  1498         const field_types = all_fields.field_types;
  1499         const field_attrs = all_fields.field_attrs;
  1500 
  1501         // Find all fields that are app-scoped
  1502         var i: comptime_int = 0;
  1503         var names: [field_names.len][]const u8 = undefined;
  1504         var types: [field_names.len]type = undefined;
  1505         var attrs: [field_names.len]std.lang.Type.Union.FieldAttributes = undefined;
  1506         var raw_values: [field_names.len]comptime_int = undefined;
  1507 
  1508         inline for (field_names, field_types, field_attrs) |field_name, field_type, field_attr| {
  1509             const action = @unionInit(Action, field_name, undefined);
  1510             if (action.scope() == s) {
  1511                 names[i] = field_name;
  1512                 types[i] = field_type;
  1513                 attrs[i] = field_attr;
  1514                 raw_values[i] = i;
  1515                 i += 1;
  1516             }
  1517         }
  1518 
  1519         const TagInt = std.math.IntFittingRange(0, i);
  1520         var values: [i]TagInt = undefined;
  1521         for (raw_values[0..i], &values) |raw, *v| v.* = raw;
  1522 
  1523         // Build our union
  1524         return @Union(
  1525             .auto,
  1526             @Enum(TagInt, .exhaustive, names[0..i], &values),
  1527             names[0..i],
  1528             types[0..i],
  1529             attrs[0..i],
  1530         );
  1531     }
  1532 
  1533     /// Returns the scoped version of this action. If the action is not
  1534     /// scoped to the given scope then this returns null.
  1535     ///
  1536     /// The benefit of this function is that it allows us to use Zig's
  1537     /// exhaustive switch safety to ensure we always properly handle certain
  1538     /// scoped actions.
  1539     pub fn scoped(self: Action, comptime s: Scope) ?Scoped(s) {
  1540         switch (self) {
  1541             inline else => |v, tag| {
  1542                 // Use comptime to prune out non-app actions
  1543                 if (comptime @unionInit(
  1544                     Action,
  1545                     @tagName(tag),
  1546                     undefined,
  1547                 ).scope() != s) return null;
  1548 
  1549                 // Initialize our app action
  1550                 return @unionInit(
  1551                     Scoped(s),
  1552                     @tagName(tag),
  1553                     v,
  1554                 );
  1555             },
  1556         }
  1557     }

n24 src/input/Binding.zig:2705–2708 DI
  2705     /// Get a binding for a given trigger.
  2706     pub fn get(self: Set, t: Trigger) ?Entry {
  2707         return self.bindings.getEntry(t);
  2708     }

n24 src/input/Binding.zig:2716–2760 DIU
  2716     /// Get an entry for the given key event. This will attempt to find
  2717     /// a binding using multiple parts of the event in the following order:
  2718     ///
  2719     ///   1. Physical key (event.physical_key)
  2720     ///   2. Unshifted Unicode codepoint (event.unshifted_codepoint)
  2721     ///
  2722     pub fn getEvent(self: *const Set, event: KeyEvent) ?Entry {
  2723         var trigger: Trigger = .{
  2724             .mods = event.mods.binding(),
  2725             .key = .{ .physical = event.key },
  2726         };
  2727         if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
  2728 
  2729         // If our UTF-8 text is exactly one codepoint, we try to match that.
  2730         if (event.utf8.len > 0) unicode: {
  2731             const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch break :unicode;
  2732             var it = view.iterator();
  2733 
  2734             // No codepoints or multiple codepoints drops to invalid format
  2735             const cp = it.nextCodepoint() orelse break :unicode;
  2736             if (it.nextCodepoint() != null) break :unicode;
  2737 
  2738             trigger.key = .{ .unicode = cp };
  2739             if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
  2740         }
  2741 
  2742         // Finally fallback to the full unshifted codepoint if we have one.
  2743         // Question: should we be doing this if we have UTF-8 text? I
  2744         // suspect "no" but we don't currently have any failing scenarios
  2745         // to verify this.
  2746         if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
  2747             trigger.key = .{ .unicode = event.unshifted_codepoint };
  2748             if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
  2749         }
  2750 
  2751         // Fallback to catch_all with modifiers first, then without modifiers.
  2752         trigger.key = .catch_all;
  2753         if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
  2754         if (!trigger.mods.empty()) {
  2755             trigger.mods = .{};
  2756             if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
  2757         }
  2758 
  2759         return null;
  2760     }

n24 src/input/function_keys.zig:12–49 D
    12 pub const CursorMode = enum { any, normal, application };
    13 pub const KeypadMode = enum { any, normal, application };
    14 
    15 /// A bit confusing so I'll document this one: this is the "modify other keys"
    16 /// setting. We only change behavior for "set_other" which is ESC [ > 4; 2 m.
    17 /// So this can be "any" which means we don't care what's going on. Or it
    18 /// can be "set" which means modify keys must be set EXCEPT FOR "other keys"
    19 /// mode, and "set_other" which means modify keys must be set to "other keys"
    20 /// mode.
    21 ///
    22 /// See: https://invisible-island.net/xterm/modified-keys.html
    23 pub const ModifyKeys = enum {
    24     any,
    25     set,
    26     set_other,
    27 };
    28 
    29 /// A single entry in the table of keys.
    30 pub const Entry = struct {
    31     /// The exact set of modifiers that must be active for this entry to match.
    32     /// If mods_empty_is_any is true then empty mods means any set of mods can
    33     /// match. Otherwise, empty mods means no mods must be active.
    34     mods: key.Mods = .{},
    35     mods_empty_is_any: bool = true,
    36 
    37     /// The state required for cursor/keypad mode.
    38     cursor: CursorMode = .any,
    39     keypad: KeypadMode = .any,
    40 
    41     /// Whether or not this entry should be used
    42     modify_other_keys: ModifyKeys = .any,
    43 
    44     /// The sequence to send to the pty if this entry matches.
    45     sequence: []const u8,
    46 
    47     /// Sequence to send to the PTY if DECBKM is set.
    48     sequence_decbkm: ?[]const u8 = null,
    49 };

    61     .{ .super = true },
    62     .{ .shift = true, .super = true },
    63     .{ .alt = true, .super = true },
    64     .{ .shift = true, .alt = true, .super = true },
    65     .{ .ctrl = true, .super = true },
    66     .{ .shift = true, .ctrl = true, .super = true },
    67     .{ .alt = true, .ctrl = true, .super = true },
    68     .{ .shift = true, .alt = true, .ctrl = true, .super = true },
    69 };

   251         .{ .mods = .{ .super = true, .shift = true }, .sequence = "\x1b[27;10;27~" },
   252         .{ .mods = .{ .alt = true, .super = true }, .sequence = "\x1b[27;11;27~" },
   253         .{ .mods = .{ .alt = true, .super = true, .shift = true }, .sequence = "\x1b[27;12;27~" },
   254         .{ .mods = .{ .super = true, .ctrl = true }, .sequence = "\x1b[27;13;27~" },
   255         .{ .mods = .{ .super = true, .ctrl = true, .shift = true }, .sequence = "\x1b[27;14;27~" },
   256         .{ .mods = .{ .alt = true, .super = true, .ctrl = true }, .sequence = "\x1b[27;15;27~" },
   257         .{ .mods = .{ .alt = true, .super = true, .ctrl = true, .shift = true }, .sequence = "\x1b[27;16;27~" },
   258         .{ .sequence = "\x1b" },
   259     });
   260 
   261     break :keys result;
   262 };

n24 src/input/function_keys.zig:264–314 DIU
   264 /// Returns the default keypad application mode entry.
   265 fn kpDefault(comptime suffix: []const u8) []const Entry {
   266     return &.{
   267         .{
   268             .mods_empty_is_any = false,
   269             .keypad = .application,
   270             .sequence = "\x1bO" ++ suffix,
   271         },
   272     };
   273 }
   274 
   275 /// Returns the entries for a keypad key. The suffix is the final character
   276 /// of the sent sequence, such as "r" for kp_2.
   277 fn kpKeys(comptime suffix: []const u8, comptime normal: []const u8) []const Entry {
   278     const pc = pcStyle("\x1bO{}" ++ suffix);
   279     for (pc) |*entry| entry.keypad = .application;
   280     return kpDefault(suffix) ++ pc ++ .{
   281         Entry{ .keypad = .normal, .sequence = normal },
   282     };
   283 }
   284 
   285 /// Returns entries that are dependent on cursor key settings.
   286 fn cursorKey(
   287     comptime normal: []const u8,
   288     comptime application: []const u8,
   289 ) []const Entry {
   290     return &.{
   291         .{ .cursor = .normal, .sequence = normal },
   292         .{ .cursor = .application, .sequence = application },
   293     };
   294 }
   295 
   296 /// Constructs a set of pcStyle function keys using the given format. The
   297 /// format should have exactly one "hole" for the mods code.
   298 /// Example: "\x1b[11;{}~" for F1.
   299 fn pcStyle(comptime fmt: []const u8) []Entry {
   300     // The comptime {} wrapper is superfluous but it prevents us from
   301     // accidentally running this function at runtime.
   302     comptime {
   303         @setEvalBranchQuota(500_000);
   304         var entries: [modifiers.len]Entry = undefined;
   305         for (modifiers, 2.., 0..) |mods, code, i| {
   306             entries[i] = .{
   307                 .mods = mods,
   308                 .sequence = std.fmt.comptimePrint(fmt, .{code}),
   309             };
   310         }
   311 
   312         return &entries;
   313     }
   314 }

n24 src/input/key.zig:16–55 DI
    16 pub const KeyEvent = struct {
    17     /// The action: press, release, etc.
    18     action: Action = .press,
    19 
    20     /// The keycode of the physical key that was pressed. This is agnostic
    21     /// to the layout. Layout-dependent matching can only be done via the
    22     /// UTF-8 or unshifted codepoint.
    23     key: Key = .unidentified,
    24 
    25     /// Mods are the modifiers that are pressed.
    26     mods: Mods = .{},
    27 
    28     /// The mods that were consumed in order to generate the text
    29     /// in utf8. This has the mods set that were consumed, so to
    30     /// get the set of mods that are effective you must negate
    31     /// mods with this.
    32     ///
    33     /// This field is meaningless if utf8 is empty.
    34     consumed_mods: Mods = .{},
    35 
    36     /// Composing is true when this key event is part of a dead key
    37     /// composition sequence and we're in the middle of it.
    38     composing: bool = false,
    39 
    40     /// The utf8 sequence that was generated by this key event.
    41     /// This will be an empty string if there is no text generated.
    42     /// If composing is true and this is non-empty, this is preedit
    43     /// text.
    44     utf8: []const u8 = "",
    45 
    46     /// The codepoint for this key when it is unshifted. For example,
    47     /// shift+a is "A" in UTF-8 but unshifted would provide 'a'.
    48     unshifted_codepoint: u21 = 0,
    49 
    50     /// Returns the effective modifiers for this event. The effective
    51     /// modifiers are the mods that should be considered for keybindings.
    52     pub fn effectiveMods(self: KeyEvent) Mods {
    53         if (self.utf8.len == 0) return self.mods;
    54         return self.mods.unset(self.consumed_mods);
    55     }

n24 src/input/key.zig:57–78 DI
    57     /// Returns a unique hash for this key event to be used for tracking
    58     /// uniquess specifically with bindings. This omits fields that are
    59     /// irrelevant for bindings.
    60     pub fn bindingHash(self: KeyEvent) u64 {
    61         var hasher = std.hash.Wyhash.init(0);
    62 
    63         // These are all the fields that are explicitly part of Trigger.
    64         std.hash.autoHash(&hasher, self.key);
    65         std.hash.autoHash(&hasher, self.unshifted_codepoint);
    66         std.hash.autoHash(&hasher, self.mods.binding());
    67 
    68         // Notes on unmapped things and why:
    69         //
    70         // - action: we don't have action-specific bindings right now
    71         //   AND we want to know if a key resulted in a binding regardless
    72         //   of action because a press should also ignore a release and so on.
    73         //
    74         // We can add to this if there is other confusion.
    75 
    76         return hasher.final();
    77     }
    78 };

    55     /// Initialize our options from the terminal state.
    56     ///
    57     /// Note that `macos_option_as_alt` cannot be determined from
    58     /// terminal state so it must be set manually after this call.
    59     pub fn fromTerminal(t: *const Terminal) Options {
    60         return .{
    61             .alt_esc_prefix = t.modes.get(.alt_esc_prefix),
    62             .cursor_key_application = t.modes.get(.cursor_keys),
    63             .keypad_key_application = t.modes.get(.keypad_keys),
    64             .backarrow_key_mode = t.modes.get(.backarrow_key_mode),
    65             .ignore_keypad_with_numlock = t.modes.get(.ignore_keypad_with_numlock),
    66             .modify_other_keys_state_2 = t.flags.modify_other_keys_2,
    67             .kitty_flags = t.screens.active.kitty_keyboard.current(),
    68 
    69             // These can't be known from the terminal state.
    70             .macos_option_as_alt = .false,
    71         };
    72     }

    74 
    75 /// Encode the key event to the writer in the proper format given
    76 /// the options. For example, this will properly encode a key press
    77 /// such as "ctrl+A" to Kitty format if Kitty encoding is enabled.
    78 ///
    79 /// Not all key events will result in output. It is up to the caller
    80 /// to use a writer that can track whether any output was written if
    81 /// they care about that.
    82 pub fn encode(
    83     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
    84     event: key.KeyEvent,
    85     opts: Options,
    86 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
    87     //std.log.warn("KEYENCODER event={} opts={}", .{ event, opts });
    88     return if (opts.kitty_flags.int() != 0) try kitty(
    89         writer,
    90         event,
    91         opts,
    92     ) else try legacy(
    93         writer,
    94         event,
    95         opts,
    96     );
    97 }

n24 src/input/key_encode.zig:99–319 DIU
    99 /// Perform Kitty keyboard protocol encoding of the key event.
   100 fn kitty(
   101     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   102     event: key.KeyEvent,
   103     opts: Options,
   104 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
   105     // This should never happen but we'll check anyway.
   106     if (opts.kitty_flags.int() == 0) return try legacy(
   107         writer,
   108         event,
   109         opts,
   110     );
   111 
   112     // We only processed "press" events unless report events is active
   113     if (event.action == .release) {
   114         if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_events) return;
   115 
   116         // Enter, backspace, and tab do not report release events unless "report
   117         // all" is set
   118         if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_all) {
   119             switch (event.key) {
   120                 .enter, .backspace, .tab => return,
   121                 else => {},
   122             }
   123         }
   124     }
   125 
   126     const all_mods = event.mods;
   127     const effective_mods = event.effectiveMods();
   128     const binding_mods = effective_mods.binding();
   129 
   130     // Find the entry for this key in the kitty table.
   131     const entry_: ?KittyEntry = entry: {
   132         // Functional or predefined keys
   133         for (kitty_entries) |entry| {
   134             if (entry.key == event.key) break :entry entry;
   135         }
   136 
   137         // Otherwise, we use our unicode codepoint from UTF8. We
   138         // always use the unshifted value.
   139         if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
   140             break :entry .{
   141                 .key = event.key,
   142                 .code = event.unshifted_codepoint,
   143                 .final = 'u',
   144                 .modifier = false,
   145             };
   146         }
   147 
   148         break :entry null;
   149     };
   150 
   151     preprocessing: {
   152         // When composing, the only keys sent are plain modifiers.
   153         if (event.composing) {
   154             if (entry_) |entry| {
   155                 if (entry.modifier) break :preprocessing;
   156             }
   157 
   158             return;
   159         }
   160 
   161         // IME confirmation still sends an enter key so if we have enter
   162         // and UTF8 text we just send it directly since we assume that is
   163         // what's happening. See legacy()'s similar logic for more details
   164         // on how to verify this.
   165         if (event.utf8.len > 0) utf8: {
   166             switch (event.key) {
   167                 else => {},
   168                 inline .enter, .backspace => |tag| {
   169                     // See legacy for why we handle this way.
   170                     if (isControlUtf8(event.utf8)) break :utf8;
   171                     if (comptime tag == .backspace) return;
   172                     return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
   173                 },
   174             }
   175         }
   176 
   177         // If we're reporting all then we always send CSI sequences.
   178         if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_all) {
   179             // Quote:
   180             // The only exceptions are the Enter, Tab and Backspace keys which
   181             // still generate the same bytes as in legacy mode this is to allow the
   182             // user to type and execute commands in the shell such as reset after a
   183             // program that sets this mode crashes without clearing it.
   184             //
   185             // Quote ("report all" mode):
   186             // Note that all keys are reported as escape codes, including Enter,
   187             // Tab, Backspace etc.
   188             if (binding_mods.empty()) {
   189                 switch (event.key) {
   190                     .enter => return try writer.writeByte('\r'),
   191                     .tab => return try writer.writeByte('\t'),
   192                     .backspace => return try writer.writeByte(0x7F),
   193                     else => {},
   194                 }
   195             }
   196 
   197             // Send plain-text non-modified text directly to the terminal.
   198             // We don't send release events because those are specially encoded.
   199             if (event.utf8.len > 0 and
   200                 binding_mods.empty() and
   201                 event.action != .release)
   202             plain_text: {
   203                 // We only do this for printable characters. We should
   204                 // inspect the real unicode codepoint properties here but
   205                 // the real world issue is usually control characters.
   206                 const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch {
   207                     // Invalid UTF-8 so let's fallback to encoding the
   208                     // key press as if it didn't produce UTF-8 text. I'm
   209                     // not sure what should happen here according to the spec,
   210                     // since it doesn't specify this behavior. Presumably
   211                     // this is a caller bug.
   212                     break :plain_text;
   213                 };
   214                 var it = view.iterator();
   215                 while (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp| {
   216                     if (isControl(cp)) break :plain_text;
   217                 }
   218 
   219                 return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
   220             }
   221         }
   222     }
   223 
   224     const entry = entry_ orelse {
   225         // No entry found. If we have UTF-8 text this is a pure text event
   226         // (e.g. composed/IME text), so send it as-is so programs can
   227         // still receive it. Release events never insert text, same as the
   228         // plain-text path above.
   229         if (event.action == .release) return;
   230         if (event.utf8.len == 0) return;
   231         return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
   232     };
   233 
   234     // If this is just a modifier we require "report all" to send the sequence.
   235     if (entry.modifier and !opts.kitty_flags.report_all) return;
   236 
   237     const seq: KittySequence = seq: {
   238         var seq: KittySequence = .{
   239             .key = entry.code,
   240             .final = entry.final,
   241             .mods = .fromInput(
   242                 event.action,
   243                 event.key,
   244                 all_mods,
   245             ),
   246         };
   247 
   248         if (opts.kitty_flags.report_events) {
   249             seq.event = switch (event.action) {
   250                 .press => .press,
   251                 .release => .release,
   252                 .repeat => .repeat,
   253             };
   254         }
   255 
   256         if (opts.kitty_flags.report_alternates) alternates: {
   257             // Break early if this is a control key
   258             if (isControl(seq.key)) break :alternates;
   259 
   260             const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch {
   261                 // Assume invalid UTF-8 means no UTF-8.
   262                 break :alternates;
   263             };
   264             var it = view.iterator();
   265 
   266             // If we have a codepoint in our UTF-8 sequence, then we can
   267             // report the shifted version.
   268             if (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp1| {
   269                 // Set the first alternate (shifted version)
   270                 if (cp1 != seq.key and seq.mods.shift) seq.alternates[0] = cp1;
   271 
   272                 // We want to know if there are additional codepoints because
   273                 // our logic below depends on the utf8 being a single codepoint.
   274                 const has_cp2 = it.nextCodepoint() != null;
   275 
   276                 // Set the base layout key. We only report this if this codepoint
   277                 // differs from our pressed key.
   278                 if (event.key.codepoint()) |base| {
   279                     if (base != seq.key and
   280                         (cp1 != base and !has_cp2))
   281                     {
   282                         seq.alternates[1] = base;
   283                     }
   284                 }
   285             } else {
   286                 // No UTF-8 so we can't report a shifted key but we can still
   287                 // report a base layout key.
   288                 if (event.key.codepoint()) |base| {
   289                     if (base != seq.key) seq.alternates[1] = base;
   290                 }
   291             }
   292         }
   293 
   294         if (opts.kitty_flags.report_associated and
   295             seq.event != .release)
   296         associated: {
   297             // Determine if the Alt modifier should be treated as an actual
   298             // modifier (in which case it prevents associated text) or as
   299             // the macOS Option key, which does not prevent associated text.
   300             const alt_prevents_text = if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos)
   301                 switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
   302                     .left => all_mods.sides.alt == .left,
   303                     .right => all_mods.sides.alt == .right,
   304                     .true => true,
   305                     .false => false,
   306                 }
   307             else
   308                 true;
   309 
   310             if (seq.mods.preventsText(alt_prevents_text)) break :associated;
   311 
   312             seq.text = event.utf8;
   313         }
   314 
   315         break :seq seq;
   316     };
   317 
   318     return try seq.encode(writer);
   319 }

n24 src/input/key_encode.zig:327–351 DIU
   327 fn legacy(
   328     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   329     event: key.KeyEvent,
   330     opts: Options,
   331 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
   332     const all_mods = event.mods;
   333     const effective_mods = event.effectiveMods();
   334     const binding_mods = effective_mods.binding();
   335 
   336     // Legacy encoding only does press/repeat
   337     if (event.action != .press and event.action != .repeat) return;
   338 
   339     // If we're in a dead key state then we never emit a sequence.
   340     if (event.composing) return;
   341 
   342     // If we match a PC style function key then that is our result.
   343     if (pcStyleFunctionKey(
   344         event.key,
   345         all_mods,
   346         opts.cursor_key_application,
   347         opts.keypad_key_application,
   348         opts.ignore_keypad_with_numlock,
   349         opts.modify_other_keys_state_2,
   350         opts.backarrow_key_mode,
   351     )) |sequence| pc_style: {

n24 src/input/key_encode.zig:352–378 I
   352         // If we have UTF-8 text, then we never emit PC style function
   353         // keys. Many function keys (escape, enter, backspace) have
   354         // a specific meaning when dead keys are active and so we don't
   355         // want to send that to the terminal. Examples:
   356         //
   357         //   - Japanese: escape clears the dead key state
   358         //   - Korean: escape commits the dead key state
   359         //   - Korean: backspace should delete a single preedit char
   360         //
   361         if (event.utf8.len > 0) utf8: {
   362             switch (event.key) {
   363                 else => {},
   364                 inline .backspace, .enter, .escape => |tag| {
   365                     // We want to ignore control characters. This is because
   366                     // some apprts (macOS) will send control characters as
   367                     // UTF-8 encodings and we handle that manually.
   368                     if (isControlUtf8(event.utf8)) break :utf8;
   369 
   370                     // Backspace encodes nothing because we modified IME.
   371                     // Enter/escape don't encode the PC-style encoding
   372                     // because we want to encode committed text.
   373                     if (comptime tag == .backspace) return;
   374                     break :pc_style;
   375                 },
   376             }
   377         }
   378 

n24 src/input/key_encode.zig:379–550 IU
   379         return try writer.writeAll(sequence);
   380     }
   381 
   382     // In modify other keys state 2, we send the CSI 27 sequence
   383     // for any char with a modifier. This must happen before converting
   384     // Ctrl+<char> to a C0 byte because mode 2 encodes those keys, too.
   385     const utf8 = event.utf8;
   386     if (opts.modify_other_keys_state_2) modify_other: {
   387         const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(utf8) catch {
   388             // Assume invalid UTF-8 means we no UTF-8.
   389             break :modify_other;
   390         };
   391         var it = view.iterator();
   392         const codepoint = it.nextCodepoint() orelse break :modify_other;
   393 
   394         // We only do this if we have a single codepoint. There shouldn't
   395         // ever be a multi-codepoint sequence that triggers this.
   396         if (it.nextCodepoint() != null) break :modify_other;
   397 
   398         // The mods we encode for this are just the binding mods (shift, ctrl,
   399         // super, alt unless it is actually option).
   400         const mods = mods: {
   401             var mods_binding = event.mods.binding();
   402             if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) alt: {
   403                 switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
   404                     .false => {},
   405                     .true => break :alt,
   406                     .left => if (event.mods.sides.alt == .left) break :alt,
   407                     .right => if (event.mods.sides.alt == .right) break :alt,
   408                 }
   409                 mods_binding.alt = false;
   410             }
   411             break :mods mods_binding;
   412         };
   413 
   414         // This copies xterm's `ModifyOtherKeys` function that returns
   415         // whether modify other keys should be encoded for the given
   416         // input.
   417         const should_modify = should_modify: {
   418             // xterm IsControlInput
   419             if (codepoint >= 0x40 and codepoint <= 0x7F)
   420                 break :should_modify true;
   421 
   422             // If we have anything other than shift pressed, encode.
   423             var mods_no_shift = mods;
   424             mods_no_shift.shift = false;
   425             if (!mods_no_shift.empty()) break :should_modify true;
   426 
   427             // We only have shift pressed. We only allow space.
   428             if (codepoint == ' ') break :should_modify true;
   429 
   430             // This logic isn't complete but I don't fully understand
   431             // the rest so I'm going to wait until we can have a
   432             // reasonable test scenario.
   433             break :should_modify false;
   434         };
   435 
   436         if (should_modify) {
   437             for (function_keys.modifiers, 2..) |modset, code| {
   438                 if (!mods.equal(modset)) continue;
   439                 return try writer.print(
   440                     "\x1B[27;{};{}~",
   441                     .{ code, codepoint },
   442                 );
   443             }
   444         }
   445     }
   446 
   447     // If we match a control sequence, we output that directly. For
   448     // ctrlSeq we have to use all mods because we want it to only
   449     // match ctrl+<char>.
   450     if (ctrlSeq(
   451         event.key,
   452         event.utf8,
   453         event.unshifted_codepoint,
   454         all_mods,
   455     )) |char| {
   456         // C0 sequences support alt-as-esc prefixing.
   457         if (binding_mods.alt) {
   458             try writer.writeByte(0x1B);
   459             try writer.writeByte(char);
   460             return;
   461         }
   462 
   463         try writer.writeByte(char);
   464         return;
   465     }
   466 
   467     // If we have no UTF8 text then the only possibility is the
   468     // alt-prefix handling of unshifted codepoints... so we process that.
   469     if (utf8.len == 0) {
   470         _ = try legacyAltPrefix(
   471             writer,
   472             event,
   473             binding_mods,
   474             all_mods,
   475             opts,
   476         );
   477         return;
   478     }
   479 
   480     // Let's see if we should apply fixterms to this codepoint.
   481     // At this stage of key processing, we only need to apply fixterms
   482     // to unicode codepoints if we have ctrl set.
   483     if (event.mods.ctrl) csiu: {
   484         // Important: we want to use the original mods here, not the
   485         // effective mods. The fixterms spec states the shifted chars
   486         // should be sent uppercase but Kitty changes that behavior
   487         // so we'll send all the mods.
   488         const csi_u_mods, const char = mods: {
   489             var mods = CsiUMods.fromInput(event.mods);
   490 
   491             // Get our codepoint. If we have more than one codepoint this
   492             // can't be valid CSIu.
   493             const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch break :csiu;
   494             var it = view.iterator();
   495             var char = it.nextCodepoint() orelse break :csiu;
   496             if (it.nextCodepoint() != null) break :csiu;
   497 
   498             // If our character is A to Z and we have shift set, then
   499             // we lowercase it. This is a Kitty-specific behavior that
   500             // we choose to follow and diverge from the fixterms spec.
   501             // This makes it easier for programs to detect shifted letters
   502             // for keybindings and is not just theoretical but used by
   503             // real programs.
   504             if (char >= 'A' and char <= 'Z' and mods.shift) {
   505                 // We want to rely on apprt to send us the correct
   506                 // unshifted codepoint...
   507                 char = @intCast(std.ascii.toLower(@intCast(char)));
   508             }
   509 
   510             // If our unshifted codepoint is identical to the shifted
   511             // then we consider shift. Otherwise, we do not because the
   512             // shift key was used to obtain the character. This is specified
   513             // by fixterms.
   514             if (event.unshifted_codepoint != char) {
   515                 mods.shift = false;
   516             }
   517 
   518             break :mods .{ mods, char };
   519         };
   520         return try writer.print(
   521             "\x1B[{};{}u",
   522             .{ char, csi_u_mods.seqInt() },
   523         );
   524     }
   525 
   526     // If we have alt-pressed and alt-esc-prefix is enabled, then
   527     // we need to prefix the utf8 sequence with an esc.
   528     if (try legacyAltPrefix(
   529         writer,
   530         event,
   531         binding_mods,
   532         all_mods,
   533         opts,
   534     )) return;
   535 
   536     // If we are on macOS, command+keys do not encode text. It isn't
   537     // typical for command+keys on macOS to ever encode text. They
   538     // don't in native text inputs (i.e. TextEdit) and they also don't
   539     // in other native terminals (Terminal.app officially but also
   540     // iTerm2).
   541     //
   542     // For Linux, we continue to encode text because it is typical.
   543     // For example on Gnome Console Super+b will encode a "b" character
   544     // with legacy encoding.
   545     if ((comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos) and all_mods.super) {
   546         return;
   547     }
   548 
   549     return try writer.writeAll(utf8);
   550 }

   552 fn legacyAltPrefix(
   553     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   554     event: key.KeyEvent,
   555     binding_mods: key.Mods,
   556     mods: key.Mods,
   557     opts: Options,
   558 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!bool {
   559     // This only takes effect with alt pressed
   560     if (!binding_mods.alt or !opts.alt_esc_prefix) return false;

n24 src/input/key_encode.zig:561–618 I
   561 
   562     // On macOS, we only handle option like alt in certain
   563     // circumstances. Otherwise, macOS does a unicode translation
   564     // and we allow that to happen.
   565     if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos) {
   566         switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
   567             .false => return false,
   568             .left => if (mods.sides.alt == .right) return false,
   569             .right => if (mods.sides.alt == .left) return false,
   570             .true => {},
   571         }
   572     }
   573 
   574     // A single byte is already the exact text we want to prefix. In
   575     // particular, this preserves shifted ASCII punctuation.
   576     const utf8 = event.utf8;
   577     if (utf8.len == 1) {
   578         try writer.writeByte(0x1B);
   579         try writer.writeAll(utf8);
   580         return true;
   581     }
   582 
   583     var unshifted_buf: [4]u8 = undefined;
   584     const value: []const u8 = value: {
   585         // On macOS, Option may translate the text into a different Unicode
   586         // value. When Option is configured as Alt, use the physical key's
   587         // unshifted codepoint just as the single-byte implementation did.
   588         if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos) {
   589             if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
   590                 const len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(
   591                     event.unshifted_codepoint,
   592                     &unshifted_buf,
   593                 ) catch return false;
   594                 break :value unshifted_buf[0..len];
   595             }
   596         }
   597 
   598         // Outside of the macOS translation case, prefix the complete UTF-8
   599         // text rather than truncating it to a single byte.
   600         if (utf8.len > 0) break :value utf8;
   601 
   602         // Frontends may omit UTF-8 while still supplying the physical key's
   603         // unshifted codepoint. Encode the complete scalar in that case.
   604         if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
   605             const len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(
   606                 event.unshifted_codepoint,
   607                 &unshifted_buf,
   608             ) catch return false;
   609             break :value unshifted_buf[0..len];
   610         }
   611 
   612         return false;
   613     };
   614 
   615     try writer.writeByte(0x1B);
   616     try writer.writeAll(value);
   617     return true;
   618 }

n24 src/input/key_encode.zig:631–699 DIU
   631 fn pcStyleFunctionKey(
   632     keyval: key.Key,
   633     mods: key.Mods,
   634     cursor_key_application: bool,
   635     keypad_key_application_req: bool,
   636     ignore_keypad_with_numlock: bool,
   637     modify_other_keys: bool, // True if state 2
   638     backarrow_key_mode: bool,
   639 ) ?[]const u8 {
   640     // We only want binding-sensitive mods because lock keys
   641     // and directional modifiers (left/right) don't matter for
   642     // pc-style function keys.
   643     const mods_int = mods.binding().int();
   644 
   645     // Keypad application keymode isn't super straightforward.
   646     // On xterm, in VT220 mode, numlock alone is enough to trigger
   647     // application mode. But in more modern modes, numlock is
   648     // ignored by default via mode 1035 (default true). If mode
   649     // 1035 is on, we always are in numerical keypad mode. If
   650     // mode 1035 is off, we are in application mode if the
   651     // proper numlock state is pressed. The numlock state is implicitly
   652     // determined based on the keycode sent (i.e. 1 with numlock
   653     // on will be kp_end).
   654     const keypad_key_application = keypad: {
   655         // If we're ignoring keypad then this is always false.
   656         // In other words, we're always in numerical keypad mode.
   657         if (ignore_keypad_with_numlock) break :keypad false;
   658 
   659         // If we're not ignoring then we enable the desired state.
   660         break :keypad keypad_key_application_req;
   661     };
   662 
   663     for (function_keys.keys.get(keyval)) |entry| {
   664         switch (entry.cursor) {
   665             .any => {},
   666             .normal => if (cursor_key_application) continue,
   667             .application => if (!cursor_key_application) continue,
   668         }
   669 
   670         switch (entry.keypad) {
   671             .any => {},
   672             .normal => if (keypad_key_application) continue,
   673             .application => if (!keypad_key_application) continue,
   674         }
   675 
   676         switch (entry.modify_other_keys) {
   677             .any => {},
   678             .set => if (modify_other_keys) continue,
   679             .set_other => if (!modify_other_keys) continue,
   680         }
   681 
   682         const entry_mods_int = entry.mods.int();
   683         if (entry_mods_int == 0) {
   684             if (mods_int != 0 and !entry.mods_empty_is_any) continue;
   685             // mods are either empty, or empty means any so we allow it.
   686         } else if (entry_mods_int != mods_int) {
   687             // any set mods require an exact match
   688             continue;
   689         }
   690 
   691         if (backarrow_key_mode)
   692             if (entry.sequence_decbkm) |sequence|
   693                 return sequence;
   694 
   695         return entry.sequence;
   696     }
   697 
   698     return null;
   699 }

n24 src/input/key_encode.zig:708–892 DIU
   708 fn ctrlSeq(
   709     logical_key: key.Key,
   710     utf8: []const u8,
   711     unshifted_codepoint: u21,
   712     mods: key.Mods,
   713 ) ?u8 {
   714     const ctrl_only = comptime (key.Mods{ .ctrl = true }).int();
   715 
   716     // If ctrl is not pressed then we never do anything.
   717     if (!mods.ctrl) return null;
   718 
   719     const char, const unset_mods = unset_mods: {
   720         // We need to only get binding modifiers so we strip lock
   721         // keys, sides, etc.
   722         var unset_mods = mods.binding();
   723 
   724         // Remove alt from our modifiers because it does not impact whether
   725         // we are generating a ctrl sequence and we handle the ESC-prefix
   726         // logic separately.
   727         unset_mods.alt = false;
   728 
   729         var char: u8 = char: {
   730             // If we have exactly one UTF8 byte, we assume that is the
   731             // character we want to convert to a C0 byte.
   732             if (utf8.len == 1) break :char utf8[0];
   733 
   734             // If we have a logical key that maps to a single byte
   735             // printable character, we use that. History to explain this:
   736             // this was added to support cyrillic keyboard layouts such
   737             // as Russian and Mongolian. These layouts have a `c` key that
   738             // maps to U+0441 (cyrillic small letter "c") but every
   739             // terminal I've tested encodes this as ctrl+c.
   740             if (logical_key.codepoint()) |cp| {
   741                 if (std.math.cast(u8, cp)) |byte| {
   742                     // For this specific case, we only map to the key if
   743                     // we have exactly ctrl pressed. This is because shift
   744                     // would modify the key and we don't know how to do that
   745                     // properly here (don't have the layout). And we want
   746                     // to encode shift as CSIu.
   747                     if (unset_mods.int() != ctrl_only) return null;
   748                     break :char byte;
   749                 }
   750             }
   751 
   752             // Otherwise we don't have a character to convert that
   753             // we can reliably map to a C0 byte.
   754             return null;
   755         };
   756 
   757         // Remove shift if we have something outside of the US letter
   758         // range. This is so that characters such as `ctrl+shift+-`
   759         // generate the correct ctrl-seq (used by emacs).
   760         if (unset_mods.shift and (char < 'A' or char > 'Z')) shift: {
   761             // Special case for fixterms awkward case as specified.
   762             if (char == '@') break :shift;
   763             unset_mods.shift = false;
   764         }
   765 
   766         // If the character is uppercase, we convert it to lowercase. We
   767         // rely on the unshifted codepoint to do this. This handles
   768         // the scenario where we have caps lock pressed. Note that
   769         // shifted characters are handled above, if we are just pressing
   770         // shift then the ctrl-only check will fail later and we won't
   771         // ctrl-seq encode.
   772         if (char >= 'A' and char <= 'Z' and unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
   773             if (std.math.cast(u8, unshifted_codepoint)) |byte| {
   774                 char = byte;
   775             }
   776         }
   777 
   778         // An additional note on caps lock and shift interaction.
   779         // If we have caps lock set and an ASCII letter is pressed,
   780         // we lowercase it (above). If we have only control pressed,
   781         // we process it as a ctrl seq. For example ctrl+M with caps
   782         // lock but no shift will encode as 0x0D.
   783         //
   784         // But, if you press ctrl+shift+m, this will not encode as a
   785         // ctrl-seq and falls through to CSIu encoding. This lets programs
   786         // detect the difference between ctrl+M and ctrl+shift+M. This
   787         // diverges from the fixterms "spec" and most terminals. This
   788         // only matches Kitty in behavior. But I believe this is a
   789         // justified divergence because it's a useful distinction.
   790 
   791         break :unset_mods .{ char, unset_mods };
   792     };
   793 
   794     // After unsetting, we only continue if we have ONLY control set.
   795     if (unset_mods.int() != ctrl_only) return null;
   796 
   797     // From Kitty's key encoding logic. I tried to discern the exact
   798     // behavior across different terminals but it's not clear, so I'm
   799     // just going to repeat what Kitty does.
   800     return switch (char) {
   801         ' ' => 0,
   802         '/' => 31,
   803         '0' => 48,
   804         '1' => 49,
   805         '2' => 0,
   806         '3' => 27,
   807         '4' => 28,
   808         '5' => 29,
   809         '6' => 30,
   810         '7' => 31,
   811         '8' => 127,
   812         '9' => 57,
   813         '?' => 127,
   814         '@' => 0,
   815         '\\' => 28,
   816         ']' => 29,
   817         '^' => 30,
   818         '_' => 31,
   819         'a' => 1,
   820         'b' => 2,
   821         'c' => 3,
   822         'd' => 4,
   823         'e' => 5,
   824         'f' => 6,
   825         'g' => 7,
   826         'h' => 8,
   827         'j' => 10,
   828         'k' => 11,
   829         'l' => 12,
   830         'n' => 14,
   831         'o' => 15,
   832         'p' => 16,
   833         'q' => 17,
   834         'r' => 18,
   835         's' => 19,
   836         't' => 20,
   837         'u' => 21,
   838         'v' => 22,
   839         'w' => 23,
   840         'x' => 24,
   841         'y' => 25,
   842         'z' => 26,
   843         '~' => 30,
   844 
   845         // These are purposely NOT handled here because of the fixterms
   846         // specification: https://www.leonerd.org.uk/hacks/fixterms/
   847         // These are processed as CSI u.
   848         // 'i' => 0x09,
   849         // 'm' => 0x0D,
   850         // '[' => 0x1B,
   851 
   852         else => null,
   853     };
   854 }
   855 
   856 /// Returns true if this is an ASCII control character, matches libc implementation.
   857 fn isControl(cp: u21) bool {
   858     return cp < 0x20 or cp == 0x7F;
   859 }
   860 
   861 /// Returns true if this string is comprised of a single
   862 /// control character. This returns false for multi-byte strings.
   863 fn isControlUtf8(str: []const u8) bool {
   864     return str.len == 1 and isControl(@intCast(str[0]));
   865 }
   866 
   867 /// This is the bitmask for fixterm CSI u modifiers.
   868 const CsiUMods = packed struct(u3) {
   869     shift: bool = false,
   870     alt: bool = false,
   871     ctrl: bool = false,
   872 
   873     /// Convert an input mods value into the CSI u mods value.
   874     pub fn fromInput(mods: key.Mods) CsiUMods {
   875         return .{
   876             .shift = mods.shift,
   877             .alt = mods.alt,
   878             .ctrl = mods.ctrl,
   879         };
   880     }
   881 
   882     /// Returns the raw int value of this packed struct.
   883     pub fn int(self: CsiUMods) u3 {
   884         return @bitCast(self);
   885     }
   886 
   887     /// Returns the integer value sent as part of the CSI u sequence.
   888     /// This adds 1 to the bitmask value as described in the spec.
   889     pub fn seqInt(self: CsiUMods) u4 {
   890         const raw: u4 = @intCast(self.int());
   891         return raw + 1;
   892     }
