n26 src/terminal/Terminal.zig:5868–5911 DIU
  5868 pub fn fullReset(self: *Terminal) void {
  5869     // Ensure we're back on primary screen
  5870     self.screens.switchTo(.primary);
  5871 
  5872     // Remove alternate screen
  5873     self.screens.remove(self.screens.active.alloc, .alternate);
  5874 
  5875     // Reset primary screen
  5876     self.screens.active.reset();
  5877 
  5878     // Reset our basic state
  5879     self.flags = .{
  5880         // Visibility belongs to the view rather than terminal state, so a
  5881         // terminal reset must not make a hidden view potentially visible.
  5882         .visible = self.flags.visible,
  5883 
  5884         // This is configuration based on the pty rather than terminal
  5885         // state, so a terminal reset must not change it.
  5886         .resize_pull_scrollback = self.flags.resize_pull_scrollback,
  5887 
  5888         .xt_checksum = self.default_xt_checksum,
  5889     };
  5890     self.modes.reset();
  5891     self.tabstops.reset(TABSTOP_INTERVAL);
  5892     self.previous_char = null;
  5893     self.pwd.clearRetainingCapacity();
  5894     self.title.clearRetainingCapacity();
  5895     self.glyph_glossary.clearAndFree(self.gpa());
  5896     // A reset only interrupts an in-progress chunked OSC 72 command;
  5897     // drag and drop registration survives, matching kitty.
  5898     if (self.kitty_dnd) |dnd| dnd.chunking = .{};
  5899     self.status_display = .main;
  5900     self.scrolling_region = .{
  5901         .top = 0,
  5902         .bottom = self.rows - 1,
  5903         .left = 0,
  5904         .right = self.cols - 1,
  5905     };
  5906     self.setCursorStyle(.default);
  5907     self.colors.palette.resetAll();
  5908 
  5909     // Always mark dirty so we redraw everything
  5910     self.flags.dirty.clear = true;
  5911 }

n26 src/termio/Termio.zig:604–679 DIU
   604 /// Clear the screen.
   605 pub fn clearScreen(self: *Termio, td: *ThreadData, history: bool) !void {
   606     {
   607         self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
   608         defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
   609 
   610         // If we're on the alternate screen, we do not clear. Since this is an
   611         // emulator-level screen clear, this messes up the running programs
   612         // knowledge of where the cursor is and causes rendering issues. So,
   613         // for alt screen, we do nothing.
   614         if (self.terminal.screens.active_key == .alternate) return;
   615 
   616         // Clear our selection
   617         self.terminal.screens.active.clearSelection();
   618 
   619         // Clear our scrollback
   620         if (history) self.terminal.eraseDisplay(.scrollback, false);
   621 
   622         // If we're not at a prompt, we just delete above the cursor.
   623         if (!self.terminal.cursorIsAtPrompt()) {
   624             if (self.terminal.screens.active.cursor.y > 0) {
   625                 self.terminal.screens.active.eraseActive(
   626                     self.terminal.screens.active.cursor.y - 1,
   627                 );
   628             }
   629 
   630             // Clear all Kitty graphics state for this screen. This copies
   631             // Kitty's behavior when Cmd+K deletes all Kitty graphics. I
   632             // didn't spend time researching whether it only deletes Kitty
   633             // graphics that are placed above the cursor or if it deletes
   634             // all of them. We delete all of them for now but if this behavior
   635             // isn't fully correct we should fix this later.
   636             self.terminal.screens.active.kitty_images.delete(
   637                 self.terminal.io(),
   638                 self.terminal.screens.active.alloc,
   639                 &self.terminal,
   640                 .{ .all = true },
   641             );
   642 
   643             return;
   644         }
   645 
   646         // At a prompt, we want to first fully clear the screen, and then after
   647         // send a FF (0x0C) to the shell so that it can repaint the screen.
   648         // Mark the current row as a not a prompt so we can properly
   649         // clear the full screen in the next eraseDisplay call.
   650         // TODO: fix this
   651         // self.terminal.markSemanticPrompt(.command);
   652         // assert(!self.terminal.cursorIsAtPrompt());
   653         self.terminal.eraseDisplay(.complete, false);
   654     }
   655 
   656     // If we reached here it means we're at a prompt, so we send a form-feed.
   657     try self.queueWrite(td, &[_]u8{0x0C}, false);
   658 }
   659 
   660 /// Scroll the viewport
   661 pub fn scrollViewport(
   662     self: *Termio,
   663     scroll: terminalpkg.Terminal.ScrollViewport,
   664 ) void {
   665     self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
   666     defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
   667     self.terminal.scrollViewport(scroll);
   668 }
   669 
   670 /// Jump the viewport to the prompt.
   671 pub fn jumpToPrompt(self: *Termio, delta: isize) !void {
   672     {
   673         self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
   674         defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
   675         self.terminal.screens.active.scroll(.{ .delta_prompt = delta });
   676     }
   677 
   678     try self.renderer_wakeup.notify();
   679 }

n27 src/Surface.zig:2938–2961 IU
  2938     // Encode and send our key. If we didn't encode anything, then we
  2939     // return the effect as ignored.
  2940     if (try self.encodeKey(
  2941         event,
  2942         if (insp_ev) |*ev| ev else null,
  2943     )) |write_req| {
  2944         // If our process is exited and we press a key that results in
  2945         // an encoded value, we close the surface. We want to eventually
  2946         // move this behavior to the apprt probably.
  2947         if (self.child_exited) {
  2948             write_req.deinit();
  2949             self.close();
  2950             return .closed;
  2951         }
  2952 
  2953         self.queueIo(switch (write_req) {
  2954             .small => |v| .{ .write_small = v },
  2955             .stable => |v| .{ .write_stable = v },
  2956             .alloc => |v| .{ .write_alloc = v },
  2957         }, .unlocked);
  2958     } else {
  2959         // No valid request means that we didn't encode anything.
  2960         return .ignored;
  2961     }

n27 src/Surface.zig:3324–3419 DIU
  3324 /// Encodes the key event into a write request. The write request will
  3325 /// always copy or allocate so the caller can safely free the event.
  3326 fn encodeKey(
  3327     self: *Surface,
  3328     event: input.KeyEvent,
  3329     insp_ev: ?*inspectorpkg.KeyEvent,
  3330 ) !?termio.Message.WriteReq {
  3331     const write_req: termio.Message.WriteReq = req: {
  3332         // Build our encoding options, which requires the lock.
  3333         const encoding_opts = self.encodeKeyOpts();
  3334 
  3335         // Try to write the input into a small array. This fits almost
  3336         // every scenario. Larger situations can happen due to long
  3337         // pre-edits.
  3338         var data: termio.Message.WriteReq.Small.Array = undefined;
  3339         var writer: std.Io.Writer = .fixed(&data);
  3340         if (input.key_encode.encode(
  3341             &writer,
  3342             event,
  3343             encoding_opts,
  3344         )) {
  3345             const written = writer.buffered();
  3346 
  3347             // Special-case: we did nothing.
  3348             if (written.len == 0) return null;
  3349 
  3350             break :req .{ .small = .{
  3351                 .data = data,
  3352                 .len = @intCast(written.len),
  3353             } };
  3354         } else |err| switch (err) {
  3355             // Means we need to allocate
  3356             error.WriteFailed => {},
  3357         }
  3358 
  3359         // We need to allocate. We allocate double the UTF-8 length
  3360         // or double the small array size, whichever is larger. That's
  3361         // a heuristic that should work. The only scenario I know while
  3362         // typing this where we don't have enough space is a long preedit,
  3363         // and in that case the size we need is exactly the UTF-8 length,
  3364         // so the double is being safe.
  3365         var alloc_writer: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = try .initCapacity(
  3366             self.alloc,
  3367             @max(event.utf8.len * 2, data.len * 2),
  3368         );
  3369         defer alloc_writer.deinit();
  3370 
  3371         try input.key_encode.encode(
  3372             &alloc_writer.writer,
  3373             event,
  3374             encoding_opts,
  3375         );
  3376         break :req .{ .alloc = .{
  3377             .alloc = self.alloc,
  3378             .data = try alloc_writer.toOwnedSlice(),
  3379         } };
  3380     };
  3381 
  3382     // Copy the encoded data into the inspector event if we have one.
  3383     // We do this before the mailbox because the IO thread could
  3384     // release the memory before we get a chance to copy it.
  3385     if (insp_ev) |ev| pty: {
  3386         const slice = write_req.slice();
  3387         const copy = self.alloc.alloc(u8, slice.len) catch |err| {
  3388             log.warn("error allocating pty data for inspector err={}", .{err});
  3389             break :pty;
  3390         };
  3391         errdefer self.alloc.free(copy);
  3392         @memcpy(copy, slice);
  3393         ev.pty = copy;
  3394     }
  3395 
  3396     return write_req;
  3397 }
  3398 
  3399 fn encodeKeyOpts(self: *const Surface) input.key_encode.Options {
  3400     self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  3401     defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  3402     const t = &self.io.terminal;
  3403 
  3404     var opts: input.key_encode.Options = .fromTerminal(t);
  3405     if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag != .macos) return opts;
  3406 
  3407     opts.macos_option_as_alt = self.config.macos_option_as_alt orelse detect: {
  3408         // If we don't have alt pressed, it doesn't matter what this
  3409         // config is so we can just say "false" and break out and avoid
  3410         // more expensive checks below.
  3411         if (!self.mouse.mods.alt) break :detect .false;
  3412 
  3413         // Alt is pressed, we're on macOS. We break some encapsulation
  3414         // here and assume libghostty for ease...
  3415         break :detect self.rt_app.keyboardLayout().detectOptionAsAlt();
  3416     };
  3417 
  3418     return opts;
  3419 }

    12 pub const CursorMode = enum { any, normal, application };
    13 pub const KeypadMode = enum { any, normal, application };
    14 
    15 /// A bit confusing so I'll document this one: this is the "modify other keys"
    16 /// setting. We only change behavior for "set_other" which is ESC [ > 4; 2 m.
    17 /// So this can be "any" which means we don't care what's going on. Or it
    18 /// can be "set" which means modify keys must be set EXCEPT FOR "other keys"
    19 /// mode, and "set_other" which means modify keys must be set to "other keys"
    20 /// mode.
    21 ///
    22 /// See: https://invisible-island.net/xterm/modified-keys.html
    23 pub const ModifyKeys = enum {
    24     any,
    25     set,
    26     set_other,
    27 };
    28 
    29 /// A single entry in the table of keys.
    30 pub const Entry = struct {
    31     /// The exact set of modifiers that must be active for this entry to match.
    32     /// If mods_empty_is_any is true then empty mods means any set of mods can
    33     /// match. Otherwise, empty mods means no mods must be active.
    34     mods: key.Mods = .{},
    35     mods_empty_is_any: bool = true,
    36 
    37     /// The state required for cursor/keypad mode.
    38     cursor: CursorMode = .any,
    39     keypad: KeypadMode = .any,
    40 
    41     /// Whether or not this entry should be used
    42     modify_other_keys: ModifyKeys = .any,
    43 
    44     /// The sequence to send to the pty if this entry matches.
    45     sequence: []const u8,
    46 
    47     /// Sequence to send to the PTY if DECBKM is set.
    48     sequence_decbkm: ?[]const u8 = null,
    49 };

n27 src/input/function_keys.zig:264–314 DIU
   264 /// Returns the default keypad application mode entry.
   265 fn kpDefault(comptime suffix: []const u8) []const Entry {
   266     return &.{
   267         .{
   268             .mods_empty_is_any = false,
   269             .keypad = .application,
   270             .sequence = "\x1bO" ++ suffix,
   271         },
   272     };
   273 }
   274 
   275 /// Returns the entries for a keypad key. The suffix is the final character
   276 /// of the sent sequence, such as "r" for kp_2.
   277 fn kpKeys(comptime suffix: []const u8, comptime normal: []const u8) []const Entry {
   278     const pc = pcStyle("\x1bO{}" ++ suffix);
   279     for (pc) |*entry| entry.keypad = .application;
   280     return kpDefault(suffix) ++ pc ++ .{
   281         Entry{ .keypad = .normal, .sequence = normal },
   282     };
   283 }
   284 
   285 /// Returns entries that are dependent on cursor key settings.
   286 fn cursorKey(
   287     comptime normal: []const u8,
   288     comptime application: []const u8,
   289 ) []const Entry {
   290     return &.{
   291         .{ .cursor = .normal, .sequence = normal },
   292         .{ .cursor = .application, .sequence = application },
   293     };
   294 }
   295 
   296 /// Constructs a set of pcStyle function keys using the given format. The
   297 /// format should have exactly one "hole" for the mods code.
   298 /// Example: "\x1b[11;{}~" for F1.
   299 fn pcStyle(comptime fmt: []const u8) []Entry {
   300     // The comptime {} wrapper is superfluous but it prevents us from
   301     // accidentally running this function at runtime.
   302     comptime {
   303         @setEvalBranchQuota(500_000);
   304         var entries: [modifiers.len]Entry = undefined;
   305         for (modifiers, 2.., 0..) |mods, code, i| {
   306             entries[i] = .{
   307                 .mods = mods,
   308                 .sequence = std.fmt.comptimePrint(fmt, .{code}),
   309             };
   310         }
   311 
   312         return &entries;
   313     }
   314 }

n27 src/input/key.zig:16–55 DI
    16 pub const KeyEvent = struct {
    17     /// The action: press, release, etc.
    18     action: Action = .press,
    19 
    20     /// The keycode of the physical key that was pressed. This is agnostic
    21     /// to the layout. Layout-dependent matching can only be done via the
    22     /// UTF-8 or unshifted codepoint.
    23     key: Key = .unidentified,
    24 
    25     /// Mods are the modifiers that are pressed.
    26     mods: Mods = .{},
    27 
    28     /// The mods that were consumed in order to generate the text
    29     /// in utf8. This has the mods set that were consumed, so to
    30     /// get the set of mods that are effective you must negate
    31     /// mods with this.
    32     ///
    33     /// This field is meaningless if utf8 is empty.
    34     consumed_mods: Mods = .{},
    35 
    36     /// Composing is true when this key event is part of a dead key
    37     /// composition sequence and we're in the middle of it.
    38     composing: bool = false,
    39 
    40     /// The utf8 sequence that was generated by this key event.
    41     /// This will be an empty string if there is no text generated.
    42     /// If composing is true and this is non-empty, this is preedit
    43     /// text.
    44     utf8: []const u8 = "",
    45 
    46     /// The codepoint for this key when it is unshifted. For example,
    47     /// shift+a is "A" in UTF-8 but unshifted would provide 'a'.
    48     unshifted_codepoint: u21 = 0,
    49 
    50     /// Returns the effective modifiers for this event. The effective
    51     /// modifiers are the mods that should be considered for keybindings.
    52     pub fn effectiveMods(self: KeyEvent) Mods {
    53         if (self.utf8.len == 0) return self.mods;
    54         return self.mods.unset(self.consumed_mods);
    55     }

    14 pub const Options = struct {
    15     /// Terminal DEC mode 1
    16     cursor_key_application: bool = false,
    17 
    18     /// Terminal DEC mode 66
    19     keypad_key_application: bool = false,
    20 
    21     // DEC Backarrow Key Mode (DECBKM)
    22     // See https://vt100.net/dec/ek-vt3xx-tp-002.pdf page 170
    23     // If `false` (the default), `backspace` emits 0x7f
    24     // If `true`, `backspace` emits 0x08
    25     backarrow_key_mode: bool = false,
    26 
    27     /// Terminal DEC mode 1035
    28     ignore_keypad_with_numlock: bool = false,
    29 
    30     /// Terminal DEC mode 1036
    31     alt_esc_prefix: bool = false,
    32 
    33     /// xterm "modifyOtherKeys mode 2". Details here:
    34     /// https://invisible-island.net/xterm/modified-keys.html
    35     modify_other_keys_state_2: bool = false,
    36 
    37     /// Kitty keyboard protocol flags.
    38     kitty_flags: KittyFlags = .disabled,

n27 src/input/key_encode.zig:55–97 DIU
    55     /// Initialize our options from the terminal state.
    56     ///
    57     /// Note that `macos_option_as_alt` cannot be determined from
    58     /// terminal state so it must be set manually after this call.
    59     pub fn fromTerminal(t: *const Terminal) Options {
    60         return .{
    61             .alt_esc_prefix = t.modes.get(.alt_esc_prefix),
    62             .cursor_key_application = t.modes.get(.cursor_keys),
    63             .keypad_key_application = t.modes.get(.keypad_keys),
    64             .backarrow_key_mode = t.modes.get(.backarrow_key_mode),
    65             .ignore_keypad_with_numlock = t.modes.get(.ignore_keypad_with_numlock),
    66             .modify_other_keys_state_2 = t.flags.modify_other_keys_2,
    67             .kitty_flags = t.screens.active.kitty_keyboard.current(),
    68 
    69             // These can't be known from the terminal state.
    70             .macos_option_as_alt = .false,
    71         };
    72     }
    73 };
    74 
    75 /// Encode the key event to the writer in the proper format given
    76 /// the options. For example, this will properly encode a key press
    77 /// such as "ctrl+A" to Kitty format if Kitty encoding is enabled.
    78 ///
    79 /// Not all key events will result in output. It is up to the caller
    80 /// to use a writer that can track whether any output was written if
    81 /// they care about that.
    82 pub fn encode(
    83     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
    84     event: key.KeyEvent,
    85     opts: Options,
    86 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
    87     //std.log.warn("KEYENCODER event={} opts={}", .{ event, opts });
    88     return if (opts.kitty_flags.int() != 0) try kitty(
    89         writer,
    90         event,
    91         opts,
    92     ) else try legacy(
    93         writer,
    94         event,
    95         opts,
    96     );
    97 }

n27 src/input/key_encode.zig:99–319 DIU
    99 /// Perform Kitty keyboard protocol encoding of the key event.
   100 fn kitty(
   101     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   102     event: key.KeyEvent,
   103     opts: Options,
   104 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
   105     // This should never happen but we'll check anyway.
   106     if (opts.kitty_flags.int() == 0) return try legacy(
   107         writer,
   108         event,
   109         opts,
   110     );
   111 
   112     // We only processed "press" events unless report events is active
   113     if (event.action == .release) {
   114         if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_events) return;
   115 
   116         // Enter, backspace, and tab do not report release events unless "report
   117         // all" is set
   118         if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_all) {
   119             switch (event.key) {
   120                 .enter, .backspace, .tab => return,
   121                 else => {},
   122             }
   123         }
   124     }
   125 
   126     const all_mods = event.mods;
   127     const effective_mods = event.effectiveMods();
   128     const binding_mods = effective_mods.binding();
   129 
   130     // Find the entry for this key in the kitty table.
   131     const entry_: ?KittyEntry = entry: {
   132         // Functional or predefined keys
   133         for (kitty_entries) |entry| {
   134             if (entry.key == event.key) break :entry entry;
   135         }
   136 
   137         // Otherwise, we use our unicode codepoint from UTF8. We
   138         // always use the unshifted value.
   139         if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
   140             break :entry .{
   141                 .key = event.key,
   142                 .code = event.unshifted_codepoint,
   143                 .final = 'u',
   144                 .modifier = false,
   145             };
   146         }
   147 
   148         break :entry null;
   149     };
   150 
   151     preprocessing: {
   152         // When composing, the only keys sent are plain modifiers.
   153         if (event.composing) {
   154             if (entry_) |entry| {
   155                 if (entry.modifier) break :preprocessing;
   156             }
   157 
   158             return;
   159         }
   160 
   161         // IME confirmation still sends an enter key so if we have enter
   162         // and UTF8 text we just send it directly since we assume that is
   163         // what's happening. See legacy()'s similar logic for more details
   164         // on how to verify this.
   165         if (event.utf8.len > 0) utf8: {
   166             switch (event.key) {
   167                 else => {},
   168                 inline .enter, .backspace => |tag| {
   169                     // See legacy for why we handle this way.
   170                     if (isControlUtf8(event.utf8)) break :utf8;
   171                     if (comptime tag == .backspace) return;
   172                     return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
   173                 },
   174             }
   175         }
   176 
   177         // If we're reporting all then we always send CSI sequences.
   178         if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_all) {
   179             // Quote:
   180             // The only exceptions are the Enter, Tab and Backspace keys which
   181             // still generate the same bytes as in legacy mode this is to allow the
   182             // user to type and execute commands in the shell such as reset after a
   183             // program that sets this mode crashes without clearing it.
   184             //
   185             // Quote ("report all" mode):
   186             // Note that all keys are reported as escape codes, including Enter,
   187             // Tab, Backspace etc.
   188             if (binding_mods.empty()) {
   189                 switch (event.key) {
   190                     .enter => return try writer.writeByte('\r'),
   191                     .tab => return try writer.writeByte('\t'),
   192                     .backspace => return try writer.writeByte(0x7F),
   193                     else => {},
   194                 }
   195             }
   196 
   197             // Send plain-text non-modified text directly to the terminal.
   198             // We don't send release events because those are specially encoded.
   199             if (event.utf8.len > 0 and
   200                 binding_mods.empty() and
   201                 event.action != .release)
   202             plain_text: {
   203                 // We only do this for printable characters. We should
   204                 // inspect the real unicode codepoint properties here but
   205                 // the real world issue is usually control characters.
   206                 const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch {
   207                     // Invalid UTF-8 so let's fallback to encoding the
   208                     // key press as if it didn't produce UTF-8 text. I'm
   209                     // not sure what should happen here according to the spec,
   210                     // since it doesn't specify this behavior. Presumably
   211                     // this is a caller bug.
   212                     break :plain_text;
   213                 };
   214                 var it = view.iterator();
   215                 while (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp| {
   216                     if (isControl(cp)) break :plain_text;
   217                 }
   218 
   219                 return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
   220             }
   221         }
   222     }
   223 
   224     const entry = entry_ orelse {
   225         // No entry found. If we have UTF-8 text this is a pure text event
   226         // (e.g. composed/IME text), so send it as-is so programs can
   227         // still receive it. Release events never insert text, same as the
   228         // plain-text path above.
   229         if (event.action == .release) return;
   230         if (event.utf8.len == 0) return;
   231         return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
   232     };
   233 
   234     // If this is just a modifier we require "report all" to send the sequence.
   235     if (entry.modifier and !opts.kitty_flags.report_all) return;
   236 
   237     const seq: KittySequence = seq: {
   238         var seq: KittySequence = .{
   239             .key = entry.code,
   240             .final = entry.final,
   241             .mods = .fromInput(
   242                 event.action,
   243                 event.key,
   244                 all_mods,
   245             ),
   246         };
   247 
   248         if (opts.kitty_flags.report_events) {
   249             seq.event = switch (event.action) {
   250                 .press => .press,
   251                 .release => .release,
   252                 .repeat => .repeat,
   253             };
   254         }
   255 
   256         if (opts.kitty_flags.report_alternates) alternates: {
   257             // Break early if this is a control key
   258             if (isControl(seq.key)) break :alternates;
   259 
   260             const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch {
   261                 // Assume invalid UTF-8 means no UTF-8.
   262                 break :alternates;
   263             };
   264             var it = view.iterator();
   265 
   266             // If we have a codepoint in our UTF-8 sequence, then we can
   267             // report the shifted version.
   268             if (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp1| {
   269                 // Set the first alternate (shifted version)
   270                 if (cp1 != seq.key and seq.mods.shift) seq.alternates[0] = cp1;
   271 
   272                 // We want to know if there are additional codepoints because
   273                 // our logic below depends on the utf8 being a single codepoint.
   274                 const has_cp2 = it.nextCodepoint() != null;
   275 
   276                 // Set the base layout key. We only report this if this codepoint
   277                 // differs from our pressed key.
   278                 if (event.key.codepoint()) |base| {
   279                     if (base != seq.key and
   280                         (cp1 != base and !has_cp2))
   281                     {
   282                         seq.alternates[1] = base;
   283                     }
   284                 }
   285             } else {
   286                 // No UTF-8 so we can't report a shifted key but we can still
   287                 // report a base layout key.
   288                 if (event.key.codepoint()) |base| {
   289                     if (base != seq.key) seq.alternates[1] = base;
   290                 }
   291             }
   292         }
   293 
   294         if (opts.kitty_flags.report_associated and
   295             seq.event != .release)
   296         associated: {
   297             // Determine if the Alt modifier should be treated as an actual
   298             // modifier (in which case it prevents associated text) or as
   299             // the macOS Option key, which does not prevent associated text.
   300             const alt_prevents_text = if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos)
   301                 switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
   302                     .left => all_mods.sides.alt == .left,
   303                     .right => all_mods.sides.alt == .right,
   304                     .true => true,
   305                     .false => false,
   306                 }
   307             else
   308                 true;
   309 
   310             if (seq.mods.preventsText(alt_prevents_text)) break :associated;
   311 
   312             seq.text = event.utf8;
   313         }
   314 
   315         break :seq seq;
   316     };
   317 
   318     return try seq.encode(writer);
   319 }

   327 fn legacy(
   328     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   329     event: key.KeyEvent,
   330     opts: Options,

   332     const all_mods = event.mods;
   333     const effective_mods = event.effectiveMods();
   334     const binding_mods = effective_mods.binding();
   335 
   336     // Legacy encoding only does press/repeat
   337     if (event.action != .press and event.action != .repeat) return;
   338 
   339     // If we're in a dead key state then we never emit a sequence.
   340     if (event.composing) return;
   341 
   342     // If we match a PC style function key then that is our result.
   343     if (pcStyleFunctionKey(
   344         event.key,

   346         opts.cursor_key_application,
   347         opts.keypad_key_application,
   348         opts.ignore_keypad_with_numlock,
   349         opts.modify_other_keys_state_2,

n27 src/input/key_encode.zig:352–378 I
   352         // If we have UTF-8 text, then we never emit PC style function
   353         // keys. Many function keys (escape, enter, backspace) have
   354         // a specific meaning when dead keys are active and so we don't
   355         // want to send that to the terminal. Examples:
   356         //
   357         //   - Japanese: escape clears the dead key state
   358         //   - Korean: escape commits the dead key state
   359         //   - Korean: backspace should delete a single preedit char
   360         //
   361         if (event.utf8.len > 0) utf8: {
   362             switch (event.key) {
   363                 else => {},
   364                 inline .backspace, .enter, .escape => |tag| {
   365                     // We want to ignore control characters. This is because
   366                     // some apprts (macOS) will send control characters as
   367                     // UTF-8 encodings and we handle that manually.
   368                     if (isControlUtf8(event.utf8)) break :utf8;
   369 
   370                     // Backspace encodes nothing because we modified IME.
   371                     // Enter/escape don't encode the PC-style encoding
   372                     // because we want to encode committed text.
   373                     if (comptime tag == .backspace) return;
   374                     break :pc_style;
   375                 },
   376             }
   377         }
   378 

n27 src/input/key_encode.zig:379–550 IU
   379         return try writer.writeAll(sequence);
   380     }
   381 
   382     // In modify other keys state 2, we send the CSI 27 sequence
   383     // for any char with a modifier. This must happen before converting
   384     // Ctrl+<char> to a C0 byte because mode 2 encodes those keys, too.
   385     const utf8 = event.utf8;
   386     if (opts.modify_other_keys_state_2) modify_other: {
   387         const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(utf8) catch {
   388             // Assume invalid UTF-8 means we no UTF-8.
   389             break :modify_other;
   390         };
   391         var it = view.iterator();
   392         const codepoint = it.nextCodepoint() orelse break :modify_other;
   393 
   394         // We only do this if we have a single codepoint. There shouldn't
   395         // ever be a multi-codepoint sequence that triggers this.
   396         if (it.nextCodepoint() != null) break :modify_other;
   397 
   398         // The mods we encode for this are just the binding mods (shift, ctrl,
   399         // super, alt unless it is actually option).
   400         const mods = mods: {
   401             var mods_binding = event.mods.binding();
   402             if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) alt: {
   403                 switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
   404                     .false => {},
   405                     .true => break :alt,
   406                     .left => if (event.mods.sides.alt == .left) break :alt,
   407                     .right => if (event.mods.sides.alt == .right) break :alt,
   408                 }
   409                 mods_binding.alt = false;
   410             }
   411             break :mods mods_binding;
   412         };
   413 
   414         // This copies xterm's `ModifyOtherKeys` function that returns
   415         // whether modify other keys should be encoded for the given
   416         // input.
   417         const should_modify = should_modify: {
   418             // xterm IsControlInput
   419             if (codepoint >= 0x40 and codepoint <= 0x7F)
   420                 break :should_modify true;
   421 
   422             // If we have anything other than shift pressed, encode.
   423             var mods_no_shift = mods;
   424             mods_no_shift.shift = false;
   425             if (!mods_no_shift.empty()) break :should_modify true;
   426 
   427             // We only have shift pressed. We only allow space.
   428             if (codepoint == ' ') break :should_modify true;
   429 
   430             // This logic isn't complete but I don't fully understand
   431             // the rest so I'm going to wait until we can have a
   432             // reasonable test scenario.
   433             break :should_modify false;
   434         };
   435 
   436         if (should_modify) {
   437             for (function_keys.modifiers, 2..) |modset, code| {
   438                 if (!mods.equal(modset)) continue;
   439                 return try writer.print(
   440                     "\x1B[27;{};{}~",
   441                     .{ code, codepoint },
   442                 );
   443             }
   444         }
   445     }
   446 
   447     // If we match a control sequence, we output that directly. For
   448     // ctrlSeq we have to use all mods because we want it to only
   449     // match ctrl+<char>.
   450     if (ctrlSeq(
   451         event.key,
   452         event.utf8,
   453         event.unshifted_codepoint,
   454         all_mods,
   455     )) |char| {
   456         // C0 sequences support alt-as-esc prefixing.
   457         if (binding_mods.alt) {
   458             try writer.writeByte(0x1B);
   459             try writer.writeByte(char);
   460             return;
   461         }
   462 
   463         try writer.writeByte(char);
   464         return;
   465     }
   466 
   467     // If we have no UTF8 text then the only possibility is the
   468     // alt-prefix handling of unshifted codepoints... so we process that.
   469     if (utf8.len == 0) {
   470         _ = try legacyAltPrefix(
   471             writer,
   472             event,
   473             binding_mods,
   474             all_mods,
   475             opts,
   476         );
   477         return;
   478     }
   479 
   480     // Let's see if we should apply fixterms to this codepoint.
   481     // At this stage of key processing, we only need to apply fixterms
   482     // to unicode codepoints if we have ctrl set.
   483     if (event.mods.ctrl) csiu: {
   484         // Important: we want to use the original mods here, not the
   485         // effective mods. The fixterms spec states the shifted chars
   486         // should be sent uppercase but Kitty changes that behavior
   487         // so we'll send all the mods.
   488         const csi_u_mods, const char = mods: {
   489             var mods = CsiUMods.fromInput(event.mods);
   490 
   491             // Get our codepoint. If we have more than one codepoint this
   492             // can't be valid CSIu.
   493             const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch break :csiu;
   494             var it = view.iterator();
   495             var char = it.nextCodepoint() orelse break :csiu;
   496             if (it.nextCodepoint() != null) break :csiu;
   497 
   498             // If our character is A to Z and we have shift set, then
   499             // we lowercase it. This is a Kitty-specific behavior that
   500             // we choose to follow and diverge from the fixterms spec.
   501             // This makes it easier for programs to detect shifted letters
   502             // for keybindings and is not just theoretical but used by
   503             // real programs.
   504             if (char >= 'A' and char <= 'Z' and mods.shift) {
   505                 // We want to rely on apprt to send us the correct
   506                 // unshifted codepoint...
   507                 char = @intCast(std.ascii.toLower(@intCast(char)));
   508             }
   509 
   510             // If our unshifted codepoint is identical to the shifted
   511             // then we consider shift. Otherwise, we do not because the
   512             // shift key was used to obtain the character. This is specified
   513             // by fixterms.
   514             if (event.unshifted_codepoint != char) {
   515                 mods.shift = false;
   516             }
   517 
   518             break :mods .{ mods, char };
   519         };
   520         return try writer.print(
   521             "\x1B[{};{}u",
   522             .{ char, csi_u_mods.seqInt() },
   523         );
   524     }
   525 
   526     // If we have alt-pressed and alt-esc-prefix is enabled, then
   527     // we need to prefix the utf8 sequence with an esc.
   528     if (try legacyAltPrefix(
   529         writer,
   530         event,
   531         binding_mods,
   532         all_mods,
   533         opts,
   534     )) return;
   535 
   536     // If we are on macOS, command+keys do not encode text. It isn't
   537     // typical for command+keys on macOS to ever encode text. They
   538     // don't in native text inputs (i.e. TextEdit) and they also don't
   539     // in other native terminals (Terminal.app officially but also
   540     // iTerm2).
   541     //
   542     // For Linux, we continue to encode text because it is typical.
   543     // For example on Gnome Console Super+b will encode a "b" character
   544     // with legacy encoding.
   545     if ((comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos) and all_mods.super) {
   546         return;
   547     }
   548 
   549     return try writer.writeAll(utf8);
   550 }

   552 fn legacyAltPrefix(

   557     opts: Options,

   559     // This only takes effect with alt pressed
   560     if (!binding_mods.alt or !opts.alt_esc_prefix) return false;

n27 src/input/key_encode.zig:561–618 I

   562     // On macOS, we only handle option like alt in certain
   563     // circumstances. Otherwise, macOS does a unicode translation
   564     // and we allow that to happen.
   565     if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos) {
   566         switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
   567             .false => return false,
   568             .left => if (mods.sides.alt == .right) return false,
   569             .right => if (mods.sides.alt == .left) return false,
   570             .true => {},

   583     var unshifted_buf: [4]u8 = undefined;

   586         // value. When Option is configured as Alt, use the physical key's

n27 src/input/key_encode.zig:631–699 DIU
   631 fn pcStyleFunctionKey(
   632     keyval: key.Key,
   633     mods: key.Mods,
   634     cursor_key_application: bool,
   635     keypad_key_application_req: bool,
   636     ignore_keypad_with_numlock: bool,
   637     modify_other_keys: bool, // True if state 2
   638     backarrow_key_mode: bool,
   639 ) ?[]const u8 {
   640     // We only want binding-sensitive mods because lock keys
   641     // and directional modifiers (left/right) don't matter for
   642     // pc-style function keys.
   643     const mods_int = mods.binding().int();
   644 
   645     // Keypad application keymode isn't super straightforward.
   646     // On xterm, in VT220 mode, numlock alone is enough to trigger
   647     // application mode. But in more modern modes, numlock is
   648     // ignored by default via mode 1035 (default true). If mode
   649     // 1035 is on, we always are in numerical keypad mode. If
   650     // mode 1035 is off, we are in application mode if the
   651     // proper numlock state is pressed. The numlock state is implicitly
   652     // determined based on the keycode sent (i.e. 1 with numlock
   653     // on will be kp_end).
   654     const keypad_key_application = keypad: {
   655         // If we're ignoring keypad then this is always false.
   656         // In other words, we're always in numerical keypad mode.
   657         if (ignore_keypad_with_numlock) break :keypad false;
   658 
   659         // If we're not ignoring then we enable the desired state.
   660         break :keypad keypad_key_application_req;
   661     };
   662 
   663     for (function_keys.keys.get(keyval)) |entry| {
   664         switch (entry.cursor) {
   665             .any => {},
   666             .normal => if (cursor_key_application) continue,
   667             .application => if (!cursor_key_application) continue,
   668         }
   669 
   670         switch (entry.keypad) {
   671             .any => {},
   672             .normal => if (keypad_key_application) continue,
   673             .application => if (!keypad_key_application) continue,
   674         }
   675 
   676         switch (entry.modify_other_keys) {
   677             .any => {},
   678             .set => if (modify_other_keys) continue,
   679             .set_other => if (!modify_other_keys) continue,
   680         }
   681 
   682         const entry_mods_int = entry.mods.int();
   683         if (entry_mods_int == 0) {
   684             if (mods_int != 0 and !entry.mods_empty_is_any) continue;
   685             // mods are either empty, or empty means any so we allow it.
   686         } else if (entry_mods_int != mods_int) {
   687             // any set mods require an exact match
   688             continue;
   689         }
   690 
   691         if (backarrow_key_mode)
   692             if (entry.sequence_decbkm) |sequence|
   693                 return sequence;
   694 
   695         return entry.sequence;
   696     }
   697 
   698     return null;
   699 }

n27 src/input/key_encode.zig:708–892 DIU
   708 fn ctrlSeq(
   709     logical_key: key.Key,
   710     utf8: []const u8,
   711     unshifted_codepoint: u21,
   712     mods: key.Mods,
   713 ) ?u8 {
   714     const ctrl_only = comptime (key.Mods{ .ctrl = true }).int();
   715 
   716     // If ctrl is not pressed then we never do anything.
   717     if (!mods.ctrl) return null;
   718 
   719     const char, const unset_mods = unset_mods: {
   720         // We need to only get binding modifiers so we strip lock
   721         // keys, sides, etc.
   722         var unset_mods = mods.binding();
   723 
   724         // Remove alt from our modifiers because it does not impact whether
   725         // we are generating a ctrl sequence and we handle the ESC-prefix
   726         // logic separately.
   727         unset_mods.alt = false;
   728 
   729         var char: u8 = char: {
   730             // If we have exactly one UTF8 byte, we assume that is the
   731             // character we want to convert to a C0 byte.
   732             if (utf8.len == 1) break :char utf8[0];
   733 
   734             // If we have a logical key that maps to a single byte
   735             // printable character, we use that. History to explain this:
   736             // this was added to support cyrillic keyboard layouts such
   737             // as Russian and Mongolian. These layouts have a `c` key that
   738             // maps to U+0441 (cyrillic small letter "c") but every
   739             // terminal I've tested encodes this as ctrl+c.
   740             if (logical_key.codepoint()) |cp| {
   741                 if (std.math.cast(u8, cp)) |byte| {
   742                     // For this specific case, we only map to the key if
   743                     // we have exactly ctrl pressed. This is because shift
   744                     // would modify the key and we don't know how to do that
   745                     // properly here (don't have the layout). And we want
   746                     // to encode shift as CSIu.
   747                     if (unset_mods.int() != ctrl_only) return null;
   748                     break :char byte;
   749                 }
   750             }
   751 
   752             // Otherwise we don't have a character to convert that
   753             // we can reliably map to a C0 byte.
   754             return null;
   755         };
   756 
   757         // Remove shift if we have something outside of the US letter
   758         // range. This is so that characters such as `ctrl+shift+-`
   759         // generate the correct ctrl-seq (used by emacs).
   760         if (unset_mods.shift and (char < 'A' or char > 'Z')) shift: {
   761             // Special case for fixterms awkward case as specified.
   762             if (char == '@') break :shift;
   763             unset_mods.shift = false;
   764         }
   765 
   766         // If the character is uppercase, we convert it to lowercase. We
   767         // rely on the unshifted codepoint to do this. This handles
   768         // the scenario where we have caps lock pressed. Note that
   769         // shifted characters are handled above, if we are just pressing
   770         // shift then the ctrl-only check will fail later and we won't
   771         // ctrl-seq encode.
   772         if (char >= 'A' and char <= 'Z' and unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
   773             if (std.math.cast(u8, unshifted_codepoint)) |byte| {
   774                 char = byte;
   775             }
   776         }
   777 
   778         // An additional note on caps lock and shift interaction.
   779         // If we have caps lock set and an ASCII letter is pressed,
   780         // we lowercase it (above). If we have only control pressed,
   781         // we process it as a ctrl seq. For example ctrl+M with caps
   782         // lock but no shift will encode as 0x0D.
   783         //
   784         // But, if you press ctrl+shift+m, this will not encode as a
   785         // ctrl-seq and falls through to CSIu encoding. This lets programs
   786         // detect the difference between ctrl+M and ctrl+shift+M. This
   787         // diverges from the fixterms "spec" and most terminals. This
   788         // only matches Kitty in behavior. But I believe this is a
   789         // justified divergence because it's a useful distinction.
   790 
   791         break :unset_mods .{ char, unset_mods };
   792     };
   793 
   794     // After unsetting, we only continue if we have ONLY control set.
   795     if (unset_mods.int() != ctrl_only) return null;
   796 
   797     // From Kitty's key encoding logic. I tried to discern the exact
   798     // behavior across different terminals but it's not clear, so I'm
   799     // just going to repeat what Kitty does.
   800     return switch (char) {
   801         ' ' => 0,
   802         '/' => 31,
   803         '0' => 48,
   804         '1' => 49,
   805         '2' => 0,
   806         '3' => 27,
   807         '4' => 28,
   808         '5' => 29,
   809         '6' => 30,
   810         '7' => 31,
   811         '8' => 127,
   812         '9' => 57,
   813         '?' => 127,
   814         '@' => 0,
   815         '\\' => 28,
   816         ']' => 29,
   817         '^' => 30,
   818         '_' => 31,
   819         'a' => 1,
   820         'b' => 2,
   821         'c' => 3,
   822         'd' => 4,
   823         'e' => 5,
   824         'f' => 6,
   825         'g' => 7,
   826         'h' => 8,
   827         'j' => 10,
   828         'k' => 11,
   829         'l' => 12,
   830         'n' => 14,
   831         'o' => 15,
   832         'p' => 16,
   833         'q' => 17,
   834         'r' => 18,
   835         's' => 19,
   836         't' => 20,
   837         'u' => 21,
   838         'v' => 22,
   839         'w' => 23,
   840         'x' => 24,
   841         'y' => 25,
   842         'z' => 26,
   843         '~' => 30,
   844 
   845         // These are purposely NOT handled here because of the fixterms
   846         // specification: https://www.leonerd.org.uk/hacks/fixterms/
   847         // These are processed as CSI u.
   848         // 'i' => 0x09,
   849         // 'm' => 0x0D,
   850         // '[' => 0x1B,
   851 
   852         else => null,
   853     };
   854 }
   855 
   856 /// Returns true if this is an ASCII control character, matches libc implementation.
   857 fn isControl(cp: u21) bool {
   858     return cp < 0x20 or cp == 0x7F;
   859 }
   860 
   861 /// Returns true if this string is comprised of a single
   862 /// control character. This returns false for multi-byte strings.
   863 fn isControlUtf8(str: []const u8) bool {
   864     return str.len == 1 and isControl(@intCast(str[0]));
   865 }
   866 
   867 /// This is the bitmask for fixterm CSI u modifiers.
   868 const CsiUMods = packed struct(u3) {
   869     shift: bool = false,
   870     alt: bool = false,
   871     ctrl: bool = false,
   872 
   873     /// Convert an input mods value into the CSI u mods value.
   874     pub fn fromInput(mods: key.Mods) CsiUMods {
   875         return .{
   876             .shift = mods.shift,
   877             .alt = mods.alt,
   878             .ctrl = mods.ctrl,
   879         };
   880     }
   881 
   882     /// Returns the raw int value of this packed struct.
   883     pub fn int(self: CsiUMods) u3 {
   884         return @bitCast(self);
   885     }
   886 
   887     /// Returns the integer value sent as part of the CSI u sequence.
   888     /// This adds 1 to the bitmask value as described in the spec.
   889     pub fn seqInt(self: CsiUMods) u4 {
   890         const raw: u4 = @intCast(self.int());
   891         return raw + 1;
   892     }

n27 src/input/key_encode.zig:924–975 DI
   924 const KittyMods = packed struct(u8) {
   925     shift: bool = false,
   926     alt: bool = false,
   927     ctrl: bool = false,
   928     super: bool = false,
   929     hyper: bool = false,
   930     meta: bool = false,
   931     caps_lock: bool = false,
   932     num_lock: bool = false,
   933 
   934     /// Convert an input mods value into the CSI u mods value.
   935     pub fn fromInput(
   936         action: key.Action,
   937         k: key.Key,
   938         mods: key.Mods,
   939     ) KittyMods {
   940         _ = action;
   941         _ = k;
   942         return .{
   943             .shift = mods.shift,
   944             .alt = mods.alt,
   945             .ctrl = mods.ctrl,
   946             .super = mods.super,
   947             .caps_lock = mods.caps_lock,
   948             .num_lock = mods.num_lock,
   949         };
   950     }
   951 
   952     /// Returns true if the modifiers prevent printable text.
   953     ///
   954     /// The alt_prevents_text parameter determines whether or not the Alt
   955     /// modifier prevents printable text. On Linux, this is always true. On
   956     /// macOS, this is only true if macos-option-as-alt is set.
   957     pub fn preventsText(self: KittyMods, alt_prevents_text: bool) bool {
   958         return (self.alt and alt_prevents_text) or
   959             self.ctrl or
   960             self.super or
   961             self.hyper or
   962             self.meta;
   963     }
   964 
   965     /// Returns the raw int value of this packed struct.
   966     pub fn int(self: KittyMods) u8 {
   967         return @bitCast(self);
   968     }
   969 
   970     /// Returns the integer value sent as part of the Kitty sequence.
   971     /// This adds 1 to the bitmask value as described in the spec.
   972     pub fn seqInt(self: KittyMods) u9 {
   973         const raw: u9 = @intCast(self.int());
   974         return raw + 1;
   975     }

n27 src/input/key_encode.zig:1010–1117 DIU
  1010 const KittySequence = struct {
  1011     key: u21,
  1012     final: u8,
  1013     mods: KittyMods = .{},
  1014     event: Event = .none,
  1015     alternates: [2]?u21 = .{ null, null },
  1016     text: []const u8 = "",
  1017 
  1018     /// Values for the event code (see "event-type" in above comment).
  1019     /// Note that Kitty omits the ":1" for the press event but other
  1020     /// terminals include it. We'll include it.
  1021     const Event = enum(u2) {
  1022         none = 0,
  1023         press = 1,
  1024         repeat = 2,
  1025         release = 3,
  1026     };
  1027 
  1028     pub fn encode(
  1029         self: KittySequence,
  1030         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
  1031     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
  1032         if (self.final == 'u' or self.final == '~') return try self.encodeFull(writer);
  1033         return try self.encodeSpecial(writer);
  1034     }
  1035 
  1036     fn encodeFull(
  1037         self: KittySequence,
  1038         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
  1039     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
  1040         // Key section
  1041         try writer.print("\x1B[{d}", .{self.key});
  1042         // Write our alternates
  1043         if (self.alternates[0]) |shifted| try writer.print(":{d}", .{shifted});
  1044         if (self.alternates[1]) |base| {
  1045             if (self.alternates[0] == null) {
  1046                 try writer.print("::{d}", .{base});
  1047             } else {
  1048                 try writer.print(":{d}", .{base});
  1049             }
  1050         }
  1051 
  1052         // Mods and events section
  1053         const mods = self.mods.seqInt();
  1054         var emit_prior = false;
  1055         if (self.event != .none and self.event != .press) {
  1056             try writer.print(
  1057                 ";{d}:{d}",
  1058                 .{ mods, @backingInt(self.event) },
  1059             );
  1060             emit_prior = true;
  1061         } else if (mods > 1) {
  1062             try writer.print(";{d}", .{mods});
  1063             emit_prior = true;
  1064         }
  1065 
  1066         // Text section
  1067         if (self.text.len > 0) text: {
  1068             const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(self.text) catch {
  1069                 // Assume invalid UTF-8 means we have no text.
  1070                 break :text;
  1071             };
  1072             var it = view.iterator();
  1073             var count: usize = 0;
  1074             while (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp| {
  1075                 // If the codepoint is non-printable ASCII character, skip.
  1076                 if (isControl(cp)) continue;
  1077 
  1078                 // We need to add our ";". We need to add two if we didn't emit
  1079                 // the modifier section. We only do this initially.
  1080                 if (count == 0) {
  1081                     if (!emit_prior) try writer.writeByte(';');
  1082                     try writer.writeByte(';');
  1083                 } else {
  1084                     try writer.writeByte(':');
  1085                 }
  1086 
  1087                 try writer.print("{d}", .{cp});
  1088                 count += 1;
  1089             }
  1090         }
  1091 
  1092         try writer.print("{c}", .{self.final});
  1093     }
  1094 
  1095     fn encodeSpecial(
  1096         self: KittySequence,
  1097         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
  1098     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
  1099         const mods = self.mods.seqInt();
  1100         if (self.event != .none) {
  1101             return try writer.print("\x1B[1;{d}:{d}{c}", .{
  1102                 mods,
  1103                 @backingInt(self.event),
  1104                 self.final,
  1105             });
  1106         }
  1107 
  1108         if (mods > 1) {
  1109             return try writer.print("\x1B[1;{d}{c}", .{
  1110                 mods,
  1111                 self.final,
  1112             });
  1113         }
  1114 
  1115         return try writer.print("\x1B[{c}", .{self.final});
  1116     }
  1117 };

    14 pub const entries: []const Entry = entries: {
    15     var result: [raw_entries.len]Entry = undefined;

    18             .key = raw[0],
    19             .code = raw[1],
    20             .final = raw[2],
    21             .modifier = raw[3],
    22         };
    23     }
    24

    26     break :entries &final;
    27 };

n27 src/terminal/kitty/key.zig:5–62 DI
     5 /// Stack for the key flags. This implements the push/pop behavior
     6 /// of the CSI > u and CSI < u sequences. We implement the stack as
     7 /// fixed size to avoid heap allocation.
     8 pub const FlagStack = struct {
     9     const len = 8;
    10 
    11     flags: [len]Flags = @splat(.disabled),
    12     idx: u3 = 0,
    13 
    14     /// Return the current stack value
    15     pub fn current(self: FlagStack) Flags {
    16         return self.flags[self.idx];
    17     }
    18 
    19     /// Perform the "set" operation as described in the spec for
    20     /// the CSI = u sequence.
    21     pub fn set(
    22         self: *FlagStack,
    23         mode: SetMode,
    24         v: Flags,
    25     ) void {
    26         switch (mode) {
    27             .set => self.flags[self.idx] = v,
    28             .@"or" => self.flags[self.idx] = @bitCast(
    29                 self.flags[self.idx].int() | v.int(),
    30             ),
    31             .not => self.flags[self.idx] = @bitCast(
    32                 self.flags[self.idx].int() & ~v.int(),
    33             ),
    34         }
    35     }
    36 
    37     /// Push a new set of flags onto the stack. If the stack is full
    38     /// then the oldest entry is evicted.
    39     pub fn push(self: *FlagStack, flags: Flags) void {
    40         // Overflow and wrap around if we're full, which evicts
    41         // the oldest entry.
    42         self.idx +%= 1;
    43         self.flags[self.idx] = flags;
    44     }
    45 
    46     /// Pop `n` entries from the stack. This will just wrap around
    47     /// if `n` is greater than the amount in the stack.
    48     pub fn pop(self: *FlagStack, n: usize) void {
    49         // If n is more than our length then we just reset the stack.
    50         // This also avoids a DoS vector where a malicious client
    51         // could send a huge number of pop commands to waste cpu.
    52         if (n >= self.flags.len) {
    53             self.idx = 0;
    54             self.flags = @splat(.disabled);
    55             return;
    56         }
    57 
    58         for (0..n) |_| {
    59             self.flags[self.idx] = .disabled;
    60             self.idx -%= 1;
    61         }
    62     }

n27 src/terminal/modes.zig:139–155 DI
   139 fn setPacked(values: *ModePacked, mode: Mode, value: bool) void {
   140     switch (mode) {
   141         inline else => |mode_comptime| {
   142             const entry = comptime entryForMode(mode_comptime);
   143             @field(values, entry.name) = value;
   144         },
   145     }
   146 }
   147 
   148 fn getPacked(values: *const ModePacked, mode: Mode) bool {
   149     switch (mode) {
   150         inline else => |mode_comptime| {
   151             const entry = comptime entryForMode(mode_comptime);
   152             return @field(values, entry.name);
   153         },
   154     }
   155 }

n27 src/terminal/modes.zig:216–231 DI
   216 pub fn modeFromInt(v: u16, ansi: bool) ?Mode {
   217     // ModeTag stores the number in 15 bits. A larger number can't be a
   218     // known mode, and packing it below would spill into the ansi bit.
   219     if (v > std.math.maxInt(u15)) return null;
   220 
   221     // This runs for every mode that is set, reset, or queried, so we use
   222     // a sorted lookup instead of comparing against each entry in turn.
   223     const tag: ModeTag = .{ .ansi = ansi, .value = @intCast(v) };
   224     const int: ModeTag.Backing = @bitCast(tag);
   225     const idx = EntrySet.indexOf(int) orelse return null;
   226     if (comptime entries_any_disabled) {
   227         if (entries_disabled[idx]) return null;
   228     }
   229 
   230     return @fromBackingInt(int);
   231 }

n27 src/terminal/modes.zig:388–393 D
   388     .{ .name = "keypad_keys", .value = 66 },
   389     // DEC Backarrow Key Mode (DECBKM)
   390     // See https://vt100.net/dec/ek-vt3xx-tp-002.pdf page 170
   391     // If `false` (the default), `backspace` emits 0x7f
   392     // If `true`, `backspace` emits 0x08

  2752                 'u' => switch (input.intermediates.len) {

  2792                                 1;

  2797                                 3 => .kitty_keyboard_set_not,

  2807                                 .kitty_keyboard_set_or => self.handler.vt(.kitty_keyboard_set_or, kitty_flags),
  2808                                 .kitty_keyboard_set_not => self.handler.vt(.kitty_keyboard_set_not, kitty_flags),
  2809                                 else => unreachable,
  2810                             }
  2811                         },

n28 src/input/function_keys.zig:12–49 D
    12 pub const CursorMode = enum { any, normal, application };
    13 pub const KeypadMode = enum { any, normal, application };
    14 
    15 /// A bit confusing so I'll document this one: this is the "modify other keys"
    16 /// setting. We only change behavior for "set_other" which is ESC [ > 4; 2 m.
    17 /// So this can be "any" which means we don't care what's going on. Or it
    18 /// can be "set" which means modify keys must be set EXCEPT FOR "other keys"
    19 /// mode, and "set_other" which means modify keys must be set to "other keys"
    20 /// mode.
    21 ///
    22 /// See: https://invisible-island.net/xterm/modified-keys.html
    23 pub const ModifyKeys = enum {
    24     any,
    25     set,
    26     set_other,
    27 };
    28 
    29 /// A single entry in the table of keys.
    30 pub const Entry = struct {
    31     /// The exact set of modifiers that must be active for this entry to match.
    32     /// If mods_empty_is_any is true then empty mods means any set of mods can
    33     /// match. Otherwise, empty mods means no mods must be active.
    34     mods: key.Mods = .{},
    35     mods_empty_is_any: bool = true,
    36 
    37     /// The state required for cursor/keypad mode.
    38     cursor: CursorMode = .any,
    39     keypad: KeypadMode = .any,
    40 
    41     /// Whether or not this entry should be used
    42     modify_other_keys: ModifyKeys = .any,
    43 
    44     /// The sequence to send to the pty if this entry matches.
    45     sequence: []const u8,
    46 
    47     /// Sequence to send to the PTY if DECBKM is set.
    48     sequence_decbkm: ?[]const u8 = null,
    49 };

n28 src/input/function_keys.zig:264–314 DIU
   264 /// Returns the default keypad application mode entry.
   265 fn kpDefault(comptime suffix: []const u8) []const Entry {
   266     return &.{
   267         .{
   268             .mods_empty_is_any = false,
   269             .keypad = .application,
   270             .sequence = "\x1bO" ++ suffix,
   271         },
   272     };
   273 }
   274 
   275 /// Returns the entries for a keypad key. The suffix is the final character
   276 /// of the sent sequence, such as "r" for kp_2.
   277 fn kpKeys(comptime suffix: []const u8, comptime normal: []const u8) []const Entry {
   278     const pc = pcStyle("\x1bO{}" ++ suffix);
   279     for (pc) |*entry| entry.keypad = .application;
   280     return kpDefault(suffix) ++ pc ++ .{
   281         Entry{ .keypad = .normal, .sequence = normal },
   282     };
   283 }
   284 
   285 /// Returns entries that are dependent on cursor key settings.
   286 fn cursorKey(
   287     comptime normal: []const u8,
   288     comptime application: []const u8,
   289 ) []const Entry {
   290     return &.{
   291         .{ .cursor = .normal, .sequence = normal },
   292         .{ .cursor = .application, .sequence = application },
   293     };
   294 }
   295 
   296 /// Constructs a set of pcStyle function keys using the given format. The
   297 /// format should have exactly one "hole" for the mods code.
   298 /// Example: "\x1b[11;{}~" for F1.
   299 fn pcStyle(comptime fmt: []const u8) []Entry {
   300     // The comptime {} wrapper is superfluous but it prevents us from
   301     // accidentally running this function at runtime.
   302     comptime {
   303         @setEvalBranchQuota(500_000);
   304         var entries: [modifiers.len]Entry = undefined;
   305         for (modifiers, 2.., 0..) |mods, code, i| {
   306             entries[i] = .{
   307                 .mods = mods,
   308                 .sequence = std.fmt.comptimePrint(fmt, .{code}),
   309             };
   310         }
   311 
   312         return &entries;
   313     }
   314 }

n28 src/input/key_encode.zig:14–38 D
    14 pub const Options = struct {
    15     /// Terminal DEC mode 1
    16     cursor_key_application: bool = false,
    17 
    18     /// Terminal DEC mode 66
    19     keypad_key_application: bool = false,
    20 
    21     // DEC Backarrow Key Mode (DECBKM)
    22     // See https://vt100.net/dec/ek-vt3xx-tp-002.pdf page 170
    23     // If `false` (the default), `backspace` emits 0x7f
    24     // If `true`, `backspace` emits 0x08
    25     backarrow_key_mode: bool = false,
    26 
    27     /// Terminal DEC mode 1035
    28     ignore_keypad_with_numlock: bool = false,
    29 
    30     /// Terminal DEC mode 1036

    34     /// https://invisible-island.net/xterm/modified-keys.html
    35     modify_other_keys_state_2: bool = false,
    36

    38     kitty_flags: KittyFlags = .disabled,

    55     /// Initialize our options from the terminal state.
    56     ///
    57     /// Note that `macos_option_as_alt` cannot be determined from
    58     /// terminal state so it must be set manually after this call.
    59     pub fn fromTerminal(t: *const Terminal) Options {
    60         return .{
    61             .alt_esc_prefix = t.modes.get(.alt_esc_prefix),
    62             .cursor_key_application = t.modes.get(.cursor_keys),
    63             .keypad_key_application = t.modes.get(.keypad_keys),
    64             .backarrow_key_mode = t.modes.get(.backarrow_key_mode),
    65             .ignore_keypad_with_numlock = t.modes.get(.ignore_keypad_with_numlock),
    66             .modify_other_keys_state_2 = t.flags.modify_other_keys_2,
    67             .kitty_flags = t.screens.active.kitty_keyboard.current(),
    68 
    69             // These can't be known from the terminal state.
    70             .macos_option_as_alt = .false,
    71         };
    72     }
