    75 /// Encode the key event to the writer in the proper format given
    76 /// the options. For example, this will properly encode a key press
    77 /// such as "ctrl+A" to Kitty format if Kitty encoding is enabled.
    78 ///
    79 /// Not all key events will result in output. It is up to the caller
    80 /// to use a writer that can track whether any output was written if
    81 /// they care about that.
    82 pub fn encode(
    83     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
    84     event: key.KeyEvent,
    85     opts: Options,
    86 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
    87     //std.log.warn("KEYENCODER event={} opts={}", .{ event, opts });
    88     return if (opts.kitty_flags.int() != 0) try kitty(
    89         writer,
    90         event,
    91         opts,
    92     ) else try legacy(
    93         writer,
    94         event,
    95         opts,
    96     );
    97 }

n28 src/input/key_encode.zig:99–319 DIU
    99 /// Perform Kitty keyboard protocol encoding of the key event.
   100 fn kitty(
   101     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   102     event: key.KeyEvent,
   103     opts: Options,
   104 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
   105     // This should never happen but we'll check anyway.
   106     if (opts.kitty_flags.int() == 0) return try legacy(
   107         writer,
   108         event,
   109         opts,
   110     );
   111 
   112     // We only processed "press" events unless report events is active
   113     if (event.action == .release) {
   114         if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_events) return;
   115 
   116         // Enter, backspace, and tab do not report release events unless "report
   117         // all" is set
   118         if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_all) {
   119             switch (event.key) {
   120                 .enter, .backspace, .tab => return,
   121                 else => {},
   122             }
   123         }
   124     }
   125 
   126     const all_mods = event.mods;
   127     const effective_mods = event.effectiveMods();
   128     const binding_mods = effective_mods.binding();
   129 
   130     // Find the entry for this key in the kitty table.
   131     const entry_: ?KittyEntry = entry: {
   132         // Functional or predefined keys
   133         for (kitty_entries) |entry| {
   134             if (entry.key == event.key) break :entry entry;
   135         }
   136 
   137         // Otherwise, we use our unicode codepoint from UTF8. We
   138         // always use the unshifted value.
   139         if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
   140             break :entry .{
   141                 .key = event.key,
   142                 .code = event.unshifted_codepoint,
   143                 .final = 'u',
   144                 .modifier = false,
   145             };
   146         }
   147 
   148         break :entry null;
   149     };
   150 
   151     preprocessing: {
   152         // When composing, the only keys sent are plain modifiers.
   153         if (event.composing) {
   154             if (entry_) |entry| {
   155                 if (entry.modifier) break :preprocessing;
   156             }
   157 
   158             return;
   159         }
   160 
   161         // IME confirmation still sends an enter key so if we have enter
   162         // and UTF8 text we just send it directly since we assume that is
   163         // what's happening. See legacy()'s similar logic for more details
   164         // on how to verify this.
   165         if (event.utf8.len > 0) utf8: {
   166             switch (event.key) {
   167                 else => {},
   168                 inline .enter, .backspace => |tag| {
   169                     // See legacy for why we handle this way.
   170                     if (isControlUtf8(event.utf8)) break :utf8;
   171                     if (comptime tag == .backspace) return;
   172                     return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
   173                 },
   174             }
   175         }
   176 
   177         // If we're reporting all then we always send CSI sequences.
   178         if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_all) {
   179             // Quote:
   180             // The only exceptions are the Enter, Tab and Backspace keys which
   181             // still generate the same bytes as in legacy mode this is to allow the
   182             // user to type and execute commands in the shell such as reset after a
   183             // program that sets this mode crashes without clearing it.
   184             //
   185             // Quote ("report all" mode):
   186             // Note that all keys are reported as escape codes, including Enter,
   187             // Tab, Backspace etc.
   188             if (binding_mods.empty()) {
   189                 switch (event.key) {
   190                     .enter => return try writer.writeByte('\r'),
   191                     .tab => return try writer.writeByte('\t'),
   192                     .backspace => return try writer.writeByte(0x7F),
   193                     else => {},
   194                 }
   195             }
   196 
   197             // Send plain-text non-modified text directly to the terminal.
   198             // We don't send release events because those are specially encoded.
   199             if (event.utf8.len > 0 and
   200                 binding_mods.empty() and
   201                 event.action != .release)
   202             plain_text: {
   203                 // We only do this for printable characters. We should
   204                 // inspect the real unicode codepoint properties here but
   205                 // the real world issue is usually control characters.
   206                 const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch {
   207                     // Invalid UTF-8 so let's fallback to encoding the
   208                     // key press as if it didn't produce UTF-8 text. I'm
   209                     // not sure what should happen here according to the spec,
   210                     // since it doesn't specify this behavior. Presumably
   211                     // this is a caller bug.
   212                     break :plain_text;
   213                 };
   214                 var it = view.iterator();
   215                 while (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp| {
   216                     if (isControl(cp)) break :plain_text;
   217                 }
   218 
   219                 return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
   220             }
   221         }
   222     }
   223 
   224     const entry = entry_ orelse {
   225         // No entry found. If we have UTF-8 text this is a pure text event
   226         // (e.g. composed/IME text), so send it as-is so programs can
   227         // still receive it. Release events never insert text, same as the
   228         // plain-text path above.
   229         if (event.action == .release) return;
   230         if (event.utf8.len == 0) return;
   231         return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
   232     };
   233 
   234     // If this is just a modifier we require "report all" to send the sequence.
   235     if (entry.modifier and !opts.kitty_flags.report_all) return;
   236 
   237     const seq: KittySequence = seq: {
   238         var seq: KittySequence = .{
   239             .key = entry.code,
   240             .final = entry.final,
   241             .mods = .fromInput(
   242                 event.action,
   243                 event.key,
   244                 all_mods,
   245             ),
   246         };
   247 
   248         if (opts.kitty_flags.report_events) {
   249             seq.event = switch (event.action) {
   250                 .press => .press,
   251                 .release => .release,
   252                 .repeat => .repeat,
   253             };
   254         }
   255 
   256         if (opts.kitty_flags.report_alternates) alternates: {
   257             // Break early if this is a control key
   258             if (isControl(seq.key)) break :alternates;
   259 
   260             const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch {
   261                 // Assume invalid UTF-8 means no UTF-8.
   262                 break :alternates;
   263             };
   264             var it = view.iterator();
   265 
   266             // If we have a codepoint in our UTF-8 sequence, then we can
   267             // report the shifted version.
   268             if (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp1| {
   269                 // Set the first alternate (shifted version)
   270                 if (cp1 != seq.key and seq.mods.shift) seq.alternates[0] = cp1;
   271 
   272                 // We want to know if there are additional codepoints because
   273                 // our logic below depends on the utf8 being a single codepoint.
   274                 const has_cp2 = it.nextCodepoint() != null;
   275 
   276                 // Set the base layout key. We only report this if this codepoint
   277                 // differs from our pressed key.
   278                 if (event.key.codepoint()) |base| {
   279                     if (base != seq.key and
   280                         (cp1 != base and !has_cp2))
   281                     {
   282                         seq.alternates[1] = base;
   283                     }
   284                 }
   285             } else {
   286                 // No UTF-8 so we can't report a shifted key but we can still
   287                 // report a base layout key.
   288                 if (event.key.codepoint()) |base| {
   289                     if (base != seq.key) seq.alternates[1] = base;
   290                 }
   291             }
   292         }
   293 
   294         if (opts.kitty_flags.report_associated and
   295             seq.event != .release)
   296         associated: {
   297             // Determine if the Alt modifier should be treated as an actual
   298             // modifier (in which case it prevents associated text) or as
   299             // the macOS Option key, which does not prevent associated text.
   300             const alt_prevents_text = if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos)
   301                 switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
   302                     .left => all_mods.sides.alt == .left,
   303                     .right => all_mods.sides.alt == .right,
   304                     .true => true,
   305                     .false => false,
   306                 }
   307             else
   308                 true;
   309 
   310             if (seq.mods.preventsText(alt_prevents_text)) break :associated;
   311 
   312             seq.text = event.utf8;
   313         }
   314 
   315         break :seq seq;
   316     };
   317 
   318     return try seq.encode(writer);
   319 }

n28 src/input/key_encode.zig:327–351 DIU
   327 fn legacy(
   328     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   329     event: key.KeyEvent,
   330     opts: Options,
   331 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
   332     const all_mods = event.mods;
   333     const effective_mods = event.effectiveMods();
   334     const binding_mods = effective_mods.binding();
   335 
   336     // Legacy encoding only does press/repeat
   337     if (event.action != .press and event.action != .repeat) return;
   338 
   339     // If we're in a dead key state then we never emit a sequence.
   340     if (event.composing) return;
   341 
   342     // If we match a PC style function key then that is our result.
   343     if (pcStyleFunctionKey(
   344         event.key,
   345         all_mods,
   346         opts.cursor_key_application,
   347         opts.keypad_key_application,
   348         opts.ignore_keypad_with_numlock,
   349         opts.modify_other_keys_state_2,
   350         opts.backarrow_key_mode,
   351     )) |sequence| pc_style: {

n28 src/input/key_encode.zig:379–445 IU
   379         return try writer.writeAll(sequence);
   380     }
   381 
   382     // In modify other keys state 2, we send the CSI 27 sequence
   383     // for any char with a modifier. This must happen before converting
   384     // Ctrl+<char> to a C0 byte because mode 2 encodes those keys, too.
   385     const utf8 = event.utf8;
   386     if (opts.modify_other_keys_state_2) modify_other: {
   387         const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(utf8) catch {
   388             // Assume invalid UTF-8 means we no UTF-8.
   389             break :modify_other;
   390         };
   391         var it = view.iterator();
   392         const codepoint = it.nextCodepoint() orelse break :modify_other;
   393 
   394         // We only do this if we have a single codepoint. There shouldn't
   395         // ever be a multi-codepoint sequence that triggers this.
   396         if (it.nextCodepoint() != null) break :modify_other;
   397 
   398         // The mods we encode for this are just the binding mods (shift, ctrl,
   399         // super, alt unless it is actually option).
   400         const mods = mods: {
   401             var mods_binding = event.mods.binding();
   402             if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) alt: {
   403                 switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
   404                     .false => {},
   405                     .true => break :alt,
   406                     .left => if (event.mods.sides.alt == .left) break :alt,
   407                     .right => if (event.mods.sides.alt == .right) break :alt,
   408                 }
   409                 mods_binding.alt = false;
   410             }
   411             break :mods mods_binding;
   412         };
   413 
   414         // This copies xterm's `ModifyOtherKeys` function that returns
   415         // whether modify other keys should be encoded for the given
   416         // input.
   417         const should_modify = should_modify: {
   418             // xterm IsControlInput
   419             if (codepoint >= 0x40 and codepoint <= 0x7F)
   420                 break :should_modify true;
   421 
   422             // If we have anything other than shift pressed, encode.
   423             var mods_no_shift = mods;
   424             mods_no_shift.shift = false;
   425             if (!mods_no_shift.empty()) break :should_modify true;
   426 
   427             // We only have shift pressed. We only allow space.
   428             if (codepoint == ' ') break :should_modify true;
   429 
   430             // This logic isn't complete but I don't fully understand
   431             // the rest so I'm going to wait until we can have a
   432             // reasonable test scenario.
   433             break :should_modify false;
   434         };
   435 
   436         if (should_modify) {
   437             for (function_keys.modifiers, 2..) |modset, code| {
   438                 if (!mods.equal(modset)) continue;
   439                 return try writer.print(
   440                     "\x1B[27;{};{}~",
   441                     .{ code, codepoint },
   442                 );
   443             }
   444         }
   445     }

   560     if (!binding_mods.alt or !opts.alt_esc_prefix) return false;

n28 src/input/key_encode.zig:631–699 DIU
   631 fn pcStyleFunctionKey(
   632     keyval: key.Key,
   633     mods: key.Mods,
   634     cursor_key_application: bool,
   635     keypad_key_application_req: bool,
   636     ignore_keypad_with_numlock: bool,
   637     modify_other_keys: bool, // True if state 2
   638     backarrow_key_mode: bool,
   639 ) ?[]const u8 {
   640     // We only want binding-sensitive mods because lock keys
   641     // and directional modifiers (left/right) don't matter for
   642     // pc-style function keys.
   643     const mods_int = mods.binding().int();
   644 
   645     // Keypad application keymode isn't super straightforward.
   646     // On xterm, in VT220 mode, numlock alone is enough to trigger
   647     // application mode. But in more modern modes, numlock is
   648     // ignored by default via mode 1035 (default true). If mode
   649     // 1035 is on, we always are in numerical keypad mode. If
   650     // mode 1035 is off, we are in application mode if the
   651     // proper numlock state is pressed. The numlock state is implicitly
   652     // determined based on the keycode sent (i.e. 1 with numlock
   653     // on will be kp_end).
   654     const keypad_key_application = keypad: {
   655         // If we're ignoring keypad then this is always false.
   656         // In other words, we're always in numerical keypad mode.
   657         if (ignore_keypad_with_numlock) break :keypad false;
   658 
   659         // If we're not ignoring then we enable the desired state.
   660         break :keypad keypad_key_application_req;
   661     };
   662 
   663     for (function_keys.keys.get(keyval)) |entry| {
   664         switch (entry.cursor) {
   665             .any => {},
   666             .normal => if (cursor_key_application) continue,
   667             .application => if (!cursor_key_application) continue,
   668         }
   669 
   670         switch (entry.keypad) {
   671             .any => {},
   672             .normal => if (keypad_key_application) continue,
   673             .application => if (!keypad_key_application) continue,
   674         }
   675 
   676         switch (entry.modify_other_keys) {
   677             .any => {},
   678             .set => if (modify_other_keys) continue,
   679             .set_other => if (!modify_other_keys) continue,
   680         }
   681 
   682         const entry_mods_int = entry.mods.int();
   683         if (entry_mods_int == 0) {
   684             if (mods_int != 0 and !entry.mods_empty_is_any) continue;
   685             // mods are either empty, or empty means any so we allow it.
   686         } else if (entry_mods_int != mods_int) {
   687             // any set mods require an exact match
   688             continue;
   689         }
   690 
   691         if (backarrow_key_mode)
   692             if (entry.sequence_decbkm) |sequence|
   693                 return sequence;
   694 
   695         return entry.sequence;
   696     }
   697 
   698     return null;
   699 }

n28 src/input/key_encode.zig:924–975 DI
   924 const KittyMods = packed struct(u8) {
   925     shift: bool = false,
   926     alt: bool = false,
   927     ctrl: bool = false,
   928     super: bool = false,
   929     hyper: bool = false,
   930     meta: bool = false,
   931     caps_lock: bool = false,
   932     num_lock: bool = false,
   933 
   934     /// Convert an input mods value into the CSI u mods value.
   935     pub fn fromInput(
   936         action: key.Action,
   937         k: key.Key,
   938         mods: key.Mods,
   939     ) KittyMods {
   940         _ = action;
   941         _ = k;
   942         return .{
   943             .shift = mods.shift,
   944             .alt = mods.alt,
   945             .ctrl = mods.ctrl,
   946             .super = mods.super,
   947             .caps_lock = mods.caps_lock,
   948             .num_lock = mods.num_lock,
   949         };
   950     }
   951 
   952     /// Returns true if the modifiers prevent printable text.
   953     ///
   954     /// The alt_prevents_text parameter determines whether or not the Alt
   955     /// modifier prevents printable text. On Linux, this is always true. On
   956     /// macOS, this is only true if macos-option-as-alt is set.
   957     pub fn preventsText(self: KittyMods, alt_prevents_text: bool) bool {
   958         return (self.alt and alt_prevents_text) or
   959             self.ctrl or
   960             self.super or
   961             self.hyper or
   962             self.meta;
   963     }
   964 
   965     /// Returns the raw int value of this packed struct.
   966     pub fn int(self: KittyMods) u8 {
   967         return @bitCast(self);
   968     }
   969 
   970     /// Returns the integer value sent as part of the Kitty sequence.
   971     /// This adds 1 to the bitmask value as described in the spec.
   972     pub fn seqInt(self: KittyMods) u9 {
   973         const raw: u9 = @intCast(self.int());
   974         return raw + 1;
   975     }

n28 src/input/key_encode.zig:1010–1117 DIU
  1010 const KittySequence = struct {
  1011     key: u21,
  1012     final: u8,
  1013     mods: KittyMods = .{},
  1014     event: Event = .none,
  1015     alternates: [2]?u21 = .{ null, null },
  1016     text: []const u8 = "",
  1017 
  1018     /// Values for the event code (see "event-type" in above comment).
  1019     /// Note that Kitty omits the ":1" for the press event but other
  1020     /// terminals include it. We'll include it.
  1021     const Event = enum(u2) {
  1022         none = 0,
  1023         press = 1,
  1024         repeat = 2,
  1025         release = 3,
  1026     };
  1027 
  1028     pub fn encode(
  1029         self: KittySequence,
  1030         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
  1031     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
  1032         if (self.final == 'u' or self.final == '~') return try self.encodeFull(writer);
  1033         return try self.encodeSpecial(writer);
  1034     }
  1035 
  1036     fn encodeFull(
  1037         self: KittySequence,
  1038         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
  1039     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
  1040         // Key section
  1041         try writer.print("\x1B[{d}", .{self.key});
  1042         // Write our alternates
  1043         if (self.alternates[0]) |shifted| try writer.print(":{d}", .{shifted});
  1044         if (self.alternates[1]) |base| {
  1045             if (self.alternates[0] == null) {
  1046                 try writer.print("::{d}", .{base});
  1047             } else {
  1048                 try writer.print(":{d}", .{base});
  1049             }
  1050         }
  1051 
  1052         // Mods and events section
  1053         const mods = self.mods.seqInt();
  1054         var emit_prior = false;
  1055         if (self.event != .none and self.event != .press) {
  1056             try writer.print(
  1057                 ";{d}:{d}",
  1058                 .{ mods, @backingInt(self.event) },
  1059             );
  1060             emit_prior = true;
  1061         } else if (mods > 1) {
  1062             try writer.print(";{d}", .{mods});
  1063             emit_prior = true;
  1064         }
  1065 
  1066         // Text section
  1067         if (self.text.len > 0) text: {
  1068             const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(self.text) catch {
  1069                 // Assume invalid UTF-8 means we have no text.
  1070                 break :text;
  1071             };
  1072             var it = view.iterator();
  1073             var count: usize = 0;
  1074             while (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp| {
  1075                 // If the codepoint is non-printable ASCII character, skip.
  1076                 if (isControl(cp)) continue;
  1077 
  1078                 // We need to add our ";". We need to add two if we didn't emit
  1079                 // the modifier section. We only do this initially.
  1080                 if (count == 0) {
  1081                     if (!emit_prior) try writer.writeByte(';');
  1082                     try writer.writeByte(';');
  1083                 } else {
  1084                     try writer.writeByte(':');
  1085                 }
  1086 
  1087                 try writer.print("{d}", .{cp});
  1088                 count += 1;
  1089             }
  1090         }
  1091 
  1092         try writer.print("{c}", .{self.final});
  1093     }
  1094 
  1095     fn encodeSpecial(
  1096         self: KittySequence,
  1097         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
  1098     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
  1099         const mods = self.mods.seqInt();
  1100         if (self.event != .none) {
  1101             return try writer.print("\x1B[1;{d}:{d}{c}", .{
  1102                 mods,
  1103                 @backingInt(self.event),
  1104                 self.final,
  1105             });
  1106         }
  1107 
  1108         if (mods > 1) {
  1109             return try writer.print("\x1B[1;{d}{c}", .{
  1110                 mods,
  1111                 self.final,
  1112             });
  1113         }
  1114 
  1115         return try writer.print("\x1B[{c}", .{self.final});
  1116     }
  1117 };

n28 src/input/kitty.zig:6–129 DI
     6 pub const Entry = struct {
     7     key: key.Key,
     8     code: u21,
     9     final: u8,
    10     modifier: bool,
    11 };
    12 
    13 /// The full list of entries for the current platform.
    14 pub const entries: []const Entry = entries: {
    15     var result: [raw_entries.len]Entry = undefined;
    16     for (raw_entries, 0..) |raw, i| {
    17         result[i] = .{
    18             .key = raw[0],
    19             .code = raw[1],
    20             .final = raw[2],
    21             .modifier = raw[3],
    22         };
    23     }
    24 
    25     const final = result;
    26     break :entries &final;
    27 };
    28 
    29 /// Raw entry is the tuple form of an entry for easy human management.
    30 /// This should never be used in a real program so it is not pub. For
    31 /// real programs, use `entries` which has properly typed, structured data.
    32 const RawEntry = struct { key.Key, u21, u8, bool };

n28 src/terminal/kitty/key.zig:5–62 DI
     5 /// Stack for the key flags. This implements the push/pop behavior
     6 /// of the CSI > u and CSI < u sequences. We implement the stack as
     7 /// fixed size to avoid heap allocation.
     8 pub const FlagStack = struct {
     9     const len = 8;
    10 
    11     flags: [len]Flags = @splat(.disabled),
    12     idx: u3 = 0,
    13 
    14     /// Return the current stack value
    15     pub fn current(self: FlagStack) Flags {
    16         return self.flags[self.idx];
    17     }
    18 
    19     /// Perform the "set" operation as described in the spec for
    20     /// the CSI = u sequence.
    21     pub fn set(
    22         self: *FlagStack,
    23         mode: SetMode,
    24         v: Flags,
    25     ) void {
    26         switch (mode) {
    27             .set => self.flags[self.idx] = v,
    28             .@"or" => self.flags[self.idx] = @bitCast(
    29                 self.flags[self.idx].int() | v.int(),
    30             ),
    31             .not => self.flags[self.idx] = @bitCast(
    32                 self.flags[self.idx].int() & ~v.int(),
    33             ),
    34         }
    35     }
    36 
    37     /// Push a new set of flags onto the stack. If the stack is full
    38     /// then the oldest entry is evicted.
    39     pub fn push(self: *FlagStack, flags: Flags) void {
    40         // Overflow and wrap around if we're full, which evicts
    41         // the oldest entry.
    42         self.idx +%= 1;
    43         self.flags[self.idx] = flags;
    44     }
    45 
    46     /// Pop `n` entries from the stack. This will just wrap around
    47     /// if `n` is greater than the amount in the stack.
    48     pub fn pop(self: *FlagStack, n: usize) void {
    49         // If n is more than our length then we just reset the stack.
    50         // This also avoids a DoS vector where a malicious client
    51         // could send a huge number of pop commands to waste cpu.
    52         if (n >= self.flags.len) {
    53             self.idx = 0;
    54             self.flags = @splat(.disabled);
    55             return;
    56         }
    57 
    58         for (0..n) |_| {
    59             self.flags[self.idx] = .disabled;
    60             self.idx -%= 1;
    61         }
    62     }

    78 /// The possible flags for the Kitty keyboard protocol.
    79 pub const Flags = packed struct(u5) {
    80     disambiguate: bool = false,
    81     report_events: bool = false,
    82     report_alternates: bool = false,
    83     report_all: bool = false,
    84     report_associated: bool = false,
    85 
    86     /// Kitty keyboard protocol disabled (all flags off).
    87     pub const disabled: Flags = .{
    88         .disambiguate = false,
    89         .report_events = false,
    90         .report_alternates = false,
    91         .report_all = false,
    92         .report_associated = false,
    93     };
    94 
    95     /// Sets all modes on.
    96     pub const @"true": Flags = .{
    97         .disambiguate = true,
    98         .report_events = true,
    99         .report_alternates = true,
   100         .report_all = true,
   101         .report_associated = true,
   102     };
   103 
   104     pub fn int(self: Flags) u5 {
   105         return @bitCast(self);
   106     }

   139 fn setPacked(values: *ModePacked, mode: Mode, value: bool) void {
   140     switch (mode) {
   141         inline else => |mode_comptime| {
   142             const entry = comptime entryForMode(mode_comptime);
   143             @field(values, entry.name) = value;
   144         },
   145     }
   146 }
   147 
   148 fn getPacked(values: *const ModePacked, mode: Mode) bool {
   149     switch (mode) {
   150         inline else => |mode_comptime| {
   151             const entry = comptime entryForMode(mode_comptime);
   152             return @field(values, entry.name);
   153         },
   154     }
   155 }

n28 src/terminal/modes.zig:216–231 DI
   216 pub fn modeFromInt(v: u16, ansi: bool) ?Mode {
   217     // ModeTag stores the number in 15 bits. A larger number can't be a
   218     // known mode, and packing it below would spill into the ansi bit.
   219     if (v > std.math.maxInt(u15)) return null;
   220 
   221     // This runs for every mode that is set, reset, or queried, so we use
   222     // a sorted lookup instead of comparing against each entry in turn.
   223     const tag: ModeTag = .{ .ansi = ansi, .value = @intCast(v) };
   224     const int: ModeTag.Backing = @bitCast(tag);
   225     const idx = EntrySet.indexOf(int) orelse return null;
   226     if (comptime entries_any_disabled) {
   227         if (entries_disabled[idx]) return null;
   228     }
   229 
   230     return @fromBackingInt(int);
   231 }

   302             mimes: UnsafePointer<UnsafePointer<CChar>>,

   304             list: Bool
   305         ) -> ghostty_clipboard_read_result_e {
   306             let surfaceView = self.surfaceUserdata(from: userdata)
   307             guard let surface = surfaceView.surface else {
   308                 return GHOSTTY_CLIPBOARD_READ_UNSUPPORTED
   309             }
   310 
   311             // Get our pasteboard
   312             guard let pasteboard = NSPasteboard.ghostty(location) else {
   313                 return GHOSTTY_CLIPBOARD_READ_UNSUPPORTED
   314             }
   315 
   316             // Gather the representation for each requested MIME type that
   317             // the pasteboard can serve. We only ever read the requested
   318             // representations so unrelated (potentially large) clipboard
   319             // contents are never loaded.
   320             var contents: [Ghostty.ClipboardContent] = []
   321             var seen = Set<String>()
   322             for i in 0..<mimesLen {
   323                 let mime = String(cString: mimes[i])
   324                 guard !seen.contains(mime) else { continue }
   325                 seen.insert(mime)
   326                 guard let data = pasteboard.ghosttyData(forMime: mime) else { continue }
   327                 contents.append(.init(mime: mime, data: data))
   328             }
   329 
   330             // The listing of available types, only gathered when requested.
   331             let available: [String] = list ? pasteboard.ghosttyAvailableMimes() : []
   332 
   333             // With nothing to serve and no listing requested there is
   334             // nothing to complete the read with.
   335             if contents.isEmpty && !list {
   336                 return GHOSTTY_CLIPBOARD_READ_UNAVAILABLE
   337             }
   338 
   339             completeClipboardRequest(
   340                 surface,
   341                 contents: contents,
   342                 available: available,
   343                 state: state)
   344             return GHOSTTY_CLIPBOARD_READ_STARTED
   345         }

n29 macos/Sources/Ghostty/Ghostty.App.swift:415–469 DIU
   415         private static func completeClipboardRequest(
   416             _ surface: ghostty_surface_t,
   417             contents: [Ghostty.ClipboardContent],
   418             available: [String],
   419             state: UnsafeMutableRawPointer,
   420             confirmed: Bool = false,
   421             remember: Bool = false
   422         ) {
   423             // Copy everything into C memory for the duration of the call.
   424             var cStrings: [UnsafeMutablePointer<CChar>] = []
   425             var cDatas: [UnsafeMutableRawPointer] = []
   426             defer {
   427                 cStrings.forEach { free($0) }
   428                 cDatas.forEach { $0.deallocate() }
   429             }
   430 
   431             var cContents: [ghostty_clipboard_content_s] = []
   432             for entry in contents {
   433                 guard let mime = strdup(entry.mime) else { continue }
   434                 cStrings.append(mime)
   435                 let buf = UnsafeMutableRawPointer.allocate(
   436                     byteCount: max(entry.data.count, 1),
   437                     alignment: 1)
   438                 cDatas.append(buf)
   439                 entry.data.withUnsafeBytes { src in
   440                     if let base = src.baseAddress {
   441                         buf.copyMemory(from: base, byteCount: src.count)
   442                     }
   443                 }
   444                 cContents.append(ghostty_clipboard_content_s(
   445                     mime: mime,
   446                     data: buf.assumingMemoryBound(to: CChar.self),
   447                     len: entry.data.count))
   448             }
   449 
   450             var cAvailable: [UnsafePointer<CChar>] = []
   451             for mime in available {
   452                 guard let str = strdup(mime) else { continue }
   453                 cStrings.append(str)
   454                 cAvailable.append(UnsafePointer(str))
   455             }
   456 
   457             cContents.withUnsafeBufferPointer { contentsBuf in
   458                 cAvailable.withUnsafeBufferPointer { availableBuf in
   459                     var complete = ghostty_clipboard_complete_s(
   460                         contents: contentsBuf.baseAddress,
   461                         contents_len: contentsBuf.count,
   462                         available: availableBuf.baseAddress,
   463                         available_len: availableBuf.count,
   464                         confirmed: confirmed,
   465                         remember: remember)
   466                     ghostty_surface_complete_clipboard_request(surface, &complete, state)
   467                 }
   468             }
   469         }

n29 src/Surface.zig:3813–3905 DIU
  3813 fn mouseReport(
  3814     self: *Surface,
  3815     button: ?input.MouseButton,
  3816     action: input.MouseAction,
  3817     mods: input.Mods,
  3818     pos: apprt.CursorPos,
  3819 ) void {
  3820     // Mouse reporting must be enabled by both config and terminal state
  3821     assert(self.config.mouse_reporting);
  3822     assert(self.io.terminal.flags.mouse_event != .none);
  3823 
  3824     // Build our encoding options.
  3825     const encoding_opts: input.mouse_encode.Options = opts: {
  3826         // Terminal and size state.
  3827         var opts: input.mouse_encode.Options = .fromTerminal(
  3828             &self.io.terminal,
  3829             self.size,
  3830         );
  3831 
  3832         // Whether any button is pressed at all.
  3833         opts.any_button_pressed = pressed: {
  3834             for (self.mouse.click_state) |state| {
  3835                 if (state != .release) break :pressed true;
  3836             }
  3837 
  3838             break :pressed false;
  3839         };
  3840 
  3841         // Keep track of our last reported viewport cell for event
  3842         // deduplication.
  3843         opts.last_cell = &self.mouse.event_point;
  3844 
  3845         break :opts opts;
  3846     };
  3847 
  3848     var data: termio.Message.WriteReq.Small.Array = undefined;
  3849     var writer: std.Io.Writer = .fixed(&data);
  3850     input.mouse_encode.encode(&writer, .{
  3851         .button = button,
  3852         .action = action,
  3853         .mods = mods,
  3854         .pos = .{
  3855             .x = pos.x,
  3856             .y = pos.y,
  3857         },
  3858     }, encoding_opts) catch |err| switch (err) {
  3859         error.WriteFailed => {
  3860             // This should never happen since mouse events should never
  3861             // be able to overflow the size of our small array. But if it
  3862             // does, let's log it and return. No need to crash upstreams.
  3863             // In the future we may want to fall back to allocation.
  3864             log.warn("failed to encode mouse event err={}", .{err});
  3865             return;
  3866         },
  3867     };
  3868     const written = writer.buffered();
  3869     if (written.len == 0) return;
  3870 
  3871     self.queueIo(.{ .write_small = .{
  3872         .data = data,
  3873         .len = @intCast(written.len),
  3874     } }, .locked);
  3875 }
  3876 
  3877 /// Returns true if the shift modifier is allowed to be captured by modifier
  3878 /// events. It is up to the caller to still verify it is a situation in which
  3879 /// shift capture makes sense (i.e. left button, mouse click, etc.)
  3880 fn mouseShiftCapture(self: *const Surface, lock: bool) bool {
  3881     // Handle our never/always case where we don't need a lock.
  3882     switch (self.config.mouse_shift_capture) {
  3883         .never => return false,
  3884         .always => return true,
  3885         .false, .true => {},
  3886     }
  3887 
  3888     if (lock) self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  3889     defer if (lock) self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  3890 
  3891     // If the terminal explicitly requests it then we always allow it
  3892     // since we processed never/always at this point.
  3893     switch (self.io.terminal.flags.mouse_shift_capture) {
  3894         .false => return false,
  3895         .true => return true,
  3896         .null => {},
  3897     }
  3898 
  3899     // Otherwise, go with the user's preference
  3900     return switch (self.config.mouse_shift_capture) {
  3901         .false => false,
  3902         .true => true,
  3903         .never, .always => unreachable, // handled earlier
  3904     };
  3905 }

n29 src/Surface.zig:6219–6262 DIU
  6219 fn startClipboardRequest(
  6220     self: *Surface,
  6221     loc: apprt.Clipboard,
  6222     req: apprt.ClipboardRequest,
  6223 ) !apprt.ClipboardReadResult {
  6224     const effective_req: apprt.ClipboardRequest = switch (req) {
  6225         .paste => |clipboard| effective: {
  6226             // Snapshot the mode before asking the apprt for clipboard data.
  6227             // Event pastes request only a MIME listing, while ordinary
  6228             // pastes request the text representation as before.
  6229             self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  6230             const event = self.io.terminal.modes.get(.kitty_paste_events);
  6231             self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  6232 
  6233             break :effective if (event)
  6234                 .{ .list = clipboard }
  6235             else
  6236                 req;
  6237         },
  6238         else => req,
  6239     };
  6240 
  6241     switch (effective_req) {
  6242         .paste, .list => {}, // always allowed
  6243         .osc_52_read => if (self.config.clipboard_read == .deny) {
  6244             log.info(
  6245                 "application attempted to read clipboard, but 'clipboard-read' is set to deny",
  6246                 .{},
  6247             );
  6248             return .unsupported;
  6249         },
  6250 
  6251         // The clipboard access policies were already applied by
  6252         // kittyClipboardRead and kittyClipboardWrite, which own
  6253         // replying on denial.
  6254         .kitty_read, .kitty_write => {},
  6255 
  6256         // OSC 52 writes don't travel through this function; they go
  6257         // straight to the apprt setClipboard API.
  6258         .osc_52_write => unreachable,
  6259     }
  6260 
  6261     return try self.rt_surface.clipboardRequest(loc, effective_req);
  6262 }

n29 src/Surface.zig:6264–6341 DIU
  6264 fn completeClipboardPaste(
  6265     self: *Surface,
  6266     data: []const u8,
  6267     allow_unsafe: bool,
  6268 ) !void {
  6269     if (data.len == 0) return;
  6270 
  6271     const encode_opts: input.paste.Options = encode_opts: {
  6272         self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  6273         defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  6274         const opts: input.paste.Options = .fromTerminal(&self.io.terminal);
  6275 
  6276         // If we have paste protection enabled, we detect unsafe pastes and return
  6277         // an error. The error approach allows apprt to attempt to complete the paste
  6278         // before falling back to requesting confirmation.
  6279         //
  6280         // We do not do this for bracketed pastes because bracketed pastes are
  6281         // by definition safe since they're framed.
  6282         const unsafe = unsafe: {
  6283             // If we've disabled paste protection then we always allow the paste.
  6284             if (!self.config.clipboard_paste_protection) break :unsafe false;
  6285 
  6286             // If we're allowed to paste unsafe data then we always allow the paste.
  6287             // This is set during confirmation usually.
  6288             if (allow_unsafe) break :unsafe false;
  6289 
  6290             if (opts.bracketed) {
  6291                 // If we're bracketed and the paste contains and ending
  6292                 // bracket then something naughty might be going on and we
  6293                 // never trust it.
  6294                 if (std.mem.indexOf(u8, data, "\x1B[201~") != null) break :unsafe true;
  6295 
  6296                 // If we are bracketed and configured to trust that then the
  6297                 // paste is not unsafe.
  6298                 if (self.config.clipboard_paste_bracketed_safe) break :unsafe false;
  6299             }
  6300 
  6301             break :unsafe !input.paste.isSafe(data);
  6302         };
  6303 
  6304         if (unsafe) {
  6305             log.info("potentially unsafe paste detected, rejecting until confirmation", .{});
  6306             return error.UnsafePaste;
  6307         }
  6308 
  6309         // With the lock held, we must scroll to the bottom.
  6310         // We always scroll to the bottom for these inputs.
  6311         self.scrollToBottom() catch |err| {
  6312             log.warn("error scrolling to bottom err={}", .{err});
  6313         };
  6314 
  6315         break :encode_opts opts;
  6316     };
  6317 
  6318     // Encode the data. In most cases this doesn't require any
  6319     // copies, so we optimize for that case.
  6320     var data_duped: ?[]u8 = null;
  6321     const vecs = input.paste.encode(data, encode_opts) catch |err| switch (err) {
  6322         error.MutableRequired => vecs: {
  6323             const buf: []u8 = try self.alloc.dupe(u8, data);
  6324             errdefer self.alloc.free(buf);
  6325             data_duped = buf;
  6326             break :vecs input.paste.encode(buf, encode_opts);
  6327         },
  6328     };
  6329     defer if (data_duped) |v| {
  6330         // This code path means the data did require a copy and mutation.
  6331         // We must free it.
  6332         self.alloc.free(v);
  6333     };
  6334 
  6335     for (vecs) |vec| if (vec.len > 0) {
  6336         self.queueIo(try termio.Message.writeReq(
  6337             self.alloc,
  6338             vec,
  6339         ), .unlocked);
  6340     };
  6341 }

n29 src/Surface.zig:6343–6398 DIU
  6343 /// Send a Kitty clipboard-protocol paste event when mode 5522 is enabled.
  6344 /// The event only lists the available MIME types; it does not read any of
  6345 /// their data. The shared terminal paste implementation generates and records
  6346 /// the one-time password used by the program's follow-up OSC 5522 read.
  6347 fn completeClipboardPasteEvent(
  6348     self: *Surface,
  6349     clipboard: apprt.Clipboard,
  6350     available: []const []const u8,
  6351 ) !bool {
  6352     if (self.readonly) return false;
  6353 
  6354     const kitty_clipboard = terminal.kitty.clipboard;
  6355     const location: terminal.clipboard.Location = switch (clipboard) {
  6356         .standard => .standard,
  6357         .selection => .selection,
  6358         .primary => .primary,
  6359     };
  6360 
  6361     // The protocol implementation caps listings at this size too. Cap here
  6362     // so the temporary Content array stays on the stack.
  6363     var contents_buf: [kitty_clipboard.max_listing_mimes]terminal.clipboard.Content = undefined;
  6364     const contents_len = @min(available.len, contents_buf.len);
  6365     for (available[0..contents_len], contents_buf[0..contents_len]) |mime, *content| {
  6366         content.* = .{ .mime = mime, .data = "" };
  6367     }
  6368 
  6369     var aw: std.Io.Writer.Allocating = .init(self.alloc);
  6370     defer aw.deinit();
  6371 
  6372     self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  6373     defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  6374 
  6375     const pasted = try terminal.paste.paste(.{
  6376         .terminal = &self.io.terminal,
  6377         .alloc = self.alloc,
  6378         .writer = &aw.writer,
  6379         .kitty_clipboard = .{
  6380             .grants = &self.io.terminal_stream.handler.kitty_clipboard_grants,
  6381             .io = global.io(),
  6382         },
  6383     }, .{
  6384         .source = .{ .clipboard = location },
  6385         .contents = .{ .memory = contents_buf[0..contents_len] },
  6386         // A paste event discloses no clipboard data, so unsafe-text
  6387         // confirmation does not apply. If mode 5522 is reset, the empty
  6388         // stand-in representations cause the shared helper to write nothing.
  6389         .allow_unsafe = true,
  6390     });
  6391     if (!pasted) return false;
  6392 
  6393     self.queueIo(.{ .write_alloc = .{
  6394         .alloc = self.alloc,
  6395         .data = try aw.toOwnedSlice(),
  6396     } }, .locked);
  6397     return true;
  6398 }

n29 src/apprt/embedded.zig:742–801 DIU
   742         // The representations the read wants served. Text-only
   743         // requesters ask for the canonical text type; Kitty clipboard
   744         // reads ask for exactly what the program requested, with
   745         // text-like aliases normalized so the embedder never has to
   746         // know about them.
   747         var mimes_buf: [terminal.kitty.clipboard.max_read_mimes][*:0]const u8 = undefined;
   748         const mimes: []const [*:0]const u8 = switch (state) {
   749             .paste, .osc_52_read => &.{"text/plain"},
   750 
   751             // A mode 5522 paste event only lists types and must not read
   752             // any clipboard data.
   753             .list => &.{},
   754 
   755             .kitty_read => |kitty| mimes: {
   756                 assert(kitty.mimes.len <= mimes_buf.len);
   757                 for (kitty.mimes, mimes_buf[0..kitty.mimes.len]) |mime, *dst| {
   758                     dst.* = if (terminal.clipboard.isTextMime(mime))
   759                         "text/plain"
   760                     else
   761                         mime.ptr;
   762                 }
   763                 break :mimes mimes_buf[0..kitty.mimes.len];
   764             },
   765 
   766             // Handled above.
   767             .kitty_write => unreachable,
   768 
   769             // No clipboard write code paths travel through this function
   770             .osc_52_write => unreachable,
   771         };
   772         const list = switch (state) {
   773             // Paste events need the full MIME listing without reading any
   774             // representation. Kitty reads only ask for it when requested.
   775             .list => true,
   776             .kitty_read => |kitty| kitty.list,
   777             else => false,
   778         };
   779 
   780         // We need to allocate to get a pointer to store our clipboard request
   781         // so that it is stable until the read_clipboard callback and call
   782         // complete_clipboard_request. This sucks but clipboard requests aren't
   783         // high throughput so it's probably fine.
   784         const alloc = self.app.core_app.alloc;
   785         const state_ptr = try alloc.create(apprt.ClipboardRequest);
   786         errdefer alloc.destroy(state_ptr);
   787         state_ptr.* = state;
   788 
   789         const result = self.app.opts.read_clipboard(
   790             self.userdata,
   791             @intCast(@backingInt(clipboard_type)),
   792             state_ptr,
   793             mimes.ptr,
   794             mimes.len,
   795             list,
   796         );
   797 
   798         // Only a started request completes later and keeps the state.
   799         if (result != .started) alloc.destroy(state_ptr);
   800         return result;
   801     }

n29 src/apprt/gtk/class/surface.zig:4851–4912 DIU
  4851     fn clipboardReadText(
  4852         source: ?*gobject.Object,
  4853         res: *gio.AsyncResult,
  4854         ud: ?*anyopaque,
  4855     ) callconv(.c) void {
  4856         const clipboard = gobject.ext.cast(
  4857             gdk.Clipboard,
  4858             source orelse return,
  4859         ) orelse return;
  4860         const req: *Request = @ptrCast(@alignCast(ud orelse return));
  4861 
  4862         const alloc = Application.default().allocator();
  4863         defer alloc.destroy(req);
  4864 
  4865         const self = req.self;
  4866         defer self.unref();
  4867 
  4868         var gerr: ?*glib.Error = null;
  4869         const cstr_ = clipboard.readTextFinish(res, &gerr);
  4870         if (gerr) |err| {
  4871             defer err.free();
  4872             log.warn(
  4873                 "failed to read clipboard err={s}",
  4874                 .{err.f_message orelse "(no message)"},
  4875             );
  4876 
  4877             // The requester may be waiting on a reply, e.g. an OSC 52
  4878             // read of a clipboard with no text representation. Denying
  4879             // the request answers the protocols that expect one.
  4880             if (self.private().core_surface) |surface| {
  4881                 surface.denyClipboardRequest(req.state);
  4882             }
  4883             return;
  4884         }
  4885         const cstr = cstr_ orelse return;
  4886         defer glib.free(cstr);
  4887         const str = std.mem.sliceTo(cstr, 0);
  4888 
  4889         const surface = self.private().core_surface orelse return;
  4890         surface.completeClipboardRequest(
  4891             req.state,
  4892             .{ .contents = &.{.{ .mime = "text/plain", .data = str }} },
  4893         ) catch |err| switch (err) {
  4894             error.UnsafePaste,
  4895             error.UnauthorizedPaste,
  4896             => {
  4897                 showClipboardConfirmation(
  4898                     self,
  4899                     req.state,
  4900                     &[_]terminal.clipboard.Content{.{ .mime = "text/plain", .data = str }},
  4901                 );
  4902                 return;
  4903             },
  4904 
  4905             else => {
  4906                 log.warn(
  4907                     "failed to complete clipboard request err={}",
  4908                     .{err},
  4909                 );
  4910                 return;
  4911             },
  4912         };

n29 src/datastruct/message_data.zig:38–74 DI
    38         /// Initializes the union for a given data type. This will
    39         /// attempt to fit into a small value if possible, otherwise
    40         /// will allocate and put into alloc.
    41         ///
    42         /// This can't and will never detect stable pointers.
    43         pub fn init(alloc: Allocator, data: anytype) !Self {
    44             switch (@typeInfo(@TypeOf(data))) {
    45                 .pointer => |info| {
    46                     assert(info.size == .slice);
    47                     assert(info.child == Elem);
    48 
    49                     // If it fits in our small request, do that.
    50                     if (data.len <= Small.Max) {
    51                         var buf: Small.Array = undefined;
    52                         @memcpy(buf[0..data.len], data);
    53                         return Self{
    54                             .small = .{
    55                                 .data = buf,
    56                                 .len = @intCast(data.len),
    57                             },
    58                         };
    59                     }
    60 
    61                     // Otherwise, allocate
    62                     const buf = try alloc.dupe(Elem, data);
    63                     errdefer alloc.free(buf);
    64                     return Self{
    65                         .alloc = .{
    66                             .alloc = alloc,
    67                             .data = buf,
    68                         },
    69                     };
    70                 },
    71 
    72                 else => unreachable,
    73             }
    74         }

n29 src/input/mouse_encode.zig:12–76 DIU
    12 /// Options that affect mouse encoding behavior and provide runtime context.
    13 pub const Options = struct {
    14     /// Terminal mouse reporting mode (X10, normal, button, any).
    15     event: terminal.MouseEvent = .none,
    16 
    17     /// Terminal mouse reporting format.
    18     format: terminal.MouseFormat = .x10,
    19 
    20     /// Full renderer size used to convert surface-space pixel positions
    21     /// into grid cell coordinates (for most formats) and terminal-space
    22     /// pixel coordinates (for SGR-Pixels), as well as to determine
    23     /// whether a position falls outside the visible viewport.
    24     size: renderer_size.Size,
    25 
    26     /// Whether any mouse button is currently pressed. When a motion
    27     /// event occurs outside the viewport, it is only reported if a
    28     /// button is held down and the event mode supports motion tracking.
    29     /// Without this, out-of-viewport motions are silently dropped.
    30     ///
    31     /// This should reflect the state of the current event as well, so
    32     /// if the encoded event is a button press, this should be true.
    33     any_button_pressed: bool = false,
    34 
    35     /// Last reported viewport cell for motion deduplication.
    36     /// If null, motion deduplication state is not tracked.
    37     last_cell: ?*?point.Coordinate = null,
    38 
    39     /// Initialize from terminal and renderer state. The caller may still
    40     /// set any_button_pressed and last_cell on the returned value.
    41     pub fn fromTerminal(
    42         t: *const Terminal,
    43         size: renderer_size.Size,
    44     ) Options {
    45         return .{
    46             .event = t.flags.mouse_event,
    47             .format = t.flags.mouse_format,
    48             .size = size,
    49         };
    50     }
    51 };
    52 
    53 /// A normalized mouse event for protocol encoding.
    54 pub const Event = struct {
    55     /// The action of this mouse event.
    56     action: mouse.Action = .press,
    57 
    58     /// The button involved in this event. This can be null in the
    59     /// case of a motion action with no pressed buttons.
    60     button: ?mouse.Button = null,
    61 
    62     /// Keyboard modifiers held during this event.
    63     mods: key.Mods = .{},
    64 
    65     /// Mouse position in terminal-space pixels, with (0, 0) at the top-left
    66     /// of the terminal. Negative values are allowed and indicate positions
    67     /// above or to the left of the terminal. Values larger than the terminal
    68     /// size are also allowed and indicate right or below the terminal.
    69     pos: Pos = .{},
    70 
    71     /// Mouse position in surface-space pixels.
    72     pub const Pos = extern struct {
    73         x: f32 = 0,
    74         y: f32 = 0,
    75     };
    76 };

n29 src/input/mouse_encode.zig:78–280 DIU
    78 /// Encode the mouse event to the writer according to the options.
    79 ///
    80 /// Not all events result in output.
    81 pub fn encode(
    82     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
    83     event: Event,
    84     opts: Options,
    85 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
    86     if (!shouldReport(event, opts)) return;
    87 
    88     // Handle scenarios where the mouse position is outside the viewport.
    89     // We always report release events no matter where they happen.
    90     if (event.action != .release and
    91         posOutOfViewport(event.pos, opts.size))
    92     {
    93         // If we don't have a motion-tracking event mode, do nothing,
    94         // because events outside the viewport are never reported in
    95         // such cases.
    96         if (!terminal.mouse.eventSendsMotion(opts.event)) return;
    97 
    98         // For motion modes, we only report if a button is currently pressed.
    99         // This lets a TUI detect a click over the surface + drag out
   100         // of the surface.
   101         if (!opts.any_button_pressed) return;
   102     }
   103 
   104     const cell = posToCell(event.pos, opts.size);
   105 
   106     // We only send motion events when the cell changed unless
   107     // we're tracking raw pixels.
   108     if (event.action == .motion and opts.format != .sgr_pixels) {
   109         if (opts.last_cell) |last| {
   110             if (last.*) |last_cell| {
   111                 if (last_cell.eql(cell)) return;
   112             }
   113         }
   114     }
   115 
   116     // Update the last reported cell if we are tracking it.
   117     if (opts.last_cell) |last| last.* = cell;
   118 
   119     const button_code = buttonCode(event, opts) orelse return;
   120     switch (opts.format) {
   121         .x10 => {
   122             if (cell.x > 222 or cell.y > 222) {
   123                 log.info("X10 mouse format can only encode X/Y up to 223", .{});
   124                 return;
   125             }
   126 
   127             // + 1 because our x/y are zero-indexed and the protocol uses 1-indexing.
   128             try writer.writeAll("\x1B[M");
   129             try writer.writeByte(32 + button_code);
   130             try writer.writeByte(32 + @as(u8, @intCast(cell.x)) + 1);
   131             try writer.writeByte(32 + @as(u8, @intCast(cell.y)) + 1);
   132         },
   133 
   134         .utf8 => {
   135             try writer.writeAll("\x1B[M");
   136             try writer.printUnicodeCodepoint(32 + @as(u21, button_code));
   137 
   138             var buf: [4]u8 = undefined;
   139             const x_cp: u21 = @intCast(@as(u32, cell.x) + 33);
   140             const y_cp: u21 = @intCast(cell.y + 33);
   141 
   142             const x_len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(x_cp, &buf) catch unreachable;
   143             try writer.writeAll(buf[0..x_len]);
   144 
   145             const y_len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(y_cp, &buf) catch unreachable;
   146             try writer.writeAll(buf[0..y_len]);
   147         },
   148 
   149         .sgr => try writer.print("\x1B[<{d};{d};{d}{c}", .{
   150             button_code,
   151             cell.x + 1,
   152             cell.y + 1,
   153             @as(u8, if (event.action == .release) 'm' else 'M'),
   154         }),
   155 
   156         .urxvt => try writer.print("\x1B[{d};{d};{d}M", .{
   157             32 + button_code,
   158             cell.x + 1,
   159             cell.y + 1,
   160         }),
   161 
   162         .sgr_pixels => {
   163             const pixels = posToPixels(event.pos, opts.size);
   164             try writer.print("\x1B[<{d};{d};{d}{c}", .{
   165                 button_code,
   166                 pixels.x,
   167                 pixels.y,
   168                 @as(u8, if (event.action == .release) 'm' else 'M'),
   169             });
   170         },
   171     }
   172 }
   173 
   174 /// Returns true if this event should be reported for the given mouse
   175 /// event mode.
   176 fn shouldReport(event: Event, opts: Options) bool {
   177     return switch (opts.event) {
   178         .none => false,
   179 
   180         // X10 only reports button presses of left, middle, and right.
   181         .x10 => event.action == .press and
   182             event.button != null and
   183             (event.button.? == .left or
   184                 event.button.? == .middle or
   185                 event.button.? == .right),
   186 
   187         // Normal mode does not report motion.
   188         .normal => event.action != .motion,
   189 
   190         // Button mode requires an active button for motion events.
   191         .button => event.button != null,
   192 
   193         // Any mode reports everything.
   194         .any => true,
   195     };
   196 }
   197 
   198 fn buttonCode(event: Event, opts: Options) ?u8 {
   199     var acc: u8 = code: {
   200         if (event.button == null) {
   201             // Null button means motion with no pressed button.
   202             break :code 3;
   203         }
   204 
   205         if (event.action == .release and
   206             opts.format != .sgr and
   207             opts.format != .sgr_pixels)
   208         {
   209             // Legacy releases are always encoded as button 3.
   210             break :code 3;
   211         }
   212 
   213         break :code switch (event.button.?) {
   214             .left => 0,
   215             .middle => 1,
   216             .right => 2,
   217             .four => 64,
   218             .five => 65,
   219             .six => 66,
   220             .seven => 67,
   221             .eight => 128,
   222             .nine => 129,
   223             else => return null,
   224         };
   225     };
   226 
   227     // X10 does not include modifiers.
   228     if (opts.event != .x10) {
   229         if (event.mods.shift) acc += 4;
   230         if (event.mods.alt) acc += 8;
   231         if (event.mods.ctrl) acc += 16;
   232     }
   233 
   234     // Motion adds another bit.
   235     if (event.action == .motion) acc += 32;
   236 
   237     return acc;
   238 }
   239 
   240 /// Terminal-space pixel position for SGR pixel reporting.
   241 const PixelPoint = struct {
   242     x: i32,
   243     y: i32,
   244 };
   245 
   246 /// Returns true if the surface-space pixel position is outside the
   247 /// visible viewport bounds (negative or beyond screen dimensions).
   248 fn posOutOfViewport(pos: Event.Pos, size: renderer_size.Size) bool {
   249     const max_x: f32 = @floatFromInt(size.screen.width);
   250     const max_y: f32 = @floatFromInt(size.screen.height);
   251     return pos.x < 0 or pos.y < 0 or pos.x > max_x or pos.y > max_y;
   252 }
   253 
   254 /// Converts a surface-space pixel position to a zero-based grid cell
   255 /// coordinate (column, row) within the terminal viewport. Out-of-bounds
   256 /// values are clamped to the valid grid range (0 to columns/rows - 1).
   257 fn posToCell(pos: Event.Pos, size: renderer_size.Size) point.Coordinate {
   258     const coord: renderer_size.Coordinate = .{ .surface = .{
   259         .x = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.x)),
   260         .y = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.y)),
   261     } };
   262     const grid = coord.convert(.grid, size).grid;
   263     return .{ .x = grid.x, .y = grid.y };
   264 }
   265 
   266 /// Converts a surface-space pixel position to terminal-space pixel
   267 /// coordinates (accounting for padding/scaling) used by SGR-Pixels mode.
   268 /// Unlike grid conversion, terminal-space coordinates are not clamped
   269 /// and may be negative or exceed the terminal dimensions.
   270 fn posToPixels(pos: Event.Pos, size: renderer_size.Size) PixelPoint {
   271     const coord: renderer_size.Coordinate.Terminal = (renderer_size.Coordinate{ .surface = .{
   272         .x = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.x)),
   273         .y = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.y)),
   274     } }).convert(.terminal, size).terminal;
   275 
   276     return .{
   277         .x = @as(i32, @intFromFloat(@round(coord.x))),
   278         .y = @as(i32, @intFromFloat(@round(coord.y))),
   279     };
   280 }

     4 /// The bracketed paste (mode 2004) frame written around the data.
     5 pub const bracketed_prefix = "\x1b[200~";
     6 pub const bracketed_suffix = "\x1b[201~";
     7 
     8 /// The maximum number of bytes `encode` adds around the data, so callers
     9 /// can size a buffer for the full encoded result.
    10 pub const max_frame_size = bracketed_prefix.len + bracketed_suffix.len;
    11 
    12 pub const Options = struct {

    14     bracketed: bool,
    15

    19             .bracketed = t.modes.get(.bracketed_paste),

n29 src/input/paste.zig:24–125 DI
    24 /// Encode the given data for pasting. The resulting value can be written
    25 /// to the pty to perform a paste of the input data.
    26 ///
    27 /// The data can be either a `[]u8` or a `[]const u8`. If the data
    28 /// type is const then `EncodeError` may be returned. If the data type
    29 /// is mutable then this function can't return an error.
    30 ///
    31 /// This slightly complex calling style allows for initially const
    32 /// data to be passed in without an allocation, since it is rare in normal
    33 /// use cases that the data will need to be modified. In the unlikely case
    34 /// data does need to be modified, the caller can make a mutable copy
    35 /// after seeing the error.
    36 ///
    37 /// The data is returned as a set of slices to limit allocations. The caller
    38 /// can combine the slices into a single buffer if desired.
    39 ///
    40 /// WARNING: The input data is not checked for safety. See the `isSafe`
    41 /// function to check if the data is safe to paste.
    42 pub fn encode(
    43     data: anytype,
    44     opts: Options,
    45 ) switch (@TypeOf(data)) {
    46     []u8 => [3][]const u8,
    47     []const u8 => Error![3][]const u8,
    48     else => unreachable,
    49 } {
    50     // These are the set of byte values that are always replaced by
    51     // a space (per xterm's behavior) for any text insertion method e.g.
    52     // a paste, drag and drop, etc. These are copied directly from xterm's
    53     // source.
    54     const strip: []const u8 = &.{
    55         0x00, // NUL
    56         0x08, // BS
    57         0x05, // ENQ
    58         0x04, // EOT
    59         0x1B, // ESC
    60         0x7F, // DEL
    61 
    62         // These can be overridden by the running terminal program
    63         // via tcsetattr, so they aren't totally safe to hardcode like
    64         // this. In practice, I haven't seen modern programs change these
    65         // and its a much bigger architectural change to pass these through
    66         // so for now they're hardcoded.
    67         0x03, // VINTR (Ctrl+C)
    68         0x1C, // VQUIT (Ctrl+\)
    69         0x15, // VKILL (Ctrl+U)
    70         0x1A, // VSUSP (Ctrl+Z)
    71         0x11, // VSTART (Ctrl+Q)
    72         0x13, // VSTOP (Ctrl+S)
    73         0x17, // VWERASE (Ctrl+W)
    74         0x16, // VLNEXT (Ctrl+V)
    75         0x12, // VREPRINT (Ctrl+R)
    76         0x0F, // VDISCARD (Ctrl+O)
    77     };
    78 
    79     const mutable = @TypeOf(data) == []u8;
    80 
    81     var result: [3][]const u8 = .{ "", data, "" };
    82 
    83     // If we have any of the strip values, then we need to replace them
    84     // with spaces. This is what xterm does and it does it regardless
    85     // of bracketed paste mode. This is a security measure to prevent pastes
    86     // from containing bytes that could be used to inject commands.
    87     if (std.mem.indexOfAny(u8, data, strip) != null) {
    88         if (comptime !mutable) return Error.MutableRequired;
    89         var offset: usize = 0;
    90         while (std.mem.indexOfAny(
    91             u8,
    92             data[offset..],
    93             strip,
    94         )) |idx| {
    95             offset += idx;
    96             data[offset] = ' ';
    97             offset += 1;
    98         }
    99     }
   100 
   101     // Bracketed paste mode (mode 2004) wraps pasted data in
   102     // fenceposts so that the terminal can ignore things like newlines.
   103     if (opts.bracketed) {
   104         result[0] = bracketed_prefix;
   105         result[2] = bracketed_suffix;
   106         return result;
   107     }
   108 
   109     // Non-bracketed. We have to replace newline with `\r`. This matches
   110     // the behavior of xterm and other terminals. For `\r\n` this will
   111     // result in `\r\r` which does match xterm.
   112     if (comptime mutable) {
   113         std.mem.replaceScalar(u8, data, '\n', '\r');
   114     } else if (std.mem.indexOfScalar(u8, data, '\n') != null) {
   115         return Error.MutableRequired;
   116     }
   117 
   118     return result;
   119 }
   120 
   121 pub const Error = error{
   122     /// Returned if encoding requires a mutable copy of the data. This
   123     /// can only be returned if the input data type is const.
   124     MutableRequired,
   125 };

n29 src/input/paste.zig:140–161 DIU
   140 pub fn encodeWriter(
   141     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   142     data: []const u8,
   143     opts: Options,
   144 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
   145     if (opts.bracketed) try writer.writeAll(bracketed_prefix);
   146 
   147     // The byte transformations are position-independent, so the data
   148     // can be copied and encoded chunk by chunk. The frame returned by
   149     // encode is ignored since it's written around the whole data here.
   150     var remaining = data;
   151     while (remaining.len > 0) {
   152         const dest = try writer.writableSliceGreedy(1);
   153         const n = @min(dest.len, remaining.len);
   154         @memcpy(dest[0..n], remaining[0..n]);
   155         _ = encode(dest[0..n], opts);
   156         writer.advance(n);
   157         remaining = remaining[n..];
   158     }
   159 
   160     if (opts.bracketed) try writer.writeAll(bracketed_suffix);
   161 }

n29 src/input/paste.zig:180–183 DI
   180 pub fn isSafe(data: []const u8) bool {
   181     return std.mem.indexOfAny(u8, data, "\r\n") == null and
   182         std.mem.indexOf(u8, data, "\x1b[201~") == null;
   183 }

   198     return isSafe(data);
   199 }

n29 src/renderer/size.zig:114–151 DIU
   114     /// Convert a coordinate to a different space within the same Size.
   115     pub fn convert(self: Coordinate, to: Tag, size: Size) Coordinate {
   116         // Unlikely fast-path but avoid work.
   117         if (@as(Tag, self) == to) return self;
   118 
   119         // To avoid the combinatorial explosion of conversion functions, we
   120         // convert to the surface system first and then reconvert from there.
   121         const surface = self.convertToSurface(size);
   122 
   123         return switch (to) {
   124             .surface => .{ .surface = surface },
   125             .terminal => .{ .terminal = .{
   126                 .x = surface.x - @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.padding.left)),
   127                 .y = surface.y - @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.padding.top)),
   128             } },
   129             .grid => grid: {
   130                 // Get rid of the padding.
   131                 const term = (Coordinate{ .surface = surface }).convert(
   132                     .terminal,
   133                     size,
   134                 ).terminal;
   135 
   136                 // We need our grid to clamp
   137                 const grid = size.grid();
   138 
   139                 // Calculate the grid position.
   140                 const cell_width: f64 = @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.cell.width));
   141                 const cell_height: f64 = @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.cell.height));
   142                 const clamped_x: f64 = @max(0, term.x);
   143                 const clamped_y: f64 = @max(0, term.y);
   144                 const col: GridSize.Unit = @intFromFloat(clamped_x / cell_width);
   145                 const row: GridSize.Unit = @intFromFloat(clamped_y / cell_height);
   146                 const clamped_col: GridSize.Unit = @min(col, grid.columns - 1);
   147                 const clamped_row: GridSize.Unit = @min(row, grid.rows - 1);
   148                 break :grid .{ .grid = .{ .x = clamped_col, .y = clamped_row } };
   149             },
   150         };
   151     }

n29 src/renderer/size.zig:154–174 DI
   154     fn convertToSurface(self: Coordinate, size: Size) Surface {
   155         return switch (self) {
   156             .surface => |v| v,
   157             .terminal => |v| .{
   158                 .x = v.x + @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.padding.left)),
   159                 .y = v.y + @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.padding.top)),
   160             },
   161             .grid => |v| grid: {
   162                 const col: f64 = @floatFromInt(v.x);
   163                 const row: f64 = @floatFromInt(v.y);
   164                 const cell_width: f64 = @floatFromInt(size.cell.width);
   165                 const cell_height: f64 = @floatFromInt(size.cell.height);
   166                 const padding_left: f64 = @floatFromInt(size.padding.left);
   167                 const padding_top: f64 = @floatFromInt(size.padding.top);
   168                 break :grid .{
   169                     .x = col * cell_width + padding_left,
   170                     .y = row * cell_height + padding_top,
   171                 };
   172             },
   173         };
   174     }
