n29 src/terminal/kitty/clipboard_response.zig:29–75 DIU
    29 /// A single response packet.
    30 pub const Response = struct {
    31     op: Operation,
    32     status: Status,
    33     primary: bool = false,
    34     id: []const u8 = "",
    35     mime: ?[]const u8 = null,
    36     pw: ?[]const u8 = null,
    37     /// The raw payload; the encoder base64-encodes it. An empty payload
    38     /// emits no payload section at all (no ';').
    39     payload: []const u8 = "",
    40     terminator: Terminator = .st,
    41 
    42     /// Encode the response. Errors may result in partially written data
    43     /// so it is up to callers to buffer it if they need to.
    44     pub fn encode(
    45         self: *const Response,
    46         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
    47     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
    48         try self.encodeMetadata(writer);
    49         if (self.payload.len > 0) {
    50             try writer.writeAll(";");
    51             try b64.encodeWriter(writer, self.payload);
    52         }
    53         try writer.writeAll(self.terminator.string());
    54     }
    55 
    56     /// Encode the escape prefix and metadata section only: everything
    57     /// up to (and not including) the payload section and terminator.
    58     fn encodeMetadata(
    59         self: *const Response,
    60         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
    61     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
    62         // The exact order of fields here matches what Kitty does.
    63         try writer.print("\x1b]5522;type={t}:status={t}", .{ self.op, self.status });
    64         if (self.primary) try writer.writeAll(":loc=primary");
    65         if (self.id.len > 0) try writer.print(":id={s}", .{self.id});
    66         if (self.mime) |mime| {
    67             try writer.writeAll(":mime=");
    68             try b64.encodeWriter(writer, mime);
    69         }
    70         if (self.pw) |pw| {
    71             try writer.writeAll(":pw=");
    72             try b64.encodeWriter(writer, pw);
    73         }
    74     }
    75 };

n29 src/terminal/kitty/clipboard_response.zig:77–186 DIU
    77 /// Encode a full successful read response: the OK packet, the targets
    78 /// listing if requested, DATA chunks for each served representation,
    79 /// and the final DONE packet. This is also the shape of an unsolicited
    80 /// paste event (list=true, pw set to the one-time password).
    81 pub const ReadSuccess = struct {
    82     primary: bool = false,
    83     id: []const u8 = "",
    84 
    85     /// One-time password echoed in every packet. Only used for
    86     /// terminal-initiated paste events.
    87     pw: ?[]const u8 = null,
    88 
    89     /// True when the targets ('.') listing was requested.
    90     list: bool = false,
    91 
    92     /// The MIME types available on the clipboard, reported by the
    93     /// targets listing. Kitty reports these space-separated in one
    94     /// packet with a trailing newline when non-empty.
    95     available: []const []const u8 = &.{},
    96 
    97     /// The representations to serve, in request order. Each entry's
    98     /// data is chunked into DATA packets under its own MIME type. An
    99     /// entry with empty data produces no packets, which is how the
   100     /// protocol communicates an unavailable type.
   101     contents: []const Content = &.{},
   102 
   103     terminator: Terminator = .st,
   104 
   105     pub fn encode(
   106         self: *const ReadSuccess,
   107         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   108     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
   109         // Initial read response
   110         try (Response{
   111             .op = .read,
   112             .status = .OK,
   113             .primary = self.primary,
   114             .id = self.id,
   115             .pw = self.pw,
   116             .terminator = self.terminator,
   117         }).encode(writer);
   118 
   119         // Listing of mimes if requested
   120         if (self.list) try self.encodeListing(writer);
   121 
   122         // Encoding of each mime-type + content
   123         for (self.contents) |content| {
   124             var i: usize = 0;
   125             while (i < content.data.len) {
   126                 const n = @min(content.data.len - i, read_chunk_size);
   127                 try (Response{
   128                     .op = .read,
   129                     .status = .DATA,
   130                     .id = self.id,
   131                     .mime = content.mime,
   132                     .pw = self.pw,
   133                     .payload = content.data[i..][0..n],
   134                     .terminator = self.terminator,
   135                 }).encode(writer);
   136                 i += n;
   137             }
   138         }
   139 
   140         // Trailing done.
   141         try (Response{
   142             .op = .read,
   143             .status = .DONE,
   144             .id = self.id,
   145             .pw = self.pw,
   146             .terminator = self.terminator,
   147         }).encode(writer);
   148     }
   149 
   150     /// Encode the targets ('.') listing packet. The listing gets a
   151     /// trailing newline when non-empty.
   152     fn encodeListing(
   153         self: *const ReadSuccess,
   154         writer: *std.Io.Writer,
   155     ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
   156         const listing: Response = .{
   157             .op = .read,
   158             .status = .DATA,
   159             .id = self.id,
   160             .mime = targets_mime,
   161             .pw = self.pw,
   162             .terminator = self.terminator,
   163         };
   164         try listing.encodeMetadata(writer);
   165         if (self.available.len > 0) {
   166             try writer.writeAll(";");
   167 
   168             // Join the types into one chunk before encoding. The
   169             // listing is a DATA packet, so it shares the pre-encoding
   170             // chunk size bound of any other data packet; types that
   171             // don't fit are dropped (a listing that large doesn't
   172             // happen in practice).
   173             var raw: [read_chunk_size]u8 = undefined;
   174             var fixed: std.Io.Writer = .fixed(&raw);
   175             for (self.available, 0..) |mime, i| {
   176                 const sep: usize = if (i > 0) 1 else 0;
   177                 if (fixed.end + sep + mime.len + "\n".len > raw.len) break;
   178                 if (i > 0) fixed.writeAll(" ") catch unreachable;
   179                 fixed.writeAll(mime) catch unreachable;
   180             }
   181             fixed.writeAll("\n") catch unreachable;
   182             try b64.encodeWriter(writer, fixed.buffered());
   183         }
   184         try writer.writeAll(self.terminator.string());
   185     }
   186 };

n29 src/terminal/paste.zig:167–184 DIU
   167 pub fn paste(ctx: Context, req: Request) Error!bool {
   168     // If the source is a clipboard and mode 5522 is enabled and
   169     // the caller can handle kitty events, then do a kitty event.
   170     if (req.source == .clipboard and
   171         ctx.terminal.modes.get(.kitty_paste_events))
   172     {
   173         if (ctx.kitty_clipboard) |kitty| {
   174             try pasteKittyEvent(ctx, kitty, req);
   175             return true;
   176         }
   177     }
   178 
   179     // For non-Kitty paste events we can only accept text content.
   180     const index: usize = for (0..req.contents.len()) |i| {
   181         if (clipboard.isTextMime(req.contents.mime(i))) break i;
   182     } else return false;
   183 
   184     const opts: input_paste.Options = .fromTerminal(ctx.terminal);

n29 src/terminal/paste.zig:202–242 DIU
   202     if (text.len == 0) return false;
   203     if (!req.allow_unsafe and !input_paste.isSafeWith(
   204         text,
   205         opts,
   206     )) return error.UnsafePaste;
   207 
   208     // The text is copied exactly once per chunk, into the writer,
   209     // where the encoder strips and converts it in place.
   210     try input_paste.encodeWriter(ctx.writer, text, opts);
   211     return true;
   212 }
   213 
   214 fn pasteKittyEvent(
   215     ctx: Context,
   216     kitty: Context.KittyClipboard,
   217     req: Request,
   218 ) Error!void {
   219     const otp = try kitty_clipboard.generateOtp(kitty.io);
   220 
   221     // Every representation is listed, never read. The listing is
   222     // bounded; a clipboard with more types than that is not a thing.
   223     var mimes_buf: [kitty_clipboard.max_listing_mimes][]const u8 = undefined;
   224     const mimes_len = @min(req.contents.len(), mimes_buf.len);
   225     for (mimes_buf[0..mimes_len], 0..) |*mime, i| mime.* = req.contents.mime(i);
   226 
   227     // The grant is recorded before the event can reach the program,
   228     // and revoked if the event fails to be written so a failure never
   229     // leaves a grant for an event that was never sent. Using a
   230     // one-time grant consumes it, which is the revocation.
   231     try kitty.grants.grant(ctx.alloc, &otp, .read, true);
   232     errdefer _ = kitty.grants.use(ctx.alloc, &otp, .read);
   233 
   234     try (kitty_clipboard.PasteEvent{
   235         // The protocol only distinguishes the clipboard from the
   236         // primary selection, so both non-standard locations report as
   237         // primary. Only a clipboard paste gets here; see `paste`.
   238         .primary = req.source.clipboard != .standard,
   239         .pw = &otp,
   240         .available = mimes_buf[0..mimes_len],
   241     }).encode(ctx.writer);
   242 }

   107     /// Return a write request for the given data. This will use
   108     /// write_small if it fits or write_alloc otherwise. This should NOT
   109     /// be used for stable pointers which can be manually set to write_stable.
   110     pub fn writeReq(alloc: Allocator, data: anytype) !Message {
   111         return switch (try WriteReq.init(alloc, data)) {
   112             .stable => unreachable,
   113             .small => |v| Message{ .write_small = v },
   114             .alloc => |v| Message{ .write_alloc = v },
   115         };
   116     }

n30 src/Surface.zig:3813–3905 DIU
  3813 fn mouseReport(
  3814     self: *Surface,
  3815     button: ?input.MouseButton,
  3816     action: input.MouseAction,
  3817     mods: input.Mods,
  3818     pos: apprt.CursorPos,
  3819 ) void {
  3820     // Mouse reporting must be enabled by both config and terminal state
  3821     assert(self.config.mouse_reporting);
  3822     assert(self.io.terminal.flags.mouse_event != .none);
  3823 
  3824     // Build our encoding options.
  3825     const encoding_opts: input.mouse_encode.Options = opts: {
  3826         // Terminal and size state.
  3827         var opts: input.mouse_encode.Options = .fromTerminal(
  3828             &self.io.terminal,
  3829             self.size,
  3830         );
  3831 
  3832         // Whether any button is pressed at all.
  3833         opts.any_button_pressed = pressed: {
  3834             for (self.mouse.click_state) |state| {
  3835                 if (state != .release) break :pressed true;
  3836             }
  3837 
  3838             break :pressed false;
  3839         };
  3840 
  3841         // Keep track of our last reported viewport cell for event
  3842         // deduplication.
  3843         opts.last_cell = &self.mouse.event_point;
  3844 
  3845         break :opts opts;
  3846     };
  3847 
  3848     var data: termio.Message.WriteReq.Small.Array = undefined;
  3849     var writer: std.Io.Writer = .fixed(&data);
  3850     input.mouse_encode.encode(&writer, .{
  3851         .button = button,
  3852         .action = action,
  3853         .mods = mods,
  3854         .pos = .{
  3855             .x = pos.x,
  3856             .y = pos.y,
  3857         },
  3858     }, encoding_opts) catch |err| switch (err) {
  3859         error.WriteFailed => {
  3860             // This should never happen since mouse events should never
  3861             // be able to overflow the size of our small array. But if it
  3862             // does, let's log it and return. No need to crash upstreams.
  3863             // In the future we may want to fall back to allocation.
  3864             log.warn("failed to encode mouse event err={}", .{err});
  3865             return;
  3866         },
  3867     };
  3868     const written = writer.buffered();
  3869     if (written.len == 0) return;
  3870 
  3871     self.queueIo(.{ .write_small = .{
  3872         .data = data,
  3873         .len = @intCast(written.len),
  3874     } }, .locked);
  3875 }
  3876 
  3877 /// Returns true if the shift modifier is allowed to be captured by modifier
  3878 /// events. It is up to the caller to still verify it is a situation in which
  3879 /// shift capture makes sense (i.e. left button, mouse click, etc.)
  3880 fn mouseShiftCapture(self: *const Surface, lock: bool) bool {
  3881     // Handle our never/always case where we don't need a lock.
  3882     switch (self.config.mouse_shift_capture) {
  3883         .never => return false,
  3884         .always => return true,
  3885         .false, .true => {},
  3886     }
  3887 
  3888     if (lock) self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  3889     defer if (lock) self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  3890 
  3891     // If the terminal explicitly requests it then we always allow it
  3892     // since we processed never/always at this point.
  3893     switch (self.io.terminal.flags.mouse_shift_capture) {
  3894         .false => return false,
  3895         .true => return true,
  3896         .null => {},
  3897     }
  3898 
  3899     // Otherwise, go with the user's preference
  3900     return switch (self.config.mouse_shift_capture) {
  3901         .false => false,
  3902         .true => true,
  3903         .never, .always => unreachable, // handled earlier
  3904     };
  3905 }

  3935     // Always record our latest mouse state
  3936     self.mouse.click_state[@intCast(@backingInt(button))] = action;
  3937 
  3938     // Always show the mouse again if it is hidden
  3939     if (self.mouse.hidden) self.showMouse();
  3940

  3945     // modifier. Note we can do this more efficiently probably with less

n30 src/Surface.zig:4057–4094 IU
  4057     // Report mouse events if enabled
  4058     {
  4059         self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  4060         defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  4061         if (self.isMouseReporting()) report: {
  4062             // If we have shift-pressed and we aren't allowed to capture it,
  4063             // then we do not do a mouse report.
  4064             if (mods.shift and !shift_capture) break :report;
  4065 
  4066             // In any other mouse button scenario without shift pressed we
  4067             // clear the selection since the underlying application can handle
  4068             // that in any way (i.e. "scrolling").
  4069             try self.setSelection(null);
  4070 
  4071             // We also set the left click count to 0 so that if mouse reporting
  4072             // is disabled in the middle of press (before release) we don't
  4073             // suddenly start selecting text.
  4074             self.mouse.selection_gesture.reset(self.renderer_state.terminal);
  4075 
  4076             const pos = try self.rt_surface.getCursorPos();
  4077 
  4078             const report_action: input.MouseAction = switch (action) {
  4079                 .press => .press,
  4080                 .release => .release,
  4081             };
  4082 
  4083             self.mouseReport(
  4084                 button,
  4085                 report_action,
  4086                 self.mouse.mods,
  4087                 pos,
  4088             );
  4089 
  4090             // If we're doing mouse reporting, we do not support any other
  4091             // selection or highlighting.
  4092             return true;
  4093         }
  4094     }

  4779     if (self.isMouseReporting()) report: {
  4780         // Shift overrides mouse "grabbing" in the window, taken from Kitty.
  4781         // This only applies if there is a mouse button pressed so that

n30 src/input/mouse_encode.zig:12–76 DIU
    12 /// Options that affect mouse encoding behavior and provide runtime context.
    13 pub const Options = struct {
    14     /// Terminal mouse reporting mode (X10, normal, button, any).
    15     event: terminal.MouseEvent = .none,
    16 
    17     /// Terminal mouse reporting format.
    18     format: terminal.MouseFormat = .x10,
    19 
    20     /// Full renderer size used to convert surface-space pixel positions
    21     /// into grid cell coordinates (for most formats) and terminal-space
    22     /// pixel coordinates (for SGR-Pixels), as well as to determine
    23     /// whether a position falls outside the visible viewport.
    24     size: renderer_size.Size,
    25 
    26     /// Whether any mouse button is currently pressed. When a motion
    27     /// event occurs outside the viewport, it is only reported if a
    28     /// button is held down and the event mode supports motion tracking.
    29     /// Without this, out-of-viewport motions are silently dropped.
    30     ///
    31     /// This should reflect the state of the current event as well, so
    32     /// if the encoded event is a button press, this should be true.
    33     any_button_pressed: bool = false,
    34 
    35     /// Last reported viewport cell for motion deduplication.
    36     /// If null, motion deduplication state is not tracked.
    37     last_cell: ?*?point.Coordinate = null,
    38 
    39     /// Initialize from terminal and renderer state. The caller may still
    40     /// set any_button_pressed and last_cell on the returned value.
    41     pub fn fromTerminal(
    42         t: *const Terminal,
    43         size: renderer_size.Size,
    44     ) Options {
    45         return .{
    46             .event = t.flags.mouse_event,
    47             .format = t.flags.mouse_format,
    48             .size = size,
    49         };
    50     }
    51 };
    52 
    53 /// A normalized mouse event for protocol encoding.
    54 pub const Event = struct {
    55     /// The action of this mouse event.
    56     action: mouse.Action = .press,
    57 
    58     /// The button involved in this event. This can be null in the
    59     /// case of a motion action with no pressed buttons.
    60     button: ?mouse.Button = null,
    61 
    62     /// Keyboard modifiers held during this event.
    63     mods: key.Mods = .{},
    64 
    65     /// Mouse position in terminal-space pixels, with (0, 0) at the top-left
    66     /// of the terminal. Negative values are allowed and indicate positions
    67     /// above or to the left of the terminal. Values larger than the terminal
    68     /// size are also allowed and indicate right or below the terminal.
    69     pos: Pos = .{},
    70 
    71     /// Mouse position in surface-space pixels.
    72     pub const Pos = extern struct {
    73         x: f32 = 0,
    74         y: f32 = 0,
    75     };
    76 };

n30 src/input/mouse_encode.zig:78–280 DIU
    78 /// Encode the mouse event to the writer according to the options.
    79 ///
    80 /// Not all events result in output.
    81 pub fn encode(
    82     writer: *std.Io.Writer,
    83     event: Event,
    84     opts: Options,
    85 ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
    86     if (!shouldReport(event, opts)) return;
    87 
    88     // Handle scenarios where the mouse position is outside the viewport.
    89     // We always report release events no matter where they happen.
    90     if (event.action != .release and
    91         posOutOfViewport(event.pos, opts.size))
    92     {
    93         // If we don't have a motion-tracking event mode, do nothing,
    94         // because events outside the viewport are never reported in
    95         // such cases.
    96         if (!terminal.mouse.eventSendsMotion(opts.event)) return;
    97 
    98         // For motion modes, we only report if a button is currently pressed.
    99         // This lets a TUI detect a click over the surface + drag out
   100         // of the surface.
   101         if (!opts.any_button_pressed) return;
   102     }
   103 
   104     const cell = posToCell(event.pos, opts.size);
   105 
   106     // We only send motion events when the cell changed unless
   107     // we're tracking raw pixels.
   108     if (event.action == .motion and opts.format != .sgr_pixels) {
   109         if (opts.last_cell) |last| {
   110             if (last.*) |last_cell| {
   111                 if (last_cell.eql(cell)) return;
   112             }
   113         }
   114     }
   115 
   116     // Update the last reported cell if we are tracking it.
   117     if (opts.last_cell) |last| last.* = cell;
   118 
   119     const button_code = buttonCode(event, opts) orelse return;
   120     switch (opts.format) {
   121         .x10 => {
   122             if (cell.x > 222 or cell.y > 222) {
   123                 log.info("X10 mouse format can only encode X/Y up to 223", .{});
   124                 return;
   125             }
   126 
   127             // + 1 because our x/y are zero-indexed and the protocol uses 1-indexing.
   128             try writer.writeAll("\x1B[M");
   129             try writer.writeByte(32 + button_code);
   130             try writer.writeByte(32 + @as(u8, @intCast(cell.x)) + 1);
   131             try writer.writeByte(32 + @as(u8, @intCast(cell.y)) + 1);
   132         },
   133 
   134         .utf8 => {
   135             try writer.writeAll("\x1B[M");
   136             try writer.printUnicodeCodepoint(32 + @as(u21, button_code));
   137 
   138             var buf: [4]u8 = undefined;
   139             const x_cp: u21 = @intCast(@as(u32, cell.x) + 33);
   140             const y_cp: u21 = @intCast(cell.y + 33);
   141 
   142             const x_len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(x_cp, &buf) catch unreachable;
   143             try writer.writeAll(buf[0..x_len]);
   144 
   145             const y_len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(y_cp, &buf) catch unreachable;
   146             try writer.writeAll(buf[0..y_len]);
   147         },
   148 
   149         .sgr => try writer.print("\x1B[<{d};{d};{d}{c}", .{
   150             button_code,
   151             cell.x + 1,
   152             cell.y + 1,
   153             @as(u8, if (event.action == .release) 'm' else 'M'),
   154         }),
   155 
   156         .urxvt => try writer.print("\x1B[{d};{d};{d}M", .{
   157             32 + button_code,
   158             cell.x + 1,
   159             cell.y + 1,
   160         }),
   161 
   162         .sgr_pixels => {
   163             const pixels = posToPixels(event.pos, opts.size);
   164             try writer.print("\x1B[<{d};{d};{d}{c}", .{
   165                 button_code,
   166                 pixels.x,
   167                 pixels.y,
   168                 @as(u8, if (event.action == .release) 'm' else 'M'),
   169             });
   170         },
   171     }
   172 }
   173 
   174 /// Returns true if this event should be reported for the given mouse
   175 /// event mode.
   176 fn shouldReport(event: Event, opts: Options) bool {
   177     return switch (opts.event) {
   178         .none => false,
   179 
   180         // X10 only reports button presses of left, middle, and right.
   181         .x10 => event.action == .press and
   182             event.button != null and
   183             (event.button.? == .left or
   184                 event.button.? == .middle or
   185                 event.button.? == .right),
   186 
   187         // Normal mode does not report motion.
   188         .normal => event.action != .motion,
   189 
   190         // Button mode requires an active button for motion events.
   191         .button => event.button != null,
   192 
   193         // Any mode reports everything.
   194         .any => true,
   195     };
   196 }
   197 
   198 fn buttonCode(event: Event, opts: Options) ?u8 {
   199     var acc: u8 = code: {
   200         if (event.button == null) {
   201             // Null button means motion with no pressed button.
   202             break :code 3;
   203         }
   204 
   205         if (event.action == .release and
   206             opts.format != .sgr and
   207             opts.format != .sgr_pixels)
   208         {
   209             // Legacy releases are always encoded as button 3.
   210             break :code 3;
   211         }
   212 
   213         break :code switch (event.button.?) {
   214             .left => 0,
   215             .middle => 1,
   216             .right => 2,
   217             .four => 64,
   218             .five => 65,
   219             .six => 66,
   220             .seven => 67,
   221             .eight => 128,
   222             .nine => 129,
   223             else => return null,
   224         };
   225     };
   226 
   227     // X10 does not include modifiers.
   228     if (opts.event != .x10) {
   229         if (event.mods.shift) acc += 4;
   230         if (event.mods.alt) acc += 8;
   231         if (event.mods.ctrl) acc += 16;
   232     }
   233 
   234     // Motion adds another bit.
   235     if (event.action == .motion) acc += 32;
   236 
   237     return acc;
   238 }
   239 
   240 /// Terminal-space pixel position for SGR pixel reporting.
   241 const PixelPoint = struct {
   242     x: i32,
   243     y: i32,
   244 };
   245 
   246 /// Returns true if the surface-space pixel position is outside the
   247 /// visible viewport bounds (negative or beyond screen dimensions).
   248 fn posOutOfViewport(pos: Event.Pos, size: renderer_size.Size) bool {
   249     const max_x: f32 = @floatFromInt(size.screen.width);
   250     const max_y: f32 = @floatFromInt(size.screen.height);
   251     return pos.x < 0 or pos.y < 0 or pos.x > max_x or pos.y > max_y;
   252 }
   253 
   254 /// Converts a surface-space pixel position to a zero-based grid cell
   255 /// coordinate (column, row) within the terminal viewport. Out-of-bounds
   256 /// values are clamped to the valid grid range (0 to columns/rows - 1).
   257 fn posToCell(pos: Event.Pos, size: renderer_size.Size) point.Coordinate {
   258     const coord: renderer_size.Coordinate = .{ .surface = .{
   259         .x = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.x)),
   260         .y = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.y)),
   261     } };
   262     const grid = coord.convert(.grid, size).grid;
   263     return .{ .x = grid.x, .y = grid.y };
   264 }
   265 
   266 /// Converts a surface-space pixel position to terminal-space pixel
   267 /// coordinates (accounting for padding/scaling) used by SGR-Pixels mode.
   268 /// Unlike grid conversion, terminal-space coordinates are not clamped
   269 /// and may be negative or exceed the terminal dimensions.
   270 fn posToPixels(pos: Event.Pos, size: renderer_size.Size) PixelPoint {
   271     const coord: renderer_size.Coordinate.Terminal = (renderer_size.Coordinate{ .surface = .{
   272         .x = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.x)),
   273         .y = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.y)),
   274     } }).convert(.terminal, size).terminal;
   275 
   276     return .{
   277         .x = @as(i32, @intFromFloat(@round(coord.x))),
   278         .y = @as(i32, @intFromFloat(@round(coord.y))),
   279     };
   280 }

n30 src/renderer/size.zig:114–151 DIU
   114     /// Convert a coordinate to a different space within the same Size.
   115     pub fn convert(self: Coordinate, to: Tag, size: Size) Coordinate {
   116         // Unlikely fast-path but avoid work.
   117         if (@as(Tag, self) == to) return self;
   118 
   119         // To avoid the combinatorial explosion of conversion functions, we
   120         // convert to the surface system first and then reconvert from there.
   121         const surface = self.convertToSurface(size);
   122 
   123         return switch (to) {
   124             .surface => .{ .surface = surface },
   125             .terminal => .{ .terminal = .{
   126                 .x = surface.x - @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.padding.left)),
   127                 .y = surface.y - @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.padding.top)),
   128             } },
   129             .grid => grid: {
   130                 // Get rid of the padding.
   131                 const term = (Coordinate{ .surface = surface }).convert(
   132                     .terminal,
   133                     size,
   134                 ).terminal;
   135 
   136                 // We need our grid to clamp
   137                 const grid = size.grid();
   138 
   139                 // Calculate the grid position.
   140                 const cell_width: f64 = @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.cell.width));
   141                 const cell_height: f64 = @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.cell.height));
   142                 const clamped_x: f64 = @max(0, term.x);
   143                 const clamped_y: f64 = @max(0, term.y);
   144                 const col: GridSize.Unit = @intFromFloat(clamped_x / cell_width);
   145                 const row: GridSize.Unit = @intFromFloat(clamped_y / cell_height);
   146                 const clamped_col: GridSize.Unit = @min(col, grid.columns - 1);
   147                 const clamped_row: GridSize.Unit = @min(row, grid.rows - 1);
   148                 break :grid .{ .grid = .{ .x = clamped_col, .y = clamped_row } };
   149             },
   150         };
   151     }

n30 src/renderer/size.zig:154–174 DI
   154     fn convertToSurface(self: Coordinate, size: Size) Surface {
   155         return switch (self) {
   156             .surface => |v| v,
   157             .terminal => |v| .{
   158                 .x = v.x + @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.padding.left)),
   159                 .y = v.y + @as(f64, @floatFromInt(size.padding.top)),
   160             },
   161             .grid => |v| grid: {
   162                 const col: f64 = @floatFromInt(v.x);
   163                 const row: f64 = @floatFromInt(v.y);
   164                 const cell_width: f64 = @floatFromInt(size.cell.width);
   165                 const cell_height: f64 = @floatFromInt(size.cell.height);
   166                 const padding_left: f64 = @floatFromInt(size.padding.left);
   167                 const padding_top: f64 = @floatFromInt(size.padding.top);
   168                 break :grid .{
   169                     .x = col * cell_width + padding_left,
   170                     .y = row * cell_height + padding_top,
   171                 };
   172             },
   173         };
   174     }

n31 macos/Sources/Ghostty/Ghostty.App.swift:298–345 DIU
   298         static func readClipboard(
   299             _ userdata: UnsafeMutableRawPointer?,
   300             location: ghostty_clipboard_e,
   301             state: UnsafeMutableRawPointer,
   302             mimes: UnsafePointer<UnsafePointer<CChar>>,
   303             mimesLen: Int,
   304             list: Bool
   305         ) -> ghostty_clipboard_read_result_e {
   306             let surfaceView = self.surfaceUserdata(from: userdata)
   307             guard let surface = surfaceView.surface else {
   308                 return GHOSTTY_CLIPBOARD_READ_UNSUPPORTED
   309             }
   310 
   311             // Get our pasteboard
   312             guard let pasteboard = NSPasteboard.ghostty(location) else {
   313                 return GHOSTTY_CLIPBOARD_READ_UNSUPPORTED
   314             }
   315 
   316             // Gather the representation for each requested MIME type that
   317             // the pasteboard can serve. We only ever read the requested
   318             // representations so unrelated (potentially large) clipboard
   319             // contents are never loaded.
   320             var contents: [Ghostty.ClipboardContent] = []
   321             var seen = Set<String>()
   322             for i in 0..<mimesLen {
   323                 let mime = String(cString: mimes[i])
   324                 guard !seen.contains(mime) else { continue }
   325                 seen.insert(mime)
   326                 guard let data = pasteboard.ghosttyData(forMime: mime) else { continue }
   327                 contents.append(.init(mime: mime, data: data))
   328             }
   329 
   330             // The listing of available types, only gathered when requested.
   331             let available: [String] = list ? pasteboard.ghosttyAvailableMimes() : []
   332 
   333             // With nothing to serve and no listing requested there is
   334             // nothing to complete the read with.
   335             if contents.isEmpty && !list {
   336                 return GHOSTTY_CLIPBOARD_READ_UNAVAILABLE
   337             }
   338 
   339             completeClipboardRequest(
   340                 surface,
   341                 contents: contents,
   342                 available: available,
   343                 state: state)
   344             return GHOSTTY_CLIPBOARD_READ_STARTED
   345         }

n31 macos/Sources/Ghostty/Ghostty.App.swift:415–469 DIU
   415         private static func completeClipboardRequest(
   416             _ surface: ghostty_surface_t,
   417             contents: [Ghostty.ClipboardContent],
   418             available: [String],
   419             state: UnsafeMutableRawPointer,
   420             confirmed: Bool = false,
   421             remember: Bool = false
   422         ) {
   423             // Copy everything into C memory for the duration of the call.
   424             var cStrings: [UnsafeMutablePointer<CChar>] = []
   425             var cDatas: [UnsafeMutableRawPointer] = []
   426             defer {
   427                 cStrings.forEach { free($0) }
   428                 cDatas.forEach { $0.deallocate() }
   429             }
   430 
   431             var cContents: [ghostty_clipboard_content_s] = []
   432             for entry in contents {
   433                 guard let mime = strdup(entry.mime) else { continue }
   434                 cStrings.append(mime)
   435                 let buf = UnsafeMutableRawPointer.allocate(
   436                     byteCount: max(entry.data.count, 1),
   437                     alignment: 1)
   438                 cDatas.append(buf)
   439                 entry.data.withUnsafeBytes { src in
   440                     if let base = src.baseAddress {
   441                         buf.copyMemory(from: base, byteCount: src.count)
   442                     }
   443                 }
   444                 cContents.append(ghostty_clipboard_content_s(
   445                     mime: mime,
   446                     data: buf.assumingMemoryBound(to: CChar.self),
   447                     len: entry.data.count))
   448             }
   449 
   450             var cAvailable: [UnsafePointer<CChar>] = []
   451             for mime in available {
   452                 guard let str = strdup(mime) else { continue }
   453                 cStrings.append(str)
   454                 cAvailable.append(UnsafePointer(str))
   455             }
   456 
   457             cContents.withUnsafeBufferPointer { contentsBuf in
   458                 cAvailable.withUnsafeBufferPointer { availableBuf in
   459                     var complete = ghostty_clipboard_complete_s(
   460                         contents: contentsBuf.baseAddress,
   461                         contents_len: contentsBuf.count,
   462                         available: availableBuf.baseAddress,
   463                         available_len: availableBuf.count,
   464                         confirmed: confirmed,
   465                         remember: remember)
   466                     ghostty_surface_complete_clipboard_request(surface, &complete, state)
   467                 }
   468             }
   469         }

n31 src/Surface.zig:6219–6262 DIU
  6219 fn startClipboardRequest(
  6220     self: *Surface,
  6221     loc: apprt.Clipboard,
  6222     req: apprt.ClipboardRequest,
  6223 ) !apprt.ClipboardReadResult {
  6224     const effective_req: apprt.ClipboardRequest = switch (req) {
  6225         .paste => |clipboard| effective: {
  6226             // Snapshot the mode before asking the apprt for clipboard data.
  6227             // Event pastes request only a MIME listing, while ordinary
  6228             // pastes request the text representation as before.
  6229             self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  6230             const event = self.io.terminal.modes.get(.kitty_paste_events);
  6231             self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  6232 
  6233             break :effective if (event)
  6234                 .{ .list = clipboard }
  6235             else
  6236                 req;
  6237         },
  6238         else => req,
  6239     };
  6240 
  6241     switch (effective_req) {
  6242         .paste, .list => {}, // always allowed
  6243         .osc_52_read => if (self.config.clipboard_read == .deny) {
  6244             log.info(
  6245                 "application attempted to read clipboard, but 'clipboard-read' is set to deny",
  6246                 .{},
  6247             );
  6248             return .unsupported;
  6249         },
  6250 
  6251         // The clipboard access policies were already applied by
  6252         // kittyClipboardRead and kittyClipboardWrite, which own
  6253         // replying on denial.
  6254         .kitty_read, .kitty_write => {},
  6255 
  6256         // OSC 52 writes don't travel through this function; they go
  6257         // straight to the apprt setClipboard API.
  6258         .osc_52_write => unreachable,
  6259     }
  6260 
  6261     return try self.rt_surface.clipboardRequest(loc, effective_req);
  6262 }

n31 src/Surface.zig:6264–6341 DIU
  6264 fn completeClipboardPaste(
  6265     self: *Surface,
  6266     data: []const u8,
  6267     allow_unsafe: bool,
  6268 ) !void {
  6269     if (data.len == 0) return;
  6270 
  6271     const encode_opts: input.paste.Options = encode_opts: {
  6272         self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
  6273         defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
  6274         const opts: input.paste.Options = .fromTerminal(&self.io.terminal);
  6275 
  6276         // If we have paste protection enabled, we detect unsafe pastes and return
  6277         // an error. The error approach allows apprt to attempt to complete the paste
  6278         // before falling back to requesting confirmation.
  6279         //
  6280         // We do not do this for bracketed pastes because bracketed pastes are
  6281         // by definition safe since they're framed.
  6282         const unsafe = unsafe: {
  6283             // If we've disabled paste protection then we always allow the paste.
  6284             if (!self.config.clipboard_paste_protection) break :unsafe false;
  6285 
  6286             // If we're allowed to paste unsafe data then we always allow the paste.
  6287             // This is set during confirmation usually.
  6288             if (allow_unsafe) break :unsafe false;
  6289 
  6290             if (opts.bracketed) {
  6291                 // If we're bracketed and the paste contains and ending
  6292                 // bracket then something naughty might be going on and we
  6293                 // never trust it.
  6294                 if (std.mem.indexOf(u8, data, "\x1B[201~") != null) break :unsafe true;
  6295 
  6296                 // If we are bracketed and configured to trust that then the
  6297                 // paste is not unsafe.
  6298                 if (self.config.clipboard_paste_bracketed_safe) break :unsafe false;
  6299             }
  6300 
  6301             break :unsafe !input.paste.isSafe(data);
  6302         };
  6303 
  6304         if (unsafe) {
  6305             log.info("potentially unsafe paste detected, rejecting until confirmation", .{});
  6306             return error.UnsafePaste;
  6307         }
  6308 
  6309         // With the lock held, we must scroll to the bottom.
  6310         // We always scroll to the bottom for these inputs.
  6311         self.scrollToBottom() catch |err| {
  6312             log.warn("error scrolling to bottom err={}", .{err});
  6313         };
  6314 
  6315         break :encode_opts opts;
  6316     };
  6317 
  6318     // Encode the data. In most cases this doesn't require any
  6319     // copies, so we optimize for that case.
  6320     var data_duped: ?[]u8 = null;
  6321     const vecs = input.paste.encode(data, encode_opts) catch |err| switch (err) {
  6322         error.MutableRequired => vecs: {
  6323             const buf: []u8 = try self.alloc.dupe(u8, data);
  6324             errdefer self.alloc.free(buf);
  6325             data_duped = buf;
  6326             break :vecs input.paste.encode(buf, encode_opts);
  6327         },
  6328     };
  6329     defer if (data_duped) |v| {
  6330         // This code path means the data did require a copy and mutation.
  6331         // We must free it.
  6332         self.alloc.free(v);
  6333     };
  6334 
  6335     for (vecs) |vec| if (vec.len > 0) {
  6336         self.queueIo(try termio.Message.writeReq(
  6337             self.alloc,
  6338             vec,
  6339         ), .unlocked);
  6340     };
  6341 }

   715     pub fn clipboardRequest(
   716         self: *Surface,
   717         clipboard_type: apprt.Clipboard,
   718         state: apprt.ClipboardRequest,
   719     ) !apprt.ClipboardReadResult {

n31 src/apprt/embedded.zig:742–801 DIU
   742         // The representations the read wants served. Text-only
   743         // requesters ask for the canonical text type; Kitty clipboard
   744         // reads ask for exactly what the program requested, with
   745         // text-like aliases normalized so the embedder never has to
   746         // know about them.
   747         var mimes_buf: [terminal.kitty.clipboard.max_read_mimes][*:0]const u8 = undefined;
   748         const mimes: []const [*:0]const u8 = switch (state) {
   749             .paste, .osc_52_read => &.{"text/plain"},
   750 
   751             // A mode 5522 paste event only lists types and must not read
   752             // any clipboard data.
   753             .list => &.{},
   754 
   755             .kitty_read => |kitty| mimes: {
   756                 assert(kitty.mimes.len <= mimes_buf.len);
   757                 for (kitty.mimes, mimes_buf[0..kitty.mimes.len]) |mime, *dst| {
   758                     dst.* = if (terminal.clipboard.isTextMime(mime))
   759                         "text/plain"
   760                     else
   761                         mime.ptr;
   762                 }
   763                 break :mimes mimes_buf[0..kitty.mimes.len];
   764             },
   765 
   766             // Handled above.
   767             .kitty_write => unreachable,
   768 
   769             // No clipboard write code paths travel through this function
   770             .osc_52_write => unreachable,
   771         };
   772         const list = switch (state) {
   773             // Paste events need the full MIME listing without reading any
   774             // representation. Kitty reads only ask for it when requested.
   775             .list => true,
   776             .kitty_read => |kitty| kitty.list,
   777             else => false,
   778         };
   779 
   780         // We need to allocate to get a pointer to store our clipboard request
   781         // so that it is stable until the read_clipboard callback and call
   782         // complete_clipboard_request. This sucks but clipboard requests aren't
   783         // high throughput so it's probably fine.
   784         const alloc = self.app.core_app.alloc;
   785         const state_ptr = try alloc.create(apprt.ClipboardRequest);
   786         errdefer alloc.destroy(state_ptr);
   787         state_ptr.* = state;
   788 
   789         const result = self.app.opts.read_clipboard(
   790             self.userdata,
   791             @intCast(@backingInt(clipboard_type)),
   792             state_ptr,
   793             mimes.ptr,
   794             mimes.len,
   795             list,
   796         );
   797 
   798         // Only a started request completes later and keeps the state.
   799         if (result != .started) alloc.destroy(state_ptr);
   800         return result;
   801     }

  4901                 );
  4902                 return;
  4903             },
  4904

  4906                 log.warn(
  4907                     "failed to complete clipboard request err={}",

  4912         };

n31 src/datastruct/message_data.zig:38–74 DI
    38         /// Initializes the union for a given data type. This will
    39         /// attempt to fit into a small value if possible, otherwise
    40         /// will allocate and put into alloc.
    41         ///
    42         /// This can't and will never detect stable pointers.
    43         pub fn init(alloc: Allocator, data: anytype) !Self {
    44             switch (@typeInfo(@TypeOf(data))) {
    45                 .pointer => |info| {
    46                     assert(info.size == .slice);
    47                     assert(info.child == Elem);
    48 
    49                     // If it fits in our small request, do that.
    50                     if (data.len <= Small.Max) {
    51                         var buf: Small.Array = undefined;
    52                         @memcpy(buf[0..data.len], data);
    53                         return Self{
    54                             .small = .{
    55                                 .data = buf,
    56                                 .len = @intCast(data.len),
    57                             },
    58                         };
    59                     }
    60 
    61                     // Otherwise, allocate
    62                     const buf = try alloc.dupe(Elem, data);
    63                     errdefer alloc.free(buf);
    64                     return Self{
    65                         .alloc = .{
    66                             .alloc = alloc,
    67                             .data = buf,
    68                         },
    69                     };
    70                 },
    71 
    72                 else => unreachable,
    73             }
    74         }

n31 src/input/paste.zig:24–125 DI
    24 /// Encode the given data for pasting. The resulting value can be written
    25 /// to the pty to perform a paste of the input data.
    26 ///
    27 /// The data can be either a `[]u8` or a `[]const u8`. If the data
    28 /// type is const then `EncodeError` may be returned. If the data type
    29 /// is mutable then this function can't return an error.
    30 ///
    31 /// This slightly complex calling style allows for initially const
    32 /// data to be passed in without an allocation, since it is rare in normal
    33 /// use cases that the data will need to be modified. In the unlikely case
    34 /// data does need to be modified, the caller can make a mutable copy
    35 /// after seeing the error.
    36 ///
    37 /// The data is returned as a set of slices to limit allocations. The caller
    38 /// can combine the slices into a single buffer if desired.
    39 ///
    40 /// WARNING: The input data is not checked for safety. See the `isSafe`
    41 /// function to check if the data is safe to paste.
    42 pub fn encode(
    43     data: anytype,
    44     opts: Options,
    45 ) switch (@TypeOf(data)) {
    46     []u8 => [3][]const u8,
    47     []const u8 => Error![3][]const u8,
    48     else => unreachable,
    49 } {
    50     // These are the set of byte values that are always replaced by
    51     // a space (per xterm's behavior) for any text insertion method e.g.
    52     // a paste, drag and drop, etc. These are copied directly from xterm's
    53     // source.
    54     const strip: []const u8 = &.{
    55         0x00, // NUL
    56         0x08, // BS
    57         0x05, // ENQ
    58         0x04, // EOT
    59         0x1B, // ESC
    60         0x7F, // DEL
    61 
    62         // These can be overridden by the running terminal program
    63         // via tcsetattr, so they aren't totally safe to hardcode like
    64         // this. In practice, I haven't seen modern programs change these
    65         // and its a much bigger architectural change to pass these through
    66         // so for now they're hardcoded.
    67         0x03, // VINTR (Ctrl+C)
    68         0x1C, // VQUIT (Ctrl+\)
    69         0x15, // VKILL (Ctrl+U)
    70         0x1A, // VSUSP (Ctrl+Z)
    71         0x11, // VSTART (Ctrl+Q)
    72         0x13, // VSTOP (Ctrl+S)
    73         0x17, // VWERASE (Ctrl+W)
    74         0x16, // VLNEXT (Ctrl+V)
    75         0x12, // VREPRINT (Ctrl+R)
    76         0x0F, // VDISCARD (Ctrl+O)
    77     };
    78 
    79     const mutable = @TypeOf(data) == []u8;
    80 
    81     var result: [3][]const u8 = .{ "", data, "" };
    82 
    83     // If we have any of the strip values, then we need to replace them
    84     // with spaces. This is what xterm does and it does it regardless
    85     // of bracketed paste mode. This is a security measure to prevent pastes
    86     // from containing bytes that could be used to inject commands.
    87     if (std.mem.indexOfAny(u8, data, strip) != null) {
    88         if (comptime !mutable) return Error.MutableRequired;
    89         var offset: usize = 0;
    90         while (std.mem.indexOfAny(
    91             u8,
    92             data[offset..],
    93             strip,
    94         )) |idx| {
    95             offset += idx;
    96             data[offset] = ' ';
    97             offset += 1;
    98         }
    99     }
   100 
   101     // Bracketed paste mode (mode 2004) wraps pasted data in
   102     // fenceposts so that the terminal can ignore things like newlines.
   103     if (opts.bracketed) {
   104         result[0] = bracketed_prefix;
   105         result[2] = bracketed_suffix;
   106         return result;
   107     }
   108 
   109     // Non-bracketed. We have to replace newline with `\r`. This matches
   110     // the behavior of xterm and other terminals. For `\r\n` this will
   111     // result in `\r\r` which does match xterm.
   112     if (comptime mutable) {
   113         std.mem.replaceScalar(u8, data, '\n', '\r');
   114     } else if (std.mem.indexOfScalar(u8, data, '\n') != null) {
   115         return Error.MutableRequired;
   116     }
   117 
   118     return result;
   119 }
   120 
   121 pub const Error = error{
   122     /// Returned if encoding requires a mutable copy of the data. This
   123     /// can only be returned if the input data type is const.
   124     MutableRequired,
   125 };

n31 src/input/paste.zig:180–183 DI
   180 pub fn isSafe(data: []const u8) bool {
   181     return std.mem.indexOfAny(u8, data, "\r\n") == null and
   182         std.mem.indexOf(u8, data, "\x1b[201~") == null;
   183 }

   107     /// Return a write request for the given data. This will use
   108     /// write_small if it fits or write_alloc otherwise. This should NOT
   109     /// be used for stable pointers which can be manually set to write_stable.
   110     pub fn writeReq(alloc: Allocator, data: anytype) !Message {
   111         return switch (try WriteReq.init(alloc, data)) {
   112             .stable => unreachable,
   113             .small => |v| Message{ .write_small = v },
   114             .alloc => |v| Message{ .write_alloc = v },
   115         };

   895 /// Queue a message for the IO thread, taking ownership of `msg`.
   896 ///
   897 /// We centralize all our logic into this spot so we can intercept
   898 /// messages for example in readonly mode.
   899 fn queueIo(
   900     self: *Surface,
   901     msg: termio.Message,
   902     mutex: termio.Termio.MutexState,
   903 ) void {
   904     // In readonly mode, we don't allow any writes through to the pty.
   905     if (self.readonly) {
   906         switch (msg) {
   907             .write_small,
   908             .write_stable,
   909             .write_alloc,
   910             => {
   911                 msg.deinit();
   912                 return;
   913             },
   914 
   915             else => {},
   916         }
   917     }
   918 
   919     self.io.queueMessage(msg, mutex);
   920 }

n32 src/datastruct/blocking_queue.zig:99–168 DI
    99         /// Push a value to the queue. This returns the total size of the
   100         /// queue (unread items) after the push. A return value of zero
   101         /// means that the push failed.
   102         pub fn push(self: *Self, io: std.Io, value: T, timeout: Timeout) Size {
   103             self.mutex.lockUncancelable(io);
   104             defer self.mutex.unlock(io);
   105 
   106             // The
   107             if (self.full()) {
   108                 switch (timeout) {
   109                     // If we're not waiting, then we failed to write.
   110                     .instant => return 0,
   111 
   112                     .forever => {
   113                         self.not_full_waiters += 1;
   114                         defer self.not_full_waiters -= 1;
   115                         self.cond_not_full.waitUncancelable(io, &self.mutex);
   116                     },
   117 
   118                     .ns => |ns| {
   119                         self.not_full_waiters += 1;
   120                         defer self.not_full_waiters -= 1;
   121                         compat_thread.waitTimeout(
   122                             &self.cond_not_full,
   123                             io,
   124                             &self.mutex,
   125                             .{
   126                                 .duration = .{
   127                                     .raw = .fromNanoseconds(ns),
   128                                     .clock = .awake,
   129                                 },
   130                             },
   131                         ) catch return 0;
   132                     },
   133                 }
   134 
   135                 // If we're still full, then we failed to write. This can
   136                 // happen in situations where we are interrupted.
   137                 if (self.full()) return 0;
   138             }
   139 
   140             // Add our data and update our accounting
   141             self.data[self.write] = value;
   142             self.write += 1;
   143             if (self.write >= bounds) self.write -= bounds;
   144             self.len += 1;
   145 
   146             return self.len;
   147         }
   148 
   149         /// Pop a value from the queue without blocking.
   150         pub fn pop(self: *Self, io: std.Io) ?T {
   151             self.mutex.lockUncancelable(io);
   152             defer self.mutex.unlock(io);
   153 
   154             // If we're empty we have nothing
   155             if (self.len == 0) return null;
   156 
   157             // Get the index we're going to read data from and do some
   158             // accounting. We don't copy the value here to avoid copying twice.
   159             const n = self.read;
   160             self.read += 1;
   161             if (self.read >= bounds) self.read -= bounds;
   162             self.len -= 1;
   163 
   164             // If we have consumers waiting on a full queue, notify.
   165             if (self.not_full_waiters > 0) self.cond_not_full.signal(io);
   166 
   167             return self.data[n];
   168         }

n32 src/termio/Exec.zig:86–104 DIU
    86 pub fn threadEnter(
    87     self: *Exec,
    88     alloc: Allocator,
    89     io: *termio.Termio,
    90     td: *termio.Termio.ThreadData,
    91 ) !void {
    92     // Start our subprocess
    93     const pty_fds = self.subprocess.start(alloc) catch |err| {
    94         // If we specifically got this error then we are in the forked
    95         // process and our child failed to execute. If we DIDN'T
    96         // get this specific error then we're in the parent and
    97         // we need to bubble it up.
    98         if (err != error.ExecFailedInChild) return err;
    99 
   100         // We're in the child. Nothing more we can do but abnormal exit.
   101         // The Command will output some additional information.
   102         std.process.exit(1);
   103     };
   104     errdefer self.subprocess.stop();

n32 src/termio/Exec.zig:403–490 DIU
   403 pub fn queueWrite(
   404     self: *Exec,
   405     alloc: Allocator,
   406     td: *termio.Termio.ThreadData,
   407     data: []const u8,
   408     linefeed: bool,
   409 ) !void {
   410     _ = self;
   411     const exec = &td.backend.exec;
   412 
   413     // If our process is exited then we don't send any more writes.
   414     if (exec.exited) return;
   415 
   416     // We go through and chunk the data if necessary to fit into
   417     // our cached buffers that we can queue to the stream.
   418     var i: usize = 0;
   419     while (i < data.len) {
   420         const w = try exec.write_pool.create(alloc);
   421         w.td = exec;
   422         const buf = &w.buf;
   423         const slice = slice: {
   424             // The maximum end index is either the end of our data or
   425             // the end of our buffer, whichever is smaller.
   426             const max = @min(data.len, i + buf.len);
   427 
   428             // Fast
   429             if (!linefeed) {
   430                 fastmem.copy(u8, buf, data[i..max]);
   431                 const len = max - i;
   432                 i = max;
   433                 break :slice buf[0..len];
   434             }
   435 
   436             // Slow, have to replace \r with \r\n
   437             var buf_i: usize = 0;
   438             while (i < data.len and buf_i < buf.len - 1) {
   439                 const ch = data[i];
   440                 i += 1;
   441 
   442                 if (ch != '\r') {
   443                     buf[buf_i] = ch;
   444                     buf_i += 1;
   445                     continue;
   446                 }
   447 
   448                 // CRLF
   449                 buf[buf_i] = '\r';
   450                 buf[buf_i + 1] = '\n';
   451                 buf_i += 2;
   452             }
   453 
   454             break :slice buf[0..buf_i];
   455         };
   456 
   457         //for (slice) |b| log.warn("write: {x}", .{b});
   458 
   459         exec.write_stream.queueWrite(
   460             td.loop,
   461             &exec.write_queue,
   462             &w.req,
   463             .{ .slice = slice },
   464             ThreadData.Write,
   465             w,
   466             ttyWrite,
   467         );
   468     }
   469 }
   470 
   471 fn ttyWrite(
   472     w_: ?*ThreadData.Write,
   473     _: *xev.Loop,
   474     _: *xev.Completion,
   475     _: xev.Stream,
   476     _: xev.WriteBuffer,
   477     r: xev.WriteError!usize,
   478 ) xev.CallbackAction {
   479     const w = w_.?;
   480     w.td.write_pool.destroy(w);
   481 
   482     const d = r catch |err| {
   483         log.err("write error: {}", .{err});
   484         return .disarm;
   485     };
   486     _ = d;
   487     //log.info("WROTE: {d}", .{d});
   488 
   489     return .disarm;
   490 }

n32 src/termio/Termio.zig:431–447 DIU
   431 /// Send a message to the mailbox. Depending on the mailbox type in use
   432 /// this may process now or it may just enqueue and process later.
   433 ///
   434 /// This will also notify the mailbox thread to process the message. If
   435 /// you're sending a lot of messages, it may be more efficient to use
   436 /// the mailbox directly and then call notify separately.
   437 pub fn queueMessage(
   438     self: *Termio,
   439     msg: termio.Message,
   440     mutex: MutexState,
   441 ) void {
   442     self.mailbox.send(msg, switch (mutex) {
   443         .locked => self.renderer_state.mutex,
   444         .unlocked => null,
   445     });
   446     self.mailbox.notify();
   447 }

n32 src/termio/Termio.zig:449–463 DIU
   449 /// Queue a write directly to the pty.
   450 ///
   451 /// If you're using termio.Thread, this must ONLY be called from the
   452 /// mailbox thread. If you're not on the thread, use queueMessage with
   453 /// mailbox messages instead.
   454 ///
   455 /// If you're not using termio.Thread, this is not threadsafe.
   456 pub inline fn queueWrite(
   457     self: *Termio,
   458     td: *ThreadData,
   459     data: []const u8,
   460     linefeed: bool,
   461 ) !void {
   462     try self.backend.queueWrite(self.alloc, td, data, linefeed);
   463 }

n32 src/termio/Thread.zig:236–236 D
   236 fn threadMain_(self: *Thread, io: *termio.Termio) !void {

n32 src/termio/Thread.zig:253–278 IU
   253     // Get the mailbox. This must be an SPSC mailbox for threading.
   254     const mailbox = switch (io.mailbox) {
   255         .spsc => |*v| v,
   256         // else => return error.TermioUnsupportedMailbox,
   257     };
   258 
   259     // This is the data sent to xev callbacks. We want a pointer to both
   260     // ourselves and the thread data so we can thread that through (pun intended).
   261     var cb: CallbackData = .{ .self = self, .io = io };
   262 
   263     // Run our thread start/end callbacks. This allows the implementation
   264     // to hook into the event loop as needed. The thread data is created
   265     // on the stack here so that it has a stable pointer throughout the
   266     // lifetime of the thread.
   267     try io.threadEnter(self, &cb.data);
   268     defer cb.data.deinit();
   269     defer io.threadExit(&cb.data);
   270 
   271     // Start the async handlers.
   272     mailbox.wakeup.wait(&self.loop, &self.wakeup_c, CallbackData, &cb, wakeupCallback);
   273     self.stop.wait(&self.loop, &self.stop_c, CallbackData, &cb, stopCallback);
   274 
   275     // Run
   276     log.debug("starting IO thread", .{});
   277     defer log.debug("starting IO thread shutdown", .{});
   278     try self.loop.run(.until_done);

n32 src/termio/Thread.zig:289–375 DIU
   289 fn drainMailbox(
   290     self: *Thread,
   291     cb: *CallbackData,
   292 ) !void {
   293     // We assert when starting the thread that this is the state
   294     const mailbox = cb.io.mailbox.spsc.queue;
   295     const io = cb.io;
   296     const data = &cb.data;
   297 
   298     // If we're draining, we just drain the mailbox and return.
   299     if (self.flags.drain) {
   300         while (mailbox.pop(global.io())) |msg| msg.deinit();
   301         return;
   302     }
   303 
   304     // This holds the mailbox lock for the duration of the drain. The
   305     // expectation is that all our message handlers will be non-blocking
   306     // ENOUGH to not mess up throughput on producers.
   307     var redraw: bool = false;
   308     while (mailbox.pop(global.io())) |message| {
   309         // If we have a message we always redraw
   310         redraw = true;
   311 
   312         log.debug("mailbox message={s}", .{@tagName(message)});
   313         switch (message) {
   314             .color_scheme_report => |v| try io.colorSchemeReport(data, v.force),
   315             .visibility_report => |v| try io.visibilityReport(
   316                 data,
   317                 v.visible,
   318                 v.force,
   319             ),
   320             .crash => @panic("crash request, crashing intentionally"),
   321             .change_config => |config| {
   322                 defer config.alloc.destroy(config.ptr);
   323                 try io.changeConfig(data, config.ptr);
   324             },
   325             .inspector => |v| self.flags.has_inspector = v,
   326             .resize => |v| self.handleResize(cb, v),
   327             .size_report => |v| try io.sizeReport(data, v),
   328             .clear_screen => |v| try io.clearScreen(data, v.history),
   329             .scroll_viewport => |v| io.scrollViewport(v),
   330             .selection_scroll => |v| {
   331                 if (v) {
   332                     self.startScrollTimer(cb);
   333                 } else {
   334                     self.stopScrollTimer();
   335                 }
   336             },
   337             .jump_to_prompt => |v| try io.jumpToPrompt(v),
   338             .kitty_clipboard_grant_read => |v| {
   339                 defer v.alloc.free(v.pw);
   340                 try io.kittyClipboardGrant(v.pw, .read);
   341             },
   342             .kitty_clipboard_grant_write => |v| {
   343                 defer v.alloc.free(v.pw);
   344                 try io.kittyClipboardGrant(v.pw, .write);
   345             },
   346             .start_synchronized_output => self.startSynchronizedOutput(cb),
   347             .linefeed_mode => |v| self.flags.linefeed_mode = v,
   348             .focused => |v| try io.focusGained(data, v),
   349             .write_small => |v| try io.queueWrite(
   350                 data,
   351                 v.data[0..v.len],
   352                 self.flags.linefeed_mode,
   353             ),
   354             .write_stable => |v| try io.queueWrite(
   355                 data,
   356                 v,
   357                 self.flags.linefeed_mode,
   358             ),
   359             .write_alloc => |v| {
   360                 defer v.alloc.free(v.data);
   361                 try io.queueWrite(
   362                     data,
   363                     v.data,
   364                     self.flags.linefeed_mode,
   365                 );
   366             },
   367         }
   368     }
   369 
   370     // Trigger a redraw after we've drained so we don't waste cyces
   371     // messaging a redraw.
   372     if (redraw) {
   373         try io.renderer_wakeup.notify();
   374     }
   375 }

n32 src/termio/Thread.zig:453–471 DIU
   453 fn wakeupCallback(
   454     cb_: ?*CallbackData,
   455     _: *xev.Loop,
   456     _: *xev.Completion,
   457     r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
   458 ) xev.CallbackAction {
   459     _ = r catch |err| {
   460         log.err("error in wakeup err={}", .{err});
   461         return .rearm;
   462     };
   463 
   464     // When we wake up, we check the mailbox. Mailbox producers should
   465     // wake up our thread after publishing.
   466     const cb = cb_ orelse return .rearm;
   467     cb.self.drainMailbox(cb) catch |err|
   468         log.err("error draining mailbox err={}", .{err});
   469 
   470     return .rearm;
   471 }

n32 src/termio/mailbox.zig:11–109 DIU
    11 /// A queue used for storing messages that is periodically drained.
    12 /// Typically used by a multi-threaded application. The capacity is
    13 /// hardcoded to a value that empirically has made sense for Ghostty usage
    14 /// but I'm open to changing it with good arguments.
    15 const Queue = BlockingQueue(termio.Message, 64);
    16 
    17 /// The location to where write-related messages are sent.
    18 pub const Mailbox = union(enum) {
    19     // /// Write messages to an unbounded list backed by an allocator.
    20     // /// This is useful for single-threaded applications where you're not
    21     // /// afraid of running out of memory. You should be careful that you're
    22     // /// processing this in a timely manner though since some heavy workloads
    23     // /// will produce a LOT of messages.
    24     // ///
    25     // /// At the time of authoring this, the primary use case for this is
    26     // /// testing more than anything, but it probably will have a use case
    27     // /// in libghostty eventually.
    28     // unbounded: std.ArrayList(termio.Message),
    29 
    30     /// Write messages to a SPSC queue for multi-threaded applications.
    31     spsc: struct {
    32         queue: *Queue,
    33         wakeup: xev.Async,
    34     },
    35 
    36     /// Init the SPSC writer.
    37     pub fn initSPSC(alloc: Allocator) !Mailbox {
    38         var queue = try Queue.create(alloc);
    39         errdefer queue.destroy(alloc);
    40 
    41         var wakeup = try xev.Async.init();
    42         errdefer wakeup.deinit();
    43 
    44         return .{ .spsc = .{ .queue = queue, .wakeup = wakeup } };
    45     }
    46 
    47     pub fn deinit(self: *Mailbox, alloc: Allocator) void {
    48         switch (self.*) {
    49             .spsc => |*v| {
    50                 while (v.queue.pop(global.io())) |msg| msg.deinit();
    51                 v.queue.destroy(alloc);
    52                 v.wakeup.deinit();
    53             },
    54         }
    55     }
    56 
    57     /// Sends the given message without notifying there are messages.
    58     ///
    59     /// If the optional mutex is given, it must already be LOCKED. If the
    60     /// send would block, we'll unlock this mutex, resend the message, and
    61     /// lock it again. This handles an edge case where queues are full.
    62     /// This may not apply to all writer types.
    63     pub fn send(
    64         self: *Mailbox,
    65         msg: termio.Message,
    66         mutex: ?*std.Io.Mutex,
    67     ) void {
    68         switch (self.*) {
    69             .spsc => |*mb| send: {
    70                 // Try to write to the queue with an instant timeout. This is the
    71                 // fast path because we can queue without a lock.
    72                 if (mb.queue.push(global.io(), msg, .{ .instant = {} }) > 0) break :send;
    73 
    74                 // If we enter this conditional, the queue is full. We wake up
    75                 // the writer thread so that it can process messages to clear up
    76                 // space. However, the writer thread may require the renderer
    77                 // lock so we need to unlock.
    78                 mb.wakeup.notify() catch |err| {
    79                     log.warn("failed to wake up writer, data will be dropped err={}", .{err});
    80                     msg.deinit();
    81                     return;
    82                 };
    83 
    84                 // Unlock the renderer state so the writer thread can acquire it.
    85                 // Then try to queue our message before continuing. This is a very
    86                 // slow path because we are having a lot of contention for data.
    87                 // But this only gets triggered in certain pathological cases.
    88                 //
    89                 // Note that writes themselves don't require a lock, but there
    90                 // are other messages in the writer queue (resize, focus) that
    91                 // could acquire the lock. This is why we have to release our lock
    92                 // here.
    93                 if (mutex) |m| m.unlock(global.io());
    94                 defer if (mutex) |m| m.lockUncancelable(global.io());
    95                 if (mb.queue.push(global.io(), msg, .{ .forever = {} }) == 0) msg.deinit();
    96             },
    97         }
    98     }
    99 
   100     /// Notify that there are new messages. This may be a noop depending
   101     /// on the writer type.
   102     pub fn notify(self: *Mailbox) void {
   103         switch (self.*) {
   104             .spsc => |*v| v.wakeup.notify() catch |err| {
   105                 log.warn("failed to notify writer, data will be dropped err={}", .{err});
   106             },
   107         }
   108     }
   109 };

    17     pub const WriteReq = MessageData(u8, 38);

    94     /// Write where the data pointer is stable.
    95     write_stable: WriteReq.Stable,

    97     /// Write where the data is allocated and must be freed.
    98     write_alloc: WriteReq.Alloc,

n33 src/Surface.zig:895–920 DIU
   895 /// Queue a message for the IO thread, taking ownership of `msg`.
   896 ///
   897 /// We centralize all our logic into this spot so we can intercept
   898 /// messages for example in readonly mode.
   899 fn queueIo(
   900     self: *Surface,
   901     msg: termio.Message,
   902     mutex: termio.Termio.MutexState,
   903 ) void {
   904     // In readonly mode, we don't allow any writes through to the pty.
   905     if (self.readonly) {
   906         switch (msg) {
   907             .write_small,
   908             .write_stable,
   909             .write_alloc,
   910             => {
   911                 msg.deinit();
   912                 return;
   913             },
   914 
   915             else => {},
   916         }
   917     }
   918 
   919     self.io.queueMessage(msg, mutex);
   920 }

n33 src/Surface.zig:2451–2473 DIU
  2451 fn setSelection(self: *Surface, sel_: ?terminal.Selection) !void {
  2452     // Compute the transition before `select` below, which untracks (frees)
  2453     // the previous selection's tracked pins; reading them after would be a
  2454     // use-after-free.
  2455     const prev_ = self.io.terminal.screens.active.selection;
  2456     const changed = changed: {
  2457         const prev = prev_ orelse break :changed sel_ != null;
  2458         const sel = sel_ orelse break :changed true;
  2459         break :changed !sel.eql(prev);
  2460     };
  2461 
  2462     try self.io.terminal.screens.active.select(sel_);
  2463 
  2464     if (changed) {
  2465         _ = self.rt_app.performAction(
  2466             .{ .surface = self },
  2467             .selection_changed,
  2468             {},
  2469         ) catch |err| {
  2470             log.warn("apprt failed selection_changed notification err={}", .{err});
  2471         };
  2472     }
  2473 }

  2498 
  2499         // Both standard and selection clipboards are set.
  2500         .both => try self.copySelectionToClipboards(

  2503             .mixed,
  2504         ),
  2505     }

n33 src/datastruct/message_data.zig:8–36 D
     8 pub fn MessageData(comptime Elem: type, comptime small_size: comptime_int) type {
     9     return union(enum) {
    10         pub const Self = @This();
    11 
    12         pub const Small = struct {
    13             pub const Max = small_size;
    14             pub const Array = [Max]Elem;
    15             pub const Len = std.math.IntFittingRange(0, small_size);
    16             data: Array = undefined,
    17             len: Len = 0,
    18         };
    19 
    20         pub const Alloc = struct {
    21             alloc: Allocator,
    22             data: []Elem,
    23         };
    24 
    25         pub const Stable = []const Elem;
    26 
    27         /// A small write where the data fits into this union size.
    28         small: Small,
    29 
    30         /// A stable pointer so we can just pass the slice directly through.
    31         /// This is useful i.e. for const data.
    32         stable: Stable,
    33 
    34         /// Allocated and must be freed with the provided allocator. This
    35         /// should be rarely used.
    36         alloc: Alloc,

n33 src/terminal/Screen.zig:3063–3097 DI
  3063 pub fn select(self: *Screen, sel_: ?Selection) Allocator.Error!void {
  3064     const sel = sel_ orelse {
  3065         self.clearSelection();
  3066         return;
  3067     };
  3068 
  3069     // If this selection is untracked then we track it.
  3070     const tracked_sel = if (sel.tracked()) sel else try sel.track(self);
  3071     errdefer if (!sel.tracked()) tracked_sel.deinit(self);
  3072 
  3073     // Untrack prior selection pins that aren't also owned by the replacement.
  3074     // A caller may pass our current tracked selection back to us by value, so
  3075     // releasing both old pins unconditionally would leave the replacement
  3076     // pointing at freed pool entries.
  3077     if (self.selection) |old| {
  3078         const new_bounds = tracked_sel.bounds.tracked;
  3079         switch (old.bounds) {
  3080             .untracked => old.deinit(self),
  3081             .tracked => |old_bounds| {
  3082                 if (old_bounds.start != new_bounds.start and
  3083                     old_bounds.start != new_bounds.end)
  3084                 {
  3085                     self.pages.untrackPin(old_bounds.start);
  3086                 }
  3087                 if (old_bounds.end != new_bounds.start and
  3088                     old_bounds.end != new_bounds.end)
  3089                 {
  3090                     self.pages.untrackPin(old_bounds.end);
  3091                 }
  3092             },
  3093         }
  3094     }

n33 src/terminal/Screen.zig:3363–3415 DI
  3363 pub fn selectAll(self: *Screen) ?Selection {
  3364     const whitespace = &[_]u32{ 0, ' ', '\t' };
  3365 
  3366     const start: Pin = start: {
  3367         var it = self.pages.cellIterator(
  3368             .right_down,
  3369             .{ .screen = .{} },
  3370             null,
  3371         );
  3372         while (it.next()) |p| {
  3373             const cell = p.rowAndCell().cell;
  3374             if (!cell.hasText()) continue;
  3375 
  3376             // Non-empty means we found it.
  3377             const this_whitespace = std.mem.indexOfAny(
  3378                 u32,
  3379                 whitespace,
  3380                 &[_]u32{cell.content.codepoint.data},
  3381             ) != null;
  3382             if (this_whitespace) continue;
  3383 
  3384             break :start p;
  3385         }
  3386 
  3387         return null;
  3388     };
  3389 
  3390     const end: Pin = end: {
  3391         var it = self.pages.cellIterator(
  3392             .left_up,
  3393             .{ .screen = .{} },
  3394             null,
  3395         );
  3396         while (it.next()) |p| {
  3397             const cell = p.rowAndCell().cell;
  3398             if (!cell.hasText()) continue;
  3399 
  3400             // Non-empty means we found it.
  3401             const this_whitespace = std.mem.indexOfAny(
  3402                 u32,
  3403                 whitespace,
  3404                 &[_]u32{cell.content.codepoint.data},
  3405             ) != null;
  3406             if (this_whitespace) continue;
  3407 
  3408             break :end p;
  3409         }
  3410 
  3411         return null;
  3412     };
  3413 
  3414     return .init(start, end, false);
  3415 }
